+++
title = "त्रिभुवनः"
+++

## I

[[P072]]

### I

> *Note:* Collection of the Arunaketuka Fire has been discussed in this Prapathaka; propitiation of the Uttara Vedi (Northern altar) being introduced in Anuvaka I. Mantras for peace and propitiation.

Harih Om.

1\. May we hear the auspicious (things) with ears, O gods; may we, the sacrificers, see the auspicious (things) with eyes; with bodies of strong limbs; may we, praising, obtain (the span of) life which is assigned by the gods.

2\. May Indra of great fame grant us welfare; may all-knowing Pusan grant us welfare; may Tarkṣya, the Ariṣṭanemi (one whose felly of wheel is unhurt) grant us welfare; may Bṛhaspati grant us welfare.[^P072_r1_2]

[^P072_r1_2]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Mantras to be uttered while placing the two imaginary watery bricks in the Uttara Vedi.

Om Peace, Peace, Peace.

[[P073]]

3\. I have obtained, I have obtained all the waters from this and this place, from here and there (1); may Agni[^P073_1], Vayu[^P073_2], and Surya[^P073_3] together purify these with prosperity.[^P073_r3_7]

[^P073_1]: Fire-god.

[^P073_2]: Wind-god.

[^P073_3]: Sun-god.

[^P073_r3_7]: *(Mantras 3–7)* Mantras to be uttered while pouring hot water in the Uttara Vedi.

4\. Having winds as horses, rays as protectors, beams as bodies, not hostile, shining goddesses, all-impelling (O you Waters), assent me for progeny.

5\. Of great name and great honour, inspirers of all mighty ones, shining goddesses, rain-impelling (O you Waters), assent me for progeny. (2)

6\. Remove away the consuming and burning fire, the demons, the consuming and burning fever, the exhaustedness caused by fever, the bad smell, and poverty from this place; shining goddesses (O Waters).

7\. Shining ones, god-inspirers (O Waters); raise up the thunderbolt (for our protection); raise us, undefeated ones, up; raise up the all (cattle etc.); raise up the Adityas and the goddess Aditi being their cause.

8\. May the divine Waters and the plants be auspicious and most wealful for us.[^P073_r8]

[^P073_r8]: *(Mantra 8)* Mantra to be uttered by the Adhvaryu after receiving the water steeped in plants.

9\. O Sarasvati, (you are) well merciful;[^P073_4] may your hole not be seen (3).[^P073_r9]

[^P073_4]: Sarasvati: the earth full of stream or watery bricks. Hole not seen on account of the covering of the plants. Cf. T.A. 1.21.3, 1.31.6, 4.42.1.

[^P073_r9]: *(Mantra 9)* Mantra to be uttered by the Adhvaryu while placing the aforesaid water on the earth.

[[P074]]

### II

> *Note:* In this Anuvaka there are the mantras for placing bricks on the altar.

1\. Memory (smrti), perception (pratyaksa), traditional instruction (aitihya), inference (anumana) (these are) fourfold. By all these, indeed, the orb of the sun is determined.[^P075_r1_8]

[^P075_r1_8]: *(Mantras 1–8)* Mantras for placing bricks on the altar.

2\. The sun receives the ray (spread) over all the world. By the distinction of the ripening of that (ray) the distinction of time is remembered.

3\. Like any river, (that) flows from an undecaying source, (is the time or year) (1); as the (other little) rivers meet that river (and) that becoming great, does not vanish—

4\. Similarly variously rising, little and great (divisions of) time are dependant upon year (samvatsara); all of them approach into the year.

5\. That (year) filled with all those (divisions of time), being wide does not vanish. (One) should know (everything dependant) upon the year. In definition of time that (year), indeed, is to be understood (2).

6\. The year, mounted on by the little and great (divisions of) time, is observed by the perception; but the Stronger One (Adhisattva, Eternal Time) is not observed (so).

[[P075]]

7\. Having a cloth-like skin, moist with perspiration, tawny[^P075_2] Varuna (covering one)—such is the form of the eye. Where (i.e., in the sight of the human eye) this (world) is observed there are lead a thousand (parts of the time).

[^P075_2]: Varuna from *vṛ* (to cover). The idea is that the eye is covered by its material form and is unable to see the Eternal Time.

8\. One, verily, is the head (i.e., the year) and there are many (two) mouths (i.e., uttarayana[^P075_4] and dakṣiṇāyana[^P075_5]). This all is the definition of the seasons (3). In both[^P075_6] there are the beings of seven organs of senses. Only the talk is increased (by these imaginary divisions of time).

[^P075_4]: Progress of the sun to the north.

[^P075_5]: Progress of the sun to the south. In Uttarayana and Daksinayana.

[^P075_6]: According to commentators these organs of senses are the seven holes of the head, i.e., eyes, nose etc.

9\. In the right and the left sides of the year there are white and black ones (day and night). As to that this is (said):—[^P075_r9_11]

[^P075_r9_11]: *(Mantras 9–11)* Rg and Brahmana mantras in support of the above.

10\. One is your white and one is your sacrificeable (time); of different forms are the day and night; you are like an impeller; possessor of food, you protect all the measurings (of time); O Pusan, may your gift be auspicious here.

11\. Here (in the reality) neither there is a being (bhuvana), nor Pusan, nor cattle, nor the sun (Aditya); Samvatsara (Eternal Year), indeed, resides; but by the perception one should know the dearest (i.e., the time that comes in daily use). This, indeed, is the dearest form of the year which is the great thing of this (world) to be produced. "Do this auspicious duty"—this practicable duty should (a teacher) give to him (to the pupil) (4).

[[P076]]

### III

> *Note:* In this Anuvaka also the Mantras for placing bricks on the altar are given.

1\. Of the co-born (rays of the sun) they call the seventh single-born; the six assistant (rays are) Rsis, born of divine ones; their desired (places) have been arranged in the places; they, various in their form, shine for the guiding Lord.[^P076_r1_11]

[^P076_r1_11]: *(Mantras 1–11)* Mantras to be uttered while placing the bricks before the altar.

2\. Which friend, unprovoked, O men, said to (his) friend: "He leaving (us) escapes from us."

3\. He who abandoned his friend, who knows friendship, has not a part even in speech; whatever he hears, hears in vain (1); he does not know the path of the good deed.

4\. A season impelled by (another) season, running hastily makes sounds. And there are sixty groups of thirty-fold ones (days and nights consisting of muhurtas or moments) (in a season); and these sixty-fold ones (days and nights) are white and black (i.e., the light half of a month and the dark half of a month).

[[P077]]

5\. With red-clothed Vasus, the spring (Vasanta) season, clever in water (i.e., having clean water) has been remembered the first envoy of the year, the impeller.

6\. Take (and feed) these (fellows who have come after the thread-ceremony or the Upanayana) (2); and protect these (who have come from abroad)—(they say these words) to others (i.e., the persons of their family). These speeches are used where this (spring-season) is perceived.

7\. This, indeed, should be understood as a standard in the change of time. We shall speak the difference of the seasons; perceive that.

8\. That white-clothed group of Rudras turns round with the summer (grisma) season, burning all the earth (3) with an uncomparable light.

9\. Perceive all kinds of clothes of Adityas, (who) give the result of the actions of the (whole) year (accompanied) with the rainy season (varsa).

10\. (Though) sorrowless (itself, it) seems like sorrowful-eyed (on account of the eye-disease of the beings); by that (disease of eye) this (rainy season also) seems yellow. (This season) is seen not suffering from coldness (though there is plenty of water); (and it also is seen as one full of antelope on account of grass etc.)

[[P078]]

11\. (It) causes delight; (its) eyes cause brightness and peace (on account of making creatures powerful by various food grown in this season). Whatever beings fell (in this season on account of not growing food) they fell for the (whole) year.

12\. They who stand firm (in this season), stand firm in the (whole) year (4).[^P078_12]

[^P078_12]: A Brahmana-passage showing the advantage of the above.

### IV

> *Note:* In this Anuvaka also the Mantras are for placing the bricks on the altar.

1\. Like risen from the disease of the eyes, the pupils of the eyes (are) very bright. (People) decorate (their eyes); there is no mist. Perceive that season of the Rbhus.[^P078_r1_6]

[^P078_r1_6]: *(Mantras 1–6)* Mantras to be uttered while placing the bricks on the altar.

2\. Perceive the clothes (of the Rbhus): "Eat food and adorn; like gold, (which are) untorn. I am your life-giver."

3\. These speeches are used where autumn (sarad) season is seen (1). Like shaking and striking, full of winds (are) the groups of Maruts.

[[P079]]

4\. From that side (of heaven) in order to win, like the mouth of the arrow (the groups of Maruts) equipped together are, verily, seen. (The people also wear clothes which are) like dirty-coloured armours; (they are) of various crests and bound (combed) hair.

5\. (One) should know the eyes of the winter (hemanta) season (which itself is) not angry, red like the moving eyes of a fighting angry person (2).

6\. Feminine (of water) in divine worlds (i.e., in the clouds) and water (only) in the house of the men—uttering these speeches the lightnings of the cool (sisira) season go (away).

7\. Agni, not seeing the means of living here, thought about these flowing clearly. As to that this is (said):—[^P079_7]

[^P079_7]: A Brahmana passage in regard to above.

8\. O Maruts of self-penance, of sun-brilliance, we pray here, here your comfort (of water) with extension (3).[^P079_8]

[^P079_8]: A Rc has been quoted.

[[P080]]

### V

> *Note:* Propitiatory mantras have been given in this Anuvaka.

1\. Very much coppery red (are) the clothes; Asti (stone), vajri (thunderbolt), Sataghni (killer of a hundred)—(these) Visvedevas (all-gods) throw; they, the fire-tongued ones, made the utterance:—[^P080_r1_4]

[^P080_r1_4]: *(Mantras 1–4)* Mantras to be uttered before and after placing the bricks.

2\. "Neither gods, nor men, nor the king Varuna, the all-pervading, nor Agni (Fire), nor Indra, nor Pavamana (wind), nor anyone is like me."

3\. One end of the bow of this divine (Fire, which is the tongue of the Visvedevas) is fixed in the heaven and the other is fixed on earth (1). Indra himself in the form of ant (vamri) cut the string of that bow.

4\. That (is) the bow of Indra (Indra-dhanus) without string in the colours of the clouds,"—(thus the people) say. This (bow) is also of Samiyu, the son of Brhaspati; this is the bow of Rudra.

5\. The end of the bow destroyed the head of Rudra, indeed. That became Pravargya-sacrifice. Hence he, who sacrifices along with the Pravargya, puts again the head of Rudra. Rudra does not become violent to him who knows thus (2).[^P080_5]

[^P080_5]: A Brahmana passage in support of the above.

[[P081]]

### VI

> *Note:* In this Anuvaka also Mantras are for placing the bricks on the altar.

1\. The cool season (sisira) is observed as having eyes fixed above, and not looking in the sides. Neither, verily, the form (of the body), nor the clothes, nor the eyes are seen.

2\. (Enemies) do not injure each other; that is the characteristic of the present (season). At the rising of the sun red (colour is) in the ayatyek eye, green (colour like that of the Sarika bird), in the head.[^P081_2]

[^P081_2]: No care is taken of these in the cool season.

3\. (O sisira,) you make the palms of the hands (of the people) downward; you make the knees downward (1). "Downward my knee, downward my palms of the hand (may become)"—with this speech may these (boys) worship.[^P081_3]

[^P081_3]: On account of the great coldness people do so, which is transferred to the season itself.

4\. To him all the seasons salute; that Brahmana obtains the great priestness being the performer of the bounds of propriety—who knows thus.[^P081_4] That year (samvatsara) accompanied with these commanders (spring etc.) carries near the sacrificer (Indra) all the desires. That (year) is the drop (of water, 'drapsa'). As to that this is (said) (2):—

[^P081_4]: According to Sayana the knower and according to Bhattabhaskara the Arunaketuka Fire.

[[P082]]

5\. That drop (drapsa), the cause of ploughing (krena) coming with ten thousand (drops) stood firm on the earth (Amsumati). You, as Indra causing that return again and again, roaring with power, thought by men, flowing downward make (that) sprinkle on the earth.[^P082_5]

[^P082_5]: A Rc. has been quoted.

6\. With this earth, covered by water, Indra pierces (the clouds), full of life (water). The earth is Amsumati. The year (samvatsara) stands firm following that (earth) and heaven. Acarya and Antevasin should not become hostile (as) each other to the knower of this.[^P082_12] He who becomes hostile, declines from the heavenly world. Thus the orbs of the seasons, the orbs of the sun and the quotations (have been discussed). After this (the quotations are) with Brahmanas (3).[^P082_6]

[^P082_12]: Adhvaryu and Yajamana, according to the commentators.

[^P082_6]: A Brahmana-passage.

[[P083]]

### VII

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks.

1\. Aroga, Bhraja, Patara, Patanga, Svarnara, Jyotimat, Vibhasa—these (seven suns) shine all round the heaven for the sake of this (world), milking vigour (and) not lashing out.[^P083_r1_2]

[^P083_r1_2]: Mantras for placing bricks.

2\. Kasyapa is the eighth; he does not leave the Great Meru (-mountain). As to that this is (said):—

3\. O Kasyapa, which is your wonderful work, shining, vigorous, all and of various lights; in which the seven suns have been put together (1); put above in that this (sacrificing) king.[^P083_3]

[^P083_3]: A Rc. has been quoted.

4\. They all find light from Kasyapa for the sake of this (world); Soma purifies them well like the goldsmith (who purifies gold by filling air to burn fire with the help of a skin).[^P083_r4_8]

[^P083_r4_8]: 4–8. Description of the suns.

5\. Breath (Prana of seven kinds), lives (of seven kinds), (seven sense-lives[^P083_s2]), seven breaths of head—thus the teachers (explain the seven) suns.[^P083_5]

[^P083_s2]: Mahat (intellect), Ahankara (individuality), and the essence of the five, i.e., earth, water, air, fire, ether.

[^P083_5]: Five senses of knowledge and mind (manas) and intellect (buddhi). (Wait, let's check seven sense-lives vs seven breaths of head. Two eyes, two ears, two holes of nose and the mouth).

6\. "I saw these seven suns"—thus says Pancakarna, the son of Vatsa and Saptakarna, the son of Plaksa (2); "Kasyapa is for us heard by the tradition. Both[^P083_6] are the informers; we could not, verily, go to the great Meru."

[^P083_6]: We: you and I.

[[P084]]

7\. "I saw this moving orb of the sun"—(says) Pranatrata, the son of Garga. "Go to the great Meru (and see there) that one (sun, viz. Kasyapa) not leaving (that mountain)."

8\. Bhraja, Patara (and) Patanga shine in the low portion (of the Meru); therefore, these are of the hot rays (3); the others (shine) at another place (above): therefore, they are not of the hot rays.

9\. As to that this is (said):—Seven suns have entered the heaven; the giver of the sacrificial fee (Daksina) follows them by the (good) paths; they shine for the sake of him (making him possessor of) the clarified butter; milking vigour (and) not lashing out.[^P084_9]

[^P084_9]: A Rc has been quoted.

10\. Seven priests (are) the (seven) suns"—thus (say) the teachers.[^P084_10]

[^P084_10]: Seven suns as the seven priests.

11\. As to that this is (said):—

12\. Seven (are) the quarters[^P084_12_q] (and) many (seven) the suns (4), who are the seven priests[^P084_12_p] Hotr (ata), and who (are) the seven divine Adityas; with them O Soma, protect us all round.[^P084_11_12]

[^P084_12_q]: East, being known as the place of the sun, has not been mentioned here.

[^P084_12_p]: The seven priests are: Hotr, Prasastr, Brahmana, Potr, Nestr, Acchavaka, Agnidhra.

[^P084_11_12]: The Rcs have been quoted.

13\. That (is) also a sacred text: shining in the quarters the suns make seasons.[^P084_13]

[^P084_13]: Seven suns of the seven quarters.

[[P085]]

14\. "By this process thousand-sunness (should be imagined)"—thus says Vaisampayana.[^P085_14]

[^P085_14]: Mention of many suns.

15\. As to that this is (said):—"If there were hundred heavens and hundred earths, O thunderbolt-holder (Indra), not the thousand suns (5) can eat you (O Indra,) born of the Rodasi (i.e., between heaven and earth)." Thus.[^P085_15]

[^P085_15]: A Rc has been quoted.

16\. On account of being the many characteristics of the seasons the many-sunness (is proved).[^P085_16]

[^P085_16]: Many suns' theory is supported by an argument.

17\. (But) the eight suns are certain.

18\. Hence eight are the orbs of the suns above.[^P085_17_18]

[^P085_17_18]: Eight suns and their orbs.

As to that this is (said):—

19\. Wonderful army of the divine ones, the eye of Mitra, Varuna and Agni has risen up. He fulfills the heaven, the earth and the mid-region; Surya (Sun) is the soul of all the moving and the fixed ones (6).[^P085_19]

[^P085_19]: A Rc has been quoted.

[[P086]]

### VIII

> *Note:* Mantras for placed bricks on the altar.

[[P087]]

1\. Where resides this cloud? Where this year of many parts? Where, O god, the day (and) the night (reside)? Where are dependent the months (and) the seasons?[^P087_r1]

[^P087_r1]: *(Kaṇḍikā 1)* Mantra to be uttered in front of the altar. Nimesas (twinklings of the eye) [^P087_1].

[^P087_1]: The various parts of the time.

2\. if they go (Where reside) the half-months, Muhurtas with the Trutis? Where reside these waters, from here now?[^P087_r2_3]

3\. The times reside in the waters; the waters are put in the sun (1)[^P087_2]; the clouds find the waters; the lightning is put in the sun.

[^P087_2]: The drying of the water is meant.

[^P087_r2_3]: *(Kaṇḍikās 2–3)* Questions and answers about the time etc.

4\. These (are) the beautiful earths—(earth) and that (heaven), the both Rodasi (heaven and earth).

5\. What is here between them by which they have been held?

"By Visnu the earths have been held"—

This is the knowledge of Vatsa.

6\. (These earths) have become for men full of food, full of cows, full of good eatable things and full of gifts (2). O Visnu, you hold the earth all-round by the rays.

[[P088]]

7\. What has been called that power of Visnu? What (is his) brilliance? What (is his) helping object held alone the shining heaven and earth?[^P088_r7_13]

[^P088_r7_13]: *(Kaṇḍikās 4–9)* Questions and answers about Visnu and his power. *(Kaṇḍikās 10–17)* Questions and answers about the death.

8\. From Wind, (they) say, the power of Visnu (has come); from the immortality, the brilliance; he held (these) by (his) three steps which is one (and) the best of Visnu (3).

9\. The Fires and the Winds are the helping object of him.

10\. I ask you about the extreme death, the lowest, the middle, and the fourth (kind of the death), and (about) the world of the propitious and the sinful men. This I ask now.

11\. To that (sun the people) say the extreme death; to wind, the middle; the Fire, indeed, is the lowest death; the moon is called the fourth (death) (4).

12\. The enjoymentless sinners go always to the extreme death; the enjoying (good ones) go, verily, (there) where the propitious people (live).

13\. Now, the others (not great sinners) come to the middle (death), to the fourth (death) and to the Fire (the lowest death), in the shape of the worms etc. they die with the touch of the rays of the sun.[^P088_1]

[^P088_1]: In the shape of the worms etc. they die with the touch of the rays of the sun.

14\. I ask you about the sinners (and the place) where Yama (the deity of the Death) punishes them (by the pains of the hell). O Brahman, tell us that, if you know the homes of the bad beings (5).

15\. The suns, risen up from Kasyapa, kill the sinners always in the intermediate regions of the earth and the heaven; they are put there by the life-giving (rays).

16\. They become bodiless as the (result) of the sin (is); they (become) there handless, footless, hairless, (and they also become) the beings not born of the womb.

17\. After death they get death again being eaten by their acts (6) like the Asatika worms; then they are purified by the life-giving (rays).

18\. He who knows thus conquers this bad death. That Brahmana, knowing thus, indeed, becomes (the possessor) of the greatest fame, the quest of Kasyapa; of successful going and of successful coming.[^P088_2]

[^P088_2]: Asstika ('killed just after coming').

19\. As to that this is (said):—

20\. In whom the seven life-giving (rays) ascend the ancient ascension (7), of (that) Indra (Kasyapa) the shining seer Dirghasruttama (was) the quest.[^P088_r18_20]

[^P088_r18_20]: *(Kaṇḍikā 18)* A Brahmana—(explanatory or praising)—passage. *(Kaṇḍikās 19–20)* A Ṛc has been quoted.

[[P089]]

21\. Kasyapa is the 'Payaka' (observer) who observes everything all round minutely.[^P088_r21]

[^P088_r21]: *(Kaṇḍikā 21)* A Brāhmaṇa-(explanatory)-passage.

22\. Now, regarding Fire consisting of eight personages. As to that this is (said):—[^P089_r22]

[^P089_r22]: *(Kaṇḍikā 22)* In praise of the Fire.

23\. "O Fire take us from the good path for wealth; O God, (you are) knower of all the paths; take away the curved sin from us; salutation-sayings to you (8).[^P089_r23]

[^P089_r23]: *(Kaṇḍikā 23)* A Ṛc has been quoted.

[[P090]]

### IX

> *Note:* Mantras for placing the bricks on the altar.

1\. Agni, Jatavedas, Sahojas, Ajiraprabhu, Vaihesnare, Naryapa, Panktiradhes the seventh and Visarpi, verily, the eighth—these eight are the life-giving ones on the earth.[^P090_1]

[^P090_1]: of the Fires.

2\. According to the seasons there are the differences of the colours in the flames of the fire, as the blue flame and the yellow flame.

3\. Now regarding the wind consisting of eleven men and eleven women:

4\. Prabhrajamanas, Vyavadatas, (they) which are Vasuki-Vaidyatas, Rejetes, Parusas, Syamas, Kapilas, Atilohitas, Urdhvas, Avapatantas and Vaidyuto (1)—these eleven.

5\. The lightning does not kill him, who knows thus.

6\. That (renowned), Vyasa, the son of Parasara said, "I wish (to say) that killing by the lightning is only the death."

7\. (The lightning) does not kill the desireless man (2) who knows thus.

8\. Now, the groups of the Gandharvas:—

9\. Svane, Bhrat, Angheri, Bambhari, Haste, Suhaste, Krasnu, Visvavasu, Murdhanvan, Suryavarcas, Krti—these (are) eleven groups of the Gandharvas.[^P090_r1_9]

[^P090_r1_9]: *(Kaṇḍikā 1)* Mantra for placing the brick uttering the eight names of the fire. *(Kaṇḍikā 2)* A Brahmana-passage. *(Kaṇḍikās 3–4)* Mantra for placing the bricks uttering the names of the wind. *(Kaṇḍikās 5–7)* Brahmana-passages. *(Kaṇḍikās 8–11)* For the placing of the bricks in the name of the Gandharvas, with the Brahmana-passage.

[[P091]]

10\. (These are) the gods, the great gods, rays and the gods, (who are) the swallowers of poison.

11\. The poison does not kill him who knows thus.

12\. Gauri measured (the world) creating the waters[^P091_3]; she desiring to become one-footed, two-footed, four-footed, eight-footed, nine-footed (and) thousand-syllabled (resides) in the highest heaven.[^P091_2]

13\. (This is) the quality of the speech (Vac).

14\. Now (we give the Mantras), explained by the statement (itself); we shall give them respectively:—

15\. The Varshus (well-invokers), the Svatapases (of self-penance) [^P091_4], the Vidyunmahesas (bright as lightning), the Dhupis (perfuming ones), the Svapis (soonly spreading) and the Grhamedhes (worth worshipping at homes)—these and these which are not hostile to the labours of the men—

16\. (These) seven Parjanyas (clouds) rain on the earth all round with the rains (waters). As to that this is said:—

17\. This similar water (5) goes above and below in some 4 days. The Parjanyas favour the earth; (and) the Agnis (fires)[^P091_5] favour the heavens.

18\. Which is undestroyable (Brahman), (which) made the matter, all the gods worship that; they appointed Jamadagni, the great seer as the preserver of that.[^P091_r12_18]

[^P091_2]: *(Footnote *2)* Waters = beings (that live by the use of the waters).

[^P091_3]: *(Footnote *1)* Gauri = white; here this word means Sarasvati, the goddess of the speech; by the speech the world is known; hence it has been said that she measures the world.

[^P091_4]: Foot = the foot of a verse.

[^P091_5]: Fires = the waters that go above are called here fires, for they, like the flames of the fire, go above (being dried by the sun).

[^P091_r12_18]: *(Kaṇḍikās 12–13)* Mantra, with Brahmana, for placing the bricks. *(Kaṇḍikās 14–17)* Mantra, with Brahmana and quotation of Ṛc. for placing bricks. *(Kaṇḍikās 18–20)* According to Baudhayana for the same subject (placing bricks).

[[P092]]

19\. Jamadagni becomes satisfied by the verses of more than four (syllables); making the king soma satisfied by the strong prayer; may the main Quarters and the (Sub-) Quarters become propitious for us.[^P092_2]

[^P092_2]: The people living in the quarters and the sub-quarters are meant. Medicine means the remover of all the sufferings.

20\. I pray for that happiness and welfare for the progress of the sacrifice (and) for the progress of the sacrificer. May divine welfare be for us. Further may the medicine obtain (place). May propitiation be for our two-footed (men); may propitiation be for the four-footed (cattle).

21\. The Soma-drinking and the non-Soma-drinking (gods have been mentioned); this much (as to the mantras), explained by the statement (itself).[^P092_r21]

[^P092_r21]: *(Kaṇḍikā 21)* A Brahmana-passage.

### X

> *Note:* Mantras for placing the bricks on the altar.

1\. This earth includes thousand beings; the highest heaven includes thousand being; (these are) Asvinas, the wealthy and the true, the lords of all the world.

2\. The earth is the wife; the heaven, the husband; they formed a pair; the sons are Brhaspati and Rudra and (the daughter) Sarama; these the male and the female (are born of them).

3\. One is your white form (i.e., the day) and one is your sacrificeable form (i.e., the night); of different forms are the day and the night; you are like an impeller (1), O possessors of the food you protect all the measurings; O thrivers (Pusans) may your gift be auspicious here.[^P092_r1_3]

[^P092_r1_3]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–3)* Asvins.

[[P093]]

4\. O Heaven and Earth, you are dusky, various, the abodes of the worlds, you move together as the friends; come to our sacrifice with Surya (as) Asvins, the Resabhasves suspices.[^P093_1]

[^P093_1]: Surya = Uses or the dawn. Having esses as horses.

5\. As a foolish dying person carries the wealth, so you carried that protecting water in the watery clouds by the boats, which were automatic, floating over the atmosphere, and water-tight (2).

6\. O true ones, lasting for three nights and three days you carried the protecting waters by the rays bringing across the wet ocean by three chariots of hundred wheels and six horses.

7\. The cloud depends upon the sun, which spreads the rays. And the cloud, full of water, becomes also the impeller (of the water) and sinless.

8\. Knowing that (cloud) well-satisfied, full of various gentle sounds the powerful sun follows that crooked cloud impelling that to rain on the sacrificers, who satisfy with soma.[^P093_3]

[^P093_3]: It is said that the sun goes on the chariot of seven horses.

9\. That group of the rays, the destroyer of the darkness, protected by the horses makes the noising cows drink that water. Then praised by those cows and by those who are not related to the demons, that (group) goes above.[^P093_r1_10]

[^P093_r1_10]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–10)* Asvins.

10\. That cloud returns again to rain (being) impelled by the sun. O Asvins, be thus. These Asvins cause the pregnance of the heaven and the earth day by day (4).

11\. Of them these are the sons: the day and the night; of the earth, the day; of the heaven, the night. They are, verily, the contiguous husband and wife.

[[P094]]

12\. Of them these are the sons: The Fire and the sun; of the night, the white Sun; of the day, the coppery red Fire. They are, verily, the contiguous husband and wife.

13\. Of them these are the sons: Vrtra (covering smoke) and the lightning; of the Fire, Vrtra; of the Sun, the lightning. They conclude them, verily, in themselves.

14\. Of them these are the sons: the heat and the dew; of Vrtra, the heat; of the lightning, the dew. They conclude them, verily, in themselves.

15\. That, verily, this pregnant night lives with the son. Of her, verily, of this the rays, that are in the night, are the membrane (enveloping the embryo). As there is a membrane of a pregnant cow, so there is the membrane of this night.

16\. He who knows thus, becomes the producer of the progeny and the cattle; he obtains this rising and setting sun. The Sun is the son of the good deed.

17\. Now (we shall deal with) the pure Angiras (-Mantras).

### XI

> *Note:* \` Mantras for placing the bricks on the alter. Pavitra = fringed filtering cloth.

1\. Pavitra fringed filtering cloth.

2\. The holders of the Pavitras sit around the sacrificial food; the ancient Father of them protects fully the function; Varuna covered the great ocean (i.e., here the mid-region by the clouds); the wise men only can begin their work in the firm grounds (of the fire).[^P094_r11]

[^P094_r11]: *(Kaṇḍikās 11–14)* Dyava-Prthivi. *(Kaṇḍikās 15–17)* Brahmana-passages.

[[P095]]

3\. O Brahmanaspati (lord of the Brahman or Soma), the Pavitra has been spread for you; as prominent one you pervade all the limbs (of the sacrifice) from all sides. The non-heated, uncooked, raw (material) does not obtain that (sacrifice), only cooked (material) fulfilling that (sacrifice) obtains that.

4\. "Brahman among the gods (is this Soma)"

5\. The seven seers and Atri, who created the being from the non-being (1), they all Atris and Agastya, the welfare-doers resided with the stars.

6\. Now, (the Mantras) of Syavasva, the inspirer and desirous of non-earning (asceticism):—

7\. These stars, which are fixed above, are seen in the night; (and they) go wherever in the day; the laws of Varuna are unbroken; the moon goes to the stars making them shine.

8\. We hold that excellent ray of the inspirer Savitr (sun)[^P095_2], which may impell our wisdoms.

[^P095_2]: This is the symbol of the Mantra, T.S. 3.4.11.

9\. We like that best, mostly all-containing food (possession) of the inspirer god Savitr (sun); we hold that excellent (possession) of that patron.

10\. The sun (Savitr) hides all the rays (favourable for) food from the heaven; they become worth seeing at the night; (it is as) we hide the bone by the blood.

11\. My name is only name, of course; I am neuter, male and female; I sacrifice; I sacrificed and I shall sacrifice; I am immovable (and) also movable.[^P095_r10]

[^P095_r10]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–4)* Mantras for placing the bricks in the north. *(Kaṇḍikā 5)* A Brahmana-passage. *(Kaṇḍikās 6–12)* Mantras for placing the bricks in the middle.

[[P096]]

12\. The beings sacrificed with me; the elements are my animals (pasavah); I, the pervading one, am related (to all these).

13\. Those women also, (who have obtained the knowledge of Brahman), in my opinion, are called men. (If) a possessor of the eyes does not know the truth, he is blind. Who is the wise son, he knows these (elements); who knows these, he becomes the father of his producer (father).

14\. The blind (i.e., the eyeless Atman) knows the gem (mani); the fingerless (he) takes that; he, the neckless wears (that); and the tongueless (he) proclaims that.

15\. Now, who knows the tree, above-rooted and of the low branches, he can never believe "Death can kill me".

16\. Perceive all laughter, weeping, singing, lute (vina), drum (panava), dancing (lalita), death, life whatsoever like the limbs (that are) sinew (only connected with the body and not related to the Atman).

17\. Though (the Atman is) not thirsty, (but) thinks of his thirst caused by this (laughter etc. the worldly elements) forming the pair (of the consciousness and the unconsciousness). The son of illusion (is) bodiless, which is unconscious and also conscious.

18\. That (Atman) knows that gem (mani), he, the fingerless takes (that); he, the neckless wears that; he, the tongueless proclaims that.

[[P097]]

19\. Knowing this fact one should not enter the town; if (he) enters, he should enter knowing (that) unreal. That is the rule of Sambhava.[^P097_r18]

[^P097_r18]: *(Kaṇḍikā 18)* A Brahmana-passage. *(Kaṇḍikās 19–21)* Mantras for placing the bricks.

20\. O Fire (Aruṇaketukāgni), O Pervading One, sit on that chariot, which is of one horse, of one yoking, of one wheel, of one axle and of the airy speed.

21\. This chariot does not perish, does not tremble (7); its axle never touches (any checking object); for (this Fire) sits on (the chariot) yoking the white and red horses of the fire in (his) chariot.

22\. By which team of the female horses (containing) one and ten; two and twenty; three and thirty, you carry (the fire) for the sacrifice, O wind, now unyoke them here.

[[P098]]

### XII

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks.

1\. Spread (yourself) all round, spread (yourself) fully; highly sound (that you have come), sound all round, sound well; put the womb of golden (waters) in the sun (Aditya), (O Indra, you) as a vigorous man.

2\. Make that (as) the juice for Candramas (moon) (which is) sprinkled from here and reached to the sun; produce that gift-giving (Fire) before (us), who is called Rudra, One.

3\. Innumerable, thousands (forms of Rudra) are remembered and not seen; thus perceive this.

4\. O Indra, come by the pleasant, peacock-haired horses; may not, verily, any trappers stop you; like the donator come to (our) those (sacrificing places).

5\. O Indra, (may) I go by the pleasant peacock-haired horses (with you); may not, verily, any trappers stop me; like the possessor of receptacle (may) I go to those (high worlds).

6\. O Indra, of thousand eyes, come by the little and concurrent times, (which) have obtained great (2), shining well, not the horseness.

7\. The fire having well-shining clothes; the wind, the injurer of the white sands; the year with various colours (of seasons)—these are your perpetual followers.[^P098_r1_7]

[^P098_r1_7]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–4)* Mantras addressed to Indra. *(Kaṇḍikā 5)* Mantra for Yajamana while placing a brick. *(Kaṇḍikās 6–8)* Mantras addressed to Indra.

[[P099]]

8\. O Subrahmanya (devoted to sacred knowledge), Om; O Subrahmanya, Om; O Subrahmanya, Om; O Indra, come; O Hariva (possessor of horses named Hari), come; (O Indra, related) to Medhatithi, O Mesa (sprinkler) of Mena, (the daughter) of Vranasva, O Gaura (white) Aveskandin (destroying antelope), O paramour for Ahalya, O Brahmana (at the place of) Kausika, O Gautama-praising (Indra, come) [^P099_1].

[^P099_1]: There are mythologies around this passage.

9\. The possessors of red horses have come here; Vasus living on the earth are the eight fires having the clothes of the quarters.

10\. Rudras having coppery horses, coppery chariots, coppery colours and not white, staff-handed, having biting teeth have gone above (in the heaven) from here (4); the place and the quantity (of them) have been mentioned before this.

11\. May Brhaspati and Savitr, the impellers of the waters, come here by various coloured (horses), by the chariot of watery path. Thus (the forms etc.) of them both (have been mentioned).

12\. The forms and clothes of the limbs of the Time (seasons) have been mentioned before here. "The abodes" in this (mantra) the (forms etc.) of Asvins have been mentioned. "who makes sound in the mid-region" considered Vasistha, the son of Rohine. As to that this is (said):—

13\. "Like a lowing (cow) the lightning" etc.[^P099_r9_13]

[^P099_r9_13]: *(Kaṇḍikās 9–12)* Mantras addressed to Agni. T.S. 3.1.11. *(Kaṇḍikā 13)* Initial words of a Ṛc quoted.

[[P100]]

14\. (O brick!) you are the up-raiser of Brahman, you are the increaser of Brahman, you are the seat of Brahman, you are the cover (of the seat) of Brahman.[^P100_r14]

[^P100_r14]: *(Kaṇḍikā 14)* Four Mantras addressed to bricks while placing them on the altar.

### XIII

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks etc.

1\. I know this earth of eight sources (Mula Prakrti, Mahad, Ahamkara and Panca-tanmatras), of eight sons (Mitra etc. to be mentioned further), and the wife of eight (Indra etc. residing in the quarters); there is not my death and the accidental death shall not bring the sins (to me).

2\. I know this mid-region of eight sources, of eight sons, the eight protectors; there is not my death and the accidental death shall not bring the sins (to me). I know the heaven of eight sources, of eight sons and the wife of eight; there is not my death and the accidental death shall not bring sins (to me).[^P100_2]

[^P100_2]: T.S. 1.5.11.

3\. "Well-protecting" and "great mother" these (mantras).

4\. Aditi is the heaven, Aditi is the mid-region, Aditi is the mother, she is the father, she is the son; Aditi is Visvedevas (all gods), Aditi is the five Janas (castes); Aditi is the born (world); Aditi is the generating essence.[^P100_r1_4]

[^P100_r1_4]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–6)* Eight Mantras for placing bricks in the quarters.

[[P101]]

5\. Eight are the sons of Aditi, who were born of her body; she went to the gods with the seven (sons) (2) and left away (the eighth) Martanda.

6\. With the seven sons Aditi went in the ancient time for progeny and left Martanda, then, to die.

7\. We shall (describe) them (the eight sons of the earth etc.) respectively:—

8\. Mitra and Varuna, Dhatr and Aryaman, Amsa and Bhaga, Indra and Vivasvan.[^P101_r7_8]

[^P101_r7_8]: *(Kaṇḍikās 7–8)* Mantras for placing bricks in the names of the eight sons mentioned before.

9\. "Hiranyagarbha", "The Swan (Atman) sitting in pure (places)", "Brahman being known", "That very place" (mantras).[^P101_r9]

[^P101_r9]: T.S. 4.1.8. T.S. 1.8.15. T.S. 4.2.8. T.Br. 3.7.10. *(Kaṇḍikā 9)* Initial words of four Mantras for placing a lotus-petal, a piece of gold, a golden figure of man and a tortoise. A Brahmana-passage.

10\. The Purusa (Person: Aditya) in Garbha (womb) of Prajapati: He is the possessor of the seven persons (servants i.e. the lunar mansion, the half of a month, the month, the season, the day, and the night).[^P101_r10]

[^P101_r10]: *(Kaṇḍikā 10)* A Brahmana-passage.

[[P102]]

### XIV

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks etc.

1\. That (sun) which rises shining, rises taking the breaths of the beings; do not rise taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

2\. That (sun), which sets, sets taking the breaths of all the beings; do not set taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

3\. That (sun), which becomes full, becomes full taking the breaths of all the beings; do not become full taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

4\. That (sun), which diminishes, diminishes taking the breaths of all the beings; do not diminish taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

5\. Those stars go away and come taking the breaths of all the beings; do not go away and come taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

6\. These months and half-months go and come taking the breaths of all the beings; do not go and come taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine. These seasons go and come taking the breaths of all the beings; do not go and come taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

7\. This year goes and comes taking the breaths of all the beings; do not go and come taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

8\. This day goes and comes taking the breaths of all the beings; do not go and come taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine. This night goes and comes taking the breaths of all the beings; do not go and come taking the breaths of my progeny, nor of my cattle, nor mine.

9\. Om, Bhuh, Bhuvah, Svah.

10\. (O stars etc.) this is your pair; do not destroy our pair.[^P102_r1_10]

[^P102_r1_10]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–10)* Addresses to Aditya.

[[P103]]

### XV

> *Note:* Mantras for placing the bricks on the altar.

1\. Now, of the sun (Aditya), consisting of eight persons:—

2\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Adityas (named) Vasus.

3\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Adityas (named) Rudras.

4\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Adityas (named) Adityas.

5\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Adityas (known as) true ones among the honest ones.

6\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Adityas (known as) Maruts, shaking round and killing round, full of winds.

7\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Adityas (known as) Ṛbhus.

8\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Adityas (known as) Visve-devas.

9\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Aditya (known as) the impeller of the year.

10\. Om, Bhuh, Bhuvah, Svah. The rays are your pair; do not destroy our pair.[^P103_r1_10]

[^P103_r1_10]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–10)* Addresses to eight personifications of Aditya.

[[P104]]

### XVI

1\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Aroga. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Patars. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Bhraja. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Patanga. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Svarnara. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Jyotismat. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vibhasa. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Kasyapa. Om, Bhuh, Bhuvah, Svah. The waters are your pair; do not destroy our pair.

### XVII

1\. Now of the wind (Vayu) consisting of eleven male and eleven female ones—

2\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Prabhrajamana Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vyavadata Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vasuki-Vaidyuta Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Rajata Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Paruṣa Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Shyama Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Kapila Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Atilohita Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Urdhva Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Avapatanta Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vaidyuta Rudras. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Prabhrajamani Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vyavadati Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vasuki-Vaidyuti Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Rajata Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Paruṣa Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Shyama Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Kapila Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Atilohiti Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Urdhva Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Avapatanti Rudranis. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vaidyuti Rudranis. Om, Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Svaraḥ. The forms (of male and female ones) are your pair. Do not destroy our pair.[^P105_r1_2]

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks.

[^P105_r1_2]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Address to wind (eleven male and eleven female Rudras).

[[P105]]

[[P106]]

### XVIII

1\. Now of Fire (Agni) consisting of eight personages—

2\. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Agni of the eastern quarter. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Jātavedas of the intermediate (south-eastern) quarter. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Sahojas of the southern quarter. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Ajiraprabhava of the intermediate (south-western) quarter. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Vaiśvānara of the western quarter. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Maryāpadas of the intermediate (north-western) quarter. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Paṅktiradhas of the northern quarter. May we shine with our own brilliance at the place of Visarpin of the intermediate (north-eastern) quarter. Om, Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Svaraḥ. The quarters are your pair (of male and female ones). Do not destroy our pair.[^P106_r1_2]

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks.

[^P106_r1_2]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Addresses to eight fires of eight quarters.

[[P107]]

### XIX

1\. In the south-eastern quarter (there is a) hell, Visarpin (by name); save us from that.[^P107_1]

[^P107_1]: Visarpin = of creeping nature. In that hell creatures move always on account of pain.

2\. In the south-western quarter (there is a) hell, Avisarpin (by name); save us from that.[^P107_2]

[^P107_2]: Avisarpin = not of creeping nature. In that hell creatures are unable even to move on account of pain.

3\. In the north-eastern quarter (there is a) hell, Visādin (by name); save us from that.[^P107_3]

[^P107_3]: Visādin = mourning. Creatures mourn for their sins in that hell.

4\. In the north-western quarter (there is a) hell, Avisādin (by name); save us from that.[^P107_4]

[^P107_4]: Avisādin = not mourning. Creatures are unable even to mourn for their sins in that hell.

5\. In whom seven life-giving (suns)[^P107_5]; O Śatakratu, these two (mantras having hundred-fold power) (your) forces[^P107_6] should be uttered.[^P107_r1_4]

[^P107_5]: Initial words of the two mantras for placing bricks.

[^P107_6]: T.S. 1.6.12.

[^P107_r1_4]: *(Mantras 1–4)* Mantras, addressing the bricks to save from the four hells of the four quarters.

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks.

### XX

1\. (O watery bricks,) may Indraghosa, with the Vasus, put you in the east; may the Manojavases, with the fathers, put you in the south; may Pracetas, with Rudras, put you in the west (paścat); may Viśvakarman, with Ādityas, put you in the north; may Tvaṣṭr, with the forms (rūpa), put you above; may Knowledge put you afterwards (paścat).

2\. (O watery bricks,) may Ādityas, the whole-fire (put you) on the earth, the wind, in the mid-region, the sun, in the heaven, the moon, in the quarters (and) the stars, in their regions.

3\. O moving one (Āditya), (you are), indeed, the obtainable things (evah); O wind, (you are), indeed, the obtainable things; O Indra, you are, indeed, the obtainable things; O Pūṣan, (you are), indeed, the obtainable things; O gods, (you are), indeed, the obtainable things (1).[^P107_r1_3]

[^P107_r1_3]: *(Mantras 1–3)* Address to bricks and gods.

> *Note:* Quotations from the first Anuvaka.

[[P108]]

### XXI

1\. "I have obtained, I have obtained all the waters from this and this place, from here and there; may Agni, Vāyu and Sūrya together purify these with prosperity. Having winds as horses, rays as protectors, beams as bodies, not hostile, shining goddesses, all-impelling (O you Waters!) assent me for progeny. Of great name and great honour, inspirers of all mighty ones (1), shining goddesses, rain-impelling (O you Waters!) assent me for progeny. Remove away the consuming and burning fire, the demons, the consuming and burning fever, the exhaustedness caused by fever, un-inhalation (breathlessness) and poverty from this place, shining goddesses (O you Waters!). Shining ones, god-inspirers, (O Waters!) raise up the thunderbolt (for our protection); raise us, undefeated ones, up; raise up the all (cattle etc.); raise up the Ādityas and the goddess Aditi being their cause (2)."[^P108_r1]

[^P108_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* See T.A. 1.1.9.

2\. May we hear the auspicious (things) with ears, O gods; may we, the sacrificers, see the auspicious things with eyes; with bodies of strong limbs may we, praising, obtain the (span of) life, which is assigned by the gods. May Indra of great fame grant us welfare; may all-knowing Pūṣan grant us welfare; may Tārkṣya, the Ariṣṭanemi (one whose felly of wheel is unhurt) grant us welfare; may Bṛhaspati grant us welfare. (May) the devout seers Ketus, Aruṇases (Aruṇas) (and) Vātaraśanas (put) the thousand-fold firm-standing in a hundred ways. May the divine waters and the plants be auspicious and most wealful for us. O Sarasvati, (you are) well merciful; may your hole not be seen (3).[^P108_2]

[^P108_2]: Sarasvati = earth full of stream or watery bricks. Hole not seen on account of the covering of the plants.

> *Note:* Mantras for placing bricks.

[[P109]]

### XXII

> *Note:* Brāhmaṇas (praises etc.) relating to the placing of bricks. 1–9. Waters as the abodes of the things.

1\. He, who knows the flower of the waters, becomes the possessor of the flower, progeny and cattle. The moon (Candramas) is, verily, the flower of the waters.[^P109_1] He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of flower, progeny and cattle.

[^P109_1]: The moon is considered Amṛtamaya (possessor of nectar), and therefore, the flower of the waters.

2\. He, who knows the abode of the waters, becomes the possessor of an abode. The fire is, verily, the abode of the waters. He, who knows the abode of the fire, becomes the possessor of an abode; (yea) becomes the possessor of an abode. Waters are, verily, the abode of the fire.[^P109_2] He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of an abode (1).

[^P109_2]: According to Sāyaṇa in the beginning of the great creation the waters were born from the fire (see agner apaḥ: T.A. 8.2) and in the creation of the Brāhmaṇas the waters come first (āpo vā idamasantī salilameva T.A. 1.23).

3\. He, who knows the abode of the waters, becomes the possessor of an abode. The wind is, verily, the abode of the waters. He, who knows the abode of the wind, becomes the possessor of an abode; (yes) becomes the possessor of an abode. The waters are, verily, the abode of the wind.[^P109_3] He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of an abode (2).[^P109_4]

[^P109_3]: In the great creation... (see vayoragniḥ / agnerapaḥ etc. as above in #2).

[^P109_4]: T.A. 8.2.

4\. He, who knows the abode of the waters, becomes the possessor of an abode. That burning (sun) is, verily, the abode of the waters. He, who knows the abode of that burning (sun), becomes the possessor of an abode, (yes) becomes the possessor of an abode.[^P109_5]

[^P109_5]: On account of the burning sun the rain is born.

[[P110]]

The waters are, verily, the abode of that burning (sun) (3). He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of an abode.

5\. He, who knows the abode of the waters, becomes the possessor of an abode. The moon, verily, is the abode of the waters. He, who knows the abode of the moon, becomes the possessor of an abode; (yea) becomes the possessor of an abode. The waters are, verily, the abode of the moon. He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of an abode (4).[^P110_1]

[^P110_1]: The moon has dewy rays, or, according to Bhaṭṭa Bhāskara, the moon has nectar, of which the water is a product; so the moon has been called the abode of the waters.

6\. He, who knows the abode of the waters, becomes the possessor of an abode. The constellations are, verily, the abode of the waters. He, who knows the abode of the constellations, becomes the possessor of an abode, (yes) becomes the possessor of an abode. The waters are, verily, the abode of constellations. He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of an abode (5).[^P110_2]

[^P110_2]: In the time of some constellations such as in Puṣya, Āśleṣā, it rains much, so they have been called the abode of the waters.

7\. He, who knows the abode of the waters, becomes the possessor of an abode. The rain is, verily, the abode of the waters. He, who knows the abode of the rain, becomes the possessor of an abode, (yea) becomes the possessor of an abode. The waters are, verily, the abode of the rain. He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of an abode.

8\. He, who knows the abode of the waters, becomes the possessor of an abode. The year is, verily, the abode of the waters.[^P110_3]

[^P110_3]: In the year it rains, so the year is the abode of the waters.

[[P111]]

He, who knows the abode of the year, becomes, verily, the possessor of an abode, (yes) becomes the possessor of an abode. The waters are, verily, the abode of the year. He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of an abode (6).

9\. He, who knows the boat established in the waters, becomes established. These worlds are, verily, established in the waters. As to that this has been mentioned:—

10\. (All the worlds) have come forth from the elixir of the waters which is procured well in the sun (as the) white (elixir).[^P111_1] (O waters!) I take your best elixir which is of the elixir of the waters.

[^P111_1]: i.e. the water of the rain.

11\. These worlds are, verily, the elixir of the waters. They are procured well in the sun (7).[^P111_2]

[^P111_2]: Meaning of the Mantra given in 10.

12\. (One should place the bricks after) digging the Uttara Vedi reaching up to the knee, filling the waters reaching up to the ankle (8); covering by the petals of the lotus, the sticks of the lotus and the lotus-flowers; (and after) bringing fire (and) establishing (that) in that atmosphere.[^P111_3]

[^P111_3]: Preparation of the altar.

13\. The speakers of the sacred text (Brāhmaṇa) say: Why this brought Fire (Aruṇaketuka) is (arranged in order) again (by placing the watery bricks etc.)? For (this fire is) with (or in) the waters (also, therefore, this is arranged in order) in the waters (after being) brought. This fire, (if) not kept, may go in the world also (afraid of the waters); (therefore,) (one) puts these watery bricks around that (fire).[^P111_4]

[^P111_4]: The reason of placing the bricks after bringing the fire.

[[P112]]

14\. (The limbs of this Aruṇaketuka Fire are) in the Agnihotra (sacrifice), in the Darśapūrṇamāsa (sacrifice), in the Paśubandha (sacrifice) (9); and (they) say "In all the sacrificial performances", thus.[^P112_1]

[^P112_1]: For what sacrifices above Fire is needed.

15\. This, indeed, said Śāṇḍilya, the descendants of Śāṇḍila (in the form of questions and answers). What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Satriya Fire? Perceptibly (giving as the result) the year (Saṁvatsara).

16\. What (resulting) Fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Sāvitra Fire? Perceptibly (giving as the result) that Āditya (sun).

17\. What (resulting) Fire (one) collects (10) (while) collecting the Naciketa Fire? Perceptibly the Prāṇas (breaths).

18\. What (resulting) Fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Caturhotriya Fire? Perceptibly (giving as the result) the Brahman.

19\. What (resulting) Fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Vaiśvasṛja Fire? Perceptibly (giving as the result) the body.

20\. What (resulting) Fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Upanuvākya Āśu Fire? Perceptibly (giving as the result) these worlds (11).

21\. What (resulting) Fire (one) collects (while) collecting this Aruṇaketuka Fire? Thus. (Giving in the result) that (God or Sun) who does not go leaving (the worlds) from here and there. Thus.

22\. Who knows the pair-goings of the Fire, becomes the possessor of (the power of forming) the pair. The Waters are, verily, the pair-goings of the Fire. He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of (the power of forming) the pair (12).[^P112_2]

[^P112_2]: 15–21. Questions and answers. 22. A Brāhmaṇa (explanatory or praising) passage.

> *Note:* 14. For what sacrifices above Fire is needed.

[[P113]]

### XXIII

1\. Waters were, verily, this (creation), water only. That one Prajāpati came forth in a lotus-petal. In the midst of his mind the Kāma (Desire) was produced: May I create this (world)! Therefore, what a man thinks in the mind, speaks that by the speech and performs that by action. As to that this is (said):—

2\. Kāma (Desire) came forth in the very beginning, which was the first (flow of) semen of the mind (1). The intelligent sages, deciding in their hearts, found the relation of the being (sat) in the non-being (asat).

3\. Who knows thus, to him that comes which he desires.

4\. He (Prajāpati) performed austerity. After performing austerity he shook (his) body. Which was his flesh, from that stood the seers Aruṇas, Ketus and Vātaraśanas (2). Which (were) the nails, they (became) Vaikhānasas; which (were) the hairs, they became Vālakhilyas; which (was) the elixir, that (became a tortoise in the midst) of the waters.

5\. (Prajāpati) said to him, creeping in the (water) in the shape of a tortoise "(You) are born from my skin and flesh (3)". "No" he said, "I was here before (you)". That is (Pūrvamāsam) the (etymological essence) Pūruṣtva of the Puruṣa (person). He stood being thousand-headed (and) thousand-eyed. (Prajāpati) said to him "You have been before (me), (so) do this (creation) as (my) predecessor".

> *Note:* In this Anuvāka the praise of the waters and the Aruṇaketuka Fire are given by giving the description of the creation and quoting some Ṛks. 1–14. The creation.

[[P114]]

6\. He taking the waters from here put by his hollowed hand (añjali) eastward (uttering the mantra) "O moving one (Āditya, i.e. sun) (you are) the obtainable things (Evah)". Then stood forth the sun (Āditya); that (is) the eastern quarter.

7\. Now Aruṇa Ketu (personified by that Puruṣa in the shape of the tortoise) put (the water) southward (uttering the Mantra) "O Agni, you are the obtainable things". Then stood forth the fire (Agni); that (in) the southern quarter. Now Aruṇa Ketu put (the water) westward (uttering the Mantra) "O Vāyu, you are the obtainable things (5)". Then stood forth the wind (Vāyu); that (is) the western quarter. Now Aruṇa Ketu put (the water) northward (uttering the Mantra) "O Indra, you are the obtainable things". Then stood forth Indra; that (is) the northern quarter. Now, Aruṇa Ketu put (the water) in the middle (uttering the Mantra) "O Pūṣan, you are the obtainable things". Then stood forth Pūṣan; that is this (belowward) quarter (6). Now, Aruṇa Ketu put (the water) above (uttering the Mantra) "O gods, you are the obtainable things". Then stood forth the gods, the men, the Gandharvas and the Apsarases; that is the aboveward quarter.

8\. Which drops fell hither and thither, by them stood forth the Asuras, the Rākṣasas and the Piśācas; therefore, they were defeated, for they were born of the drops.

9\. As to that this has been said (7):—

10\. When the great waters, verily, obtained the womb; keeping the increasing (womb); producing the Svayambhū (born himself); then these creations were created. This was produced, verily, from the waters. Therefore, all this is Brahma Svayambhū.

11\. (For the creation was from the loose waters,) therefore, all this became as loose and not fixed.

12\. Prajāpati, indeed, making himself the soul entered that very (creation).

[[P115]]

13\. As to that this has been said:—

14\. Disposing the world, disposing the beings, disposing the all main quarters and (the other) quarters Prajāpati, the first-born of the Ṛta (settled order), entered in himself (in the world or body) as Ātman (1).

15\. He, who knows thus, enters that after obtaining all this and enclosing all (2).[^P115_15]

[^P115_15]: A Brāhmaṇa-passage (mentioning the fruit of knowing the description of the creation).

> *Note:* Collection of the waters has been discussed in this Anuvāka. 1–7. Collection of the waters from the various sources.

### XXIV

1\. (One) takes the waters of the four kinds. Four are the forms of the waters: the cloud, the lightning, the thunder (and) the rain. (He) encloses those verily.

[[P116]]

2\. (One) takes the waters of the rain in a hot (place); puts them in the east. These are, verily, the waters having the brilliance of the Brahman. (He) encloses the brilliance of the Brahman by (his) mouth, verily. Therefore, (one) is more brilliant by (his) mouth (1).

3\. (One) takes the waters of the well; puts them in the south. These are, verily, the splendid waters. (Hence) the splendidness is put in his right (i.e. Dakṣiṇa = south and right) side (2).

4\. (One) takes the waters of the immovable (abyss); puts them in the west. Fixed are, verily, these waters of the immovable (abyss). (He) becomes fixed, verily, by (his) lower half of the limbs (paścat) (3).

5\. (One) takes the flowing waters; puts them in the north. By vigour, verily, these are (seen) like flowing, like rising, like noising and running. (He) puts vigour, verily, in his higher (limb). Therefore, the higher-half (of the body) is more vigorous (4).

6\. (One) takes the waters (that are) filled (in the houses by vessels etc.); puts them in the middle. This (earth) is, verily, (that, which is) filled. (He) becomes established in this very (earth) (5).

7\. (One) takes the waters of the tanks; puts them above.[^P116_7] These are, verily, waters of the tanks (which exist on the earth after being dug). He becomes established in that (heaven) (6).

[^P116_7]: cf. 1.21.3.

8\. (One) puts the (waters) in the quarters. In the quarters are, verily, the waters. The waters are, verily, the food; for the food is grown by the waters. (He) encloses all food which is grown by the waters (7).[^P116_r8]

[^P116_r8]: *(Kaṇḍikā 8)* Putting of the Waters in the quarters.

9\. Aruṇas, Ketus and Vātaraśanas collected this very Fire; so this is called Aruṇaketuka (8).[^P116_r9_11]

[^P116_r9_11]: *(Kaṇḍikās 9–11)* Aruṇas, Ketus and Vātaraśanas, the collectors of the Aruṇaketuka Fire.

10\. As to that this has been said:—

11\. (May) the devout seers Ketus, Aruṇases (Aruṇas) and Vātaraśanas (put) the thousand-fold, firm-standing in a hundred ways (9).

12\. He stands firmly in hundred and thousand ways, who collects this fire and also he, who knows thus (10).[^P116_12]

[^P116_12]: A Brāhmaṇa-passage (mentioning the fruit of knowing this).

[[P117]]

### XXV

1\. Digging the Uttara Vedi up to the knee (one) fills (that) by the waters (reaching up to the ankle) for the wholeness of the waters (1).

2\. (One) puts the lotus-petal, the gold and the man (made of gold); for the lotus-petal is, verily, the austerity; the gold, the truth; the man (made of gold), the nectar. This is, verily, all whatever is this (existence). Whatever is (existent) encloses (2); that.[^P117_1]

[^P117_1]: cf. T.S. 5.2.3.

3\. (One) puts the tortoise, (hence) encloses the essence of the waters; and (he puts that) for the sake of obtaining well the heavenly world (3).

4\. "I have obtained, I have obtained all the waters from this and this place, from here and there; may Agni[^P117_1a], Vāyu[^P117_2a] and Sūrya[^P117_3a] together purify these with prosperity."[^P117_r4_1] "Having winds as horses, rays as beams (4)."[^P117_r4_2]

[^P117_1a]: Fire-god.

[^P117_2a]: Wind-god.

[^P117_3a]: Sun-god.

[^P117_r4_1]: *(Mantras 1 and 2)* Initial words of the Mantras, T.A. 1.1 for placing the watery bricks.

[^P117_r4_2]: *(Mantras 1 and 2)* Initial words of the Mantras, T.A. 1.20 for placing the bricks.

5\. (O bricks) fill the (empty) place; fill the hole (5); (fill also those) that are three born of the best.

6\. "(May) Indraghosa with the Vasus put you" (and) "O moving one (Āditya), (you are) the obtainable things (Evah) (6)."[^P117_6]

[^P117_6]: Initial words of the Mantras, T.A. 1.20 for placing the bricks.

7\. (One) puts the five piles (of water, lotus etc.); Agni is five-fold; (he) collects that (7).[^P117_r7]

[^P117_r7]: *(From 1 to 9)* Placing of lotus-petal etc.

[[P118]]

8\. By (the Mantra) "fill the place" (one) puts the second. Virāt (verse) is, verily, five-footed. Of that this (earth) is foot; mid-region (is) foot; heaven (is) foot; quarters (are) foot; one beyond the regions is foot (8).

9\. He stands firm in the Virāt (food or body) who collects this fire and (he) also who knows thus (9).

> *Note:* Brāhmaṇas (praises etc.) and Mantras concerning placing of lotus-petal etc. (from 1 to 9).

[[P119]]

### XXVI

1\. After bringing (and) establishing fire (one) puts these watery bricks around that. (The limbs of this Aruṇaketuka fire are) in the Agnihotra (sacrifice), in the Darśapūrṇamāsa (sacrifice), in the Paśubandha (sacrifice), in the Cāturmāsya (sacrifice) and (they) say "In all the sacrificial performances". Thus (1).

2\. Aruṇa, the son of Svāyambhu, said, "The Sāvitra Fire is all the fires"; (but we) consider the fires not concurrent; for there are various powers of the same (2).[^P119_1]

[^P119_1]: Aruṇas, Ketus and Vātaraśanas collected this very Fire; so this is called Aruṇaketuka. According to that view Sāvitra Fire should be collected while collecting the other fires as a rule, which is contradicted here.

3\. What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Satriya Fire? What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Sāvitra Fire? What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Naciketa Fire? What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Caturhotra Fire? What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Vaiśvasṛja Fire? What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting the Upanuvākya Āśu Fire? What (resulting) fire (one) collects (while) collecting this Aruṇaketuka Fire? This (3).[^P119_2]

[^P119_2]: cf. T.A. 1.20.

4\. Impregnating is, verily, this fire: (some enemy) may trouble his impregnating (testicles) and destroy the sacrifice. Therefore (one) should not make (the fire) concurrent (4).

5\. (One) should collect (the Aruṇaketuka Fire) in the sacrifices which have the Uttara Vedi (eastern altar); for this fire is collected in the Uttara Vedi (5).

6\. (One who is) desirous of offspring should collect (this fire) (6); this is, verily, related to Prajāpati; offspring are related to Prajapati. He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of offspring.

7\. (One who is) desirous of cattle should collect (this fire); this is, verily, knowledge of cattle; in knowledge of cattle (one) collects this fire. He, who knows thus, becomes the possessor of cattle (7).[^P119_3]

[^P119_3]: The cattle know where there is water and this fire is collected placing the watery bricks.

[[P120]]

8\. (One who is) desirous of the rain should collect (this fire); the waters are, verily, the rain; the cloud becomes raining (for him) who knows thus (8).

9\. (One) who is desirous of protection from the disease should collect (this fire); the waters are, verily, the medicine; it makes medicine for him; he obtains the whole span of life (9).

10\. (One who is) desirous of exorcising should collect (this fire); the waters are, verily, the thunderbolt; he strikes the thunderbolt upon his rivals (and) kills those rivals (10).

11\. (One who is) desirous of brilliance, desirous of fame, desirous of brilliance of sacred knowledge (and) desirous of the heaven should collect (this fire). This much is, verily, whatever is this. He encloses whatever is this (11).

12\. This is the rule of that: (one) should not run (while it is) raining; the waters are, verily, the nectar: for not making away the nectar (12).

13\. (One) should not void urine and excrement in the waters, should not spit, should not bathe without clothes; for this is hidden fire, (so) for not burning this (13).

14\. (One) should not stand on the-lotus-petal or the gold; for not standing on this fire (14).

15\. (One) should not eat the (meat) of the tortoise nor of the (beings) of the water; the waters become not killing for him. The waters (become) not killing (for him) who collects this fire (and) even he, who knows thus (15).

> *Note:* Discussions on fire piling and rules about that (from 1 to 15).

### XXVII

1\. Indra, Viśvedevas (All-gods) (and we) shall prosper these worlds happily and with offspring.

2\. May Indra with Ādityas prosper our sacrifice, body and offspring.[^P120_1]

[^P120_1]: *(Mantras 1–20)* Praises for the protection (from 1 to 20).

3\. May Indra with (his) group, with Ādityas (and) Rudras be the protector of our bodies.

[[P121]]

4\. O born (Yajamāna) do not come (be born) and go (die) and become a being in the world again and again; hold your own place which is the beginning of the happiness and the end of the troubles (1).

5\. May the rays of Svayaṁbhū, which created the bodies (of the worlds), create your body and may not your fame be vanished.

6\. Stand firm, do not sleep, O Bhāratas, desire the Fire (sacrifice) being satisfied by drinking the King Soma and rising as the sun.

7\. "The well-clothed young..."[^P121_1]

[^P121_1]: Initial words of a Mantra of another Śākhā. See T.S. 2.5.9.

8\. Of eight wheels, nine doors (2) is the town (Pur) Ayodhyā of the gods. There is a golden sheath, the heavenly world covered of the brilliance.[^P121_2]

[^P121_2]: An allegorical passage. Pur: body; eight wheels: blood, flesh, semen etc.; nine doors: nine holes of the body, etc.

9\. Brahman and Brahmā grant him life, fame and offspring, who knows that town (Purī) of Brahman, full of nectar.

10\. In that shining, (sin-)ending, full of fame, golden, not defeated town (Purā) Brahmā entered[^P121_3] (3).

[^P121_3]: I.e. Brahmā (Ātman) entered the body.

11\. The knower goes to the not returning world, where there is no end of life, disease and destruction; he pervades here and there in the gods and the Asuras (demons) both.

12\. What (an unmarried) girl enjoys; and what a woman, follower of her husband (enjoys sinfully); whatever sin is done, the Fire destroys all that.

[[P122]]

13\. Young and old (4), sacrificing and even not sacrificing ones, who know Indra and Fire, do not want anything while going to the heavenly world.

14\. (The beings) like the sands impelled by the rays become gathered at some place running from this world to that world. (After this the God) has given (Knowledge) through the brilliant seers.

15\. (O men of Yama), who are here old and new, escape sway, be separated and run away from here. Full of day and night (5) this place may Yama grant for him.

16\. May the (wicked) men, who are born of bad or good family or who have been in the wombs of the unmarried girls and women having paramour, move away (from here) and may (their) masters protect us (by going away).

17\. I separate those going to Yama (to move away) from here who have drunk the semen and testicles; who have been burnt on the flames (and become ghosts) and both (may they be even) sons and grandsons.

18\. "Hundred autumns, indeed..."[^P122_1]

[^P122_1]: Initial words of a Mantra of another Śākhā.

19\. Which is this Brahman, that is the depending place of the Fathers, Yama, Varuṇa, Aśvins, Fire, Maruts and beings of the sky.

20\. May the desire-fulfilling (God grant) mine; for I am that eternal one. Hence O divine Waters grant for me heaven, bearing of sacred knowledge, gold, wealth and offspring (6).

[[P123]]

### XXVIII

1\. Remove away from here the headless and vulture-headed misfortune and the hinderers having the white cavity of the belly, low shanks and variegated belly.

2\. O Fire, you as such destroy from (our) view those (hinderers) with (that) abusable (misfortune) and those who increase envy (Trāyas), irritation (Asūyā), anger and bewitchery with their creature-perishing chariot (1).[^P123_1]

[^P123_1]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Addresses to Agni to remove away the evils.

### XXIX

1\. Laud Parjanya (rain), the son of heaven, the sprinkler; may that desire food for us.

2\. May this praise enter the heart of Parjanya, make it mingled there. May the wind and all the creatures be comfortable for us; and may the plants full of fruits become the protectors of the gods (through sacrifice).

3\. (May that Parjanya be comfortable for us) which causes impregnation of the cows, mares and women (1).[^P123_2]

[^P123_2]: *(Mantras 1–3)* Addresses to Parjanya to remove away the evils.

[[P124]]

### XXX

1\. May the powers of the senses (Indriya) come to me again; (may) life (come) again, (may) fortune (come) again; may the (brilliance) of sacred knowledge (Brahma) come to me again; may the wealth come to me again.

2\. I take that semen again for the long life and brilliance, which has fallen on earth or which has gone in plants or in waters.

3\. Make me immortal and possessor of good offspring by that semen which is sprinkled and which is born of me (in the form of an offspring) again (1).[^P124_1]

[^P124_1]: *(Mantras 1–3)* Mantras for the protection of the semen and becoming brilliant, immortal etc.

### XXXI

1\. (O Kubera!) Vaiśravaṇa was born the coverer of your deeds always; cover our enemies who cover our deeds.

2\. Come to our sacrifice (O you) Vaiśravaṇa riding the chariot which is (wonderful like the) illusion of Tvaṣṭṛ, which has thousand fellies, many wheels and thousand horses.

3\. May we be under the mercy of that holy one, who has the shining prosperity of food etc.; for whom the beings bring oblation, wealth, cows, elephant, gold and horses (1).

4\. On the Sudarśana and the Krauñca and the great mountain Maināka is your worth leaving (for coming to our sacrifices) city having hundred doors, rooms and highways.[^P124_2]

[^P124_2]: *(Mantras 1–15)* The Vaiśravaṇa ceremony.

[[P125]]

5\. These are the Mantras; now (we shall give) the Kalpa (manner of practice) (further):—

6\. If (one) has to bring the oblation, he should bring that saying, "This oblation is for (Vaiśravaṇa who is) related to Kubera, who has gold on his navel (as an ornament), who gives trouble (by having gold centred around him only)" (2); salutation to the lord of all. After bringing the oblation he should be present there.

7\. Vaiśravaṇa is the protector, (he is) Kṣatriya (by caste); we are the Brāhmaṇas; may salutation be to you; do not, do not kill; for our sake take the food entering here.

8\. After this (one) should establish that fire in which he has to use this work.

9\. (O fire) cover the earth, cover the mid-region (3), cover the heaven; cover the earth, the mid-region and the heaven. Sit in the sovereignty of all the worlds.

10\. After this (one) should burn that fire in which he has to use this work.

11\. (O Agni) cover the earth; hail! cover the mid-region; hail! cover the heaven; hail! cover the earth, the mid-region and the heaven; hail!

12\. In which time all his oblations (of Aruṇaketuka Fire) are finished (4), even those uttered in the Brāhmaṇa (i.e. Śatarudrīya, etc.), in that time of that day he should use this (Vaiśravaṇa-sacrifice); or (he should use it) when all are slept.

13\. (One) should not teach a swerving (pupil); all the sins, all the aims become accomplished (of him), who knows thus; hungry (persons, who) do not know this, are not accomplished.

[[P126]]

14\. (O Kubera), who is your killing brother, (who) entered my heart (5), for him I sacrifice this first oblation (Agrapiṇḍa); may he not kill our aim; (may he put that) in me; hail!

15\. We salute Vaiśravaṇa (who is) the great king of the kings, able to force; may that lord of the desires, Vaiśravaṇa grant me, (who am) desirous of the desirable things, my desires. Salutation to Kubera, to Vaiśravaṇa, the great king. May the devout seers Ketus, Āruṇasas (Aruṇas) and Vātaraśanas put the thousand-fold firm-standing in a hundred ways. May the divine waters and the plants be auspicious and most wealful for us. O Sarasvatī, (you are) well merciful; may your hole not be seen (6).[^P126_1]

[^P126_1]: *(Mantra 15)* Two months.

### XXXII

1\. One should follow this rule (Vrata) for a year, or for two months.[^P126_r1_4]

[^P126_r1_4]: *(Mantras 1–4)* The rules (Vratas) to be followed before collecting the Aruṇaketuka Fire.

2\. In short the rule (is):—

3\. There are the special rules in that. He should touch the water (take bath) at the three Savanas (three times of pressing the soma-juice in a day); he should take drink and rice in the fourth time (of the day). He should serve the fire by the fuels of the Ficus Glomerata (Udumbara) by the (Mantras of) the Anuvāka (beginning with the words), "may the powers of the senses (Indriya) come to me". He should work by the brought and filtered waters (1).[^P126_2] He should be one not collecting (money etc.). The oblations of this rule (Vrata) are for Fire, Wind, Sun, Brahman, Prajāpati, Candramas, Stars, Seasons and Year, Varuṇa and Aruna. The order is like the Pravargya (sacrifice). In this Kāṇḍa Arunas (etc.) are the seers.

[^P126_2]: See T.A. 1.1.9 & 1.21.3 etc.

[[P127]]

4\. One should read this in a forest after repeating twice the Mantra, "(may we hear) the auspicious things with ears";[^P127_1] by the Mahānāmnī Mantras touching the water the teacher should give to him (the sacrificer); by the Mantras, "(may the divine waters and the plants be) wealful and most auspicious" he touches the plants, and by the Mantras, "well merciful" he touches the earth. (He should do) thus in the end (of the reading). The Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee) is a cow, a vessel made of metal and the cloth made of linen or any other white (cloth) or according to the power (one should give the sacrificial fee). In this way, as the rule of the study, he should read in a forest. He becomes the pious ascetic, (yes) becomes the pious ascetic.

[^P127_1]: > *Note:* May we hear the auspicious (things) with ears, O gods; may we, the sacrificers see the auspicious (things) with eyes; with bodies of strong limbs may we, praising, obtain (the span of) life which is assigned by the gods. May Indra of great fame grant us welfare; may all-knowing Pūṣan grant us welfare; may Tārkṣya, the Ariṣṭanemi (one whose felly of wheel is unhurt) grant us welfare; may Bṛhaspati grant us welfare. Om Peace! Peace! Peace! *(Mantras for auspices and peace uttered again at the end of the Prapāṭhaka.)*

[[P128]]

## II

[[P129]]

### I

Hariḥ Om.

Salutation to Brahman, may salutation be to Fire, salutation to earth; salutation to the plants, salutation to Vāc (speech), salutation to Vācaspati (lord of speech), I make salutation to great Viṣṇu.

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

1\. Om the sacrifices of the gods (Devas) and the demons (Asuras) were spread together (with the desire) 'we shall go to the heavenly world, we shall go to the heavenly world'. Those Asuras being equipped conducted all of a sudden; and the Devas (performed their sacrifice) by the austerity. Those Asuras became stupified; they did not know, they were defeated and did not reach the heaven. The Devas reached to the heavenly world by the well-performed sacrifice and they defeated the Asuras, having no well-performed sacrifice.

2\. The sacrifice of the Yajñopavītin[^P129_1] is, indeed, the well-performed one and not Yajñopavītin is not well-performed. Whatever a Brāhmaṇa, the Yajñopavītin reads, he sacrifices, verily, (in) that (way).[^P129_r1_3]

[^P129_1]: Yajñopavītin = one who wears the sacred thread called Yajñopavīta.

[^P129_r1_3]: *(Mantras 1–3)* Importance of the sacrificial thread. The description of the Yajñopavīta (sacrificial thread).

[[P130]]

3\. Therefore, after being Yajñopavītin one should read, cause sacrifice or sacrifice himself for the sake of well-performance.

4\. (One becomes Yajñopavītin) wearing the hairy skin of an antelope or cloth in the right side.[^P130_r4_5]

[^P130_r4_5]: *(Mantras 4–5)* The articles of the Yajñopavīta and its wearing.
