5\. (One) puts upon the right hand and beneath the left hand; this is (called) the Yajñopavīta; contradictory to this is (called) the Prācīnāvīta; and that which is worn upon both shoulders is called the Mānuṣa (manly) (1).

### II

1\. Rākṣasas (demons) did, verily, great austerity in the ancient time. Prajāpati asked them to choose some boon. (They said), "May Āditya become fighter against us". Prajāpati said to them, "fight". Therefore, the Rākṣasas fight against the rising sun (Āditya) until that sets. These Rākṣasas become destroyed by the waters consecrated by the Gāyatrī Mantra.

2\. Therefore, these Brahmavādins (devoted to sacred knowledge), sitting for Sandhyā (worship) with their face to the east throw above the waters consecrated by the Gāyatrī Mantra.[^P130_r1_5] These waters becoming as thunderbolt put away those Rākṣasas in the Maṇḍaheruṇa island.

[^P130_r1_5]: *(Mantras 1–5)* The importance of the Gāyatrī-Mantra (Sandhyā).

3\. They move in which Pradakṣiṇa (to turn towards something so as to place that on one's right)[^P130_1] by that they vanish (their) sins.

[^P130_1]: "To turn towards something so as to place that on one's right".

[[P131]]

4\. A learned Brāhmaṇa making meditation and Pradakṣiṇa of the rising and setting sun (Āditya), obtains all auspicious things; that sun (Āditya) is Brahman.

5\. He, who knows thus, obtains Brahman being Brahman (himself) (1).

### III

1\. O divine gods, Ādityas, whatever have we caused, the anger to the gods, set us free from that and obtain me by the Ṛta (settled order) of the Ṛtas (sacrifice).

2\. O gods, whatever have we said false by the speech for the desire of life, set us free here from that, O all-gods (Viśvedevas) having mutual love.

3\. By Ṛtas, O Heaven and Earth, by Ṛta, O you Sarasvatī, protect us from the sin, which has been done and from the false which we have spoken.

4\. May these Indra, Fire, Mitra, Varuṇa, Soma, Dhātṛ and Bṛhaspati set us free from the sin, done by the others, which we have obtained.

5\. O Fire (Jātavedas), set us free from that invincible sin which is done in relation to the gods (on account of being careless) from the praise of the friends, from the praise of the sisters, from the praise of elder ones or even from the praise of the younger ones.[^P131_1]

[^P131_1]: *(Mantras 1–9)* Mantras of the Kūṣmāṇḍa Homa.

[[P132]]

6\. May the Household Fire set us free from the violation of the settled order (Anṛta) and from the misdeeds which we have done by the speech, by the mind, by the hands, by the thighs, by the knees and by the generative organs.

7\. By whom Trita[^P132_1] became out of the ocean (of the sin); by whom (the God himself) set free the sun from the darkness; by whom Indra killed all the enemies; being pervaded by that Brilliance (God) may we obtain the brilliance.

[^P132_1]: Trita = fire; cf. T.Br. 3.2.8.

8\. O Fire, may we become free from the debt, which has not been paid by me and by which I move as the treasure of Yama; may I pay that in my living period here by this sacrifice.

9\. "What the mother in my..."[^P132_2]; "what troubled..."[^P132_3]; "what to mid-region", "what by great violence"; "I come over"; "In Trita (fire) the gods"; "Born in heaven"; "what the waters..."[^P132_4]; "O Varṇa my this..."[^P132_5]; "That I beg you..."[^P132_6]; "you for us, O Fire..."[^P132_7], "O Fire, you as such for us..."[^P132_8]; "O Fire you (are) the obtainable..."[^P132_9] (Mantras beginning with these words should be uttered) (1).

[^P132_2]: #1-8. Mantras from the T.Br. 3.7.12.
[^P132_3]: #1-8. Mantras from the T.Br. 3.7.12.
[^P132_4]: #1-8. Mantras from the T.Br. 3.7.12.
[^P132_5]: #9-10. T.S. 2.1.11.
[^P132_6]: #9-10. T.S. 2.1.11.
[^P132_7]: #11-12. T.S. 2.5.12.
[^P132_8]: #11-12. T.S. 2.5.12.
[^P132_9]: T.Br. 2.4.1.

### IV

1\. Whatever debt I have found, being unable; or whatever, not paying, have I eaten of the men (who gave me debt); from that may Fire and Indra being concordant, set me free.

[[P133]]

2\. Whatever sins have we committed by our hands, by avoiding the works of the eyes (and other senses); may the Apsarases (named) Ugrampaśyā and Rāṣṭrabhṛt pay out those debts.

3\. O Ugrampaśyā and Rāṣṭrabhṛt, whatever sins have we committed and what (have we not paid in) the gambling; pay that; may not the giver of the debt, equal to us (being a man), use the rope (for binding) us, the indebted ones in the region of Yama.

4\. "Down your anger, O Varuna..."[^P133_1]; "Above, (your) best..."[^P133_2]; "O Varuna this my..."[^P133_3]; "I go to you..."[^P133_4]; "O Fire, you... our..."[^P133_5]; "you as such, O Fire, ... our..."[^P133_6] (these six Mantras should be repeated).

[^P133_1]: T.S. 1.5.11.
[^P133_2]: T.S. 1.5.11.
[^P133_3]: T.S. 2.1.11.
[^P133_4]: T.S. 2.1.11.
[^P133_5]: T.S. 2.5.12.
[^P133_6]: T.S. 2.5.12.

5\. (If we are) those (sinners and) abusers, abuse-spreading ones, troublesome and backbiters; may the sinless (gods) destroy that disease (sin) from us (taking that) away and away.

6\. I destroy the disease (sin), bewitching and poverty; I impell these (bewitching etc.) (to return) to him, who used these for our destruction.

7\. Who desires to destroy us by the abuser, repeating abuser, killer and an assistant killer; for him I impell these (to return).

8\. May we go together with brilliance, with milk, with bodies, and with auspicious mind; may Tvaṣṭṛ give us wealth and wash away whatever the little sins are with our bodies (1).

[[P134]]

### V

> *Note:* Mantras of the Kūṣmāṇḍa Homa. (From 1 to 17).

1\. May this fire put your life all round; your breath (Prāṇa) comes again; I destroy your disease away.

2\. O Fire, become the giver of the life taking oblations, beginning with ghee and caused by ghee; drinking the sweet and clean ghee of the cow protect this (sacrificer), as a father (protects) the son.

3\. O Fire, make this (sacrificer) powerful for life and brilliance; O Varuna, make the sharp vitality more sharp; O Aditi, give him comfort as a mother (gives to her son); O All-gods (Viśvedevas), make him so that he may be alive till the old age.

4\. O Fire, make our lives pure; impell vigour and food (to come) to us; destroy away the poverty.

5\. O Fire, (you have) good deeds; make our brilliance and good vigour pure giving me wealth and nourishment (1).

6\. Fire is the seer (Ṛṣi), the purifying one, related to the fire Janas (castes) and the priest (Purohita = one who is put forward first); we pray that great household Fire.

7\. O Fire destroy all our enemies, who have been born; and also destroy those, who have not been born; make us shine being pleasant-minded and not angry, protecting in three ways; may we be under your shining comfort.

8\. O Fire (Jātavedas), destroy our enemies, who have been born suddenly, and also destroy those, who have not been born; being pleasant-minded speak in our favour; may we become the destroyers of our enemies.

[[P135]]

9\. O Fire, destroyer of the enemies, kill those, who are desirous to kill us as a wolf (or) a restraining person; fulfil wealth for us.

10\. O Fire, we pour him to you as fuel, who treats us as enemies (may he be) equal (to us) or lower (than us) (2).

11\. May Uṣas (Dawn) and Nimruk (Evening) carry all the sins for him, who curses us (while) we curse him or we do not curse him.

12\. As the fire does not leave the little of the burning and vanishing fuel, similarly O gods, do not leave anything of that man, who is our enemy and fighter.

13\. O Fire, burn all those who hate me, whom I hate, and who hate me.

14\. Making the sounds of the flames burn all those who become our enemy, who hates us, who abuses us and who harms us.

15\. Sharpened (is) my Brahma (sacred knowledge); sharpened (is) the semen and vigour; sharpened (is) my King (Kṣatriya), whose I am the priest.

16\. I have made strong the hands and power of these people; by my Brahman (sacred knowledge) I destroy the enemies and raise up our own people.

17\. May the mind come to me again; may the life come to me again; may the eye come to me again; may the ear come to me again; may the breath come to me again; may the appointed work come to me again; may the heart come to me again; may the study come to me again. May the Fire (Vaiśvānara) destroy away all our sins, being our unharmed protector of the body (3).

[[P136]]

### VI

> *Note:* Mantras of the Kūṣmāṇḍa Homa. (From 1 to 13).

1\. Whatever is the debt of the gods in the form of promise;[^P136_1] I inform that to the Fire (Vaiśvānara); he knows how to set free from these bindings; may he set us free from the abusable sin.

[^P136_1]: The man is born with three debts and with the promise to pay that; see T.S. 6.3.10.

2\. May Fire purify us by the purifying things so that we may go forward to fulfil the hope of the promise (of the debts). Not knowing, begging the Fire may I destroy wherever is the sin.

3\. Which are the two stars in the sky named Vicṛt; may they give here the nectar which is the releaser of the bound ones.

4\. Move freely, obtain the (good) worlds; make yourself, (who is a) bound one, free from the binding; like the being of the womb, (which has) come out of the orifice of the womb enjoy all the ways (of life).

5\. May that Prajāpati, the knower (of our prayers), the first born of the Ṛta (settled order) accept (the offerings) given by us so that we may move well after the age with the line (of the progeny, which is) not cut (1).

6\. Who paid the debt of the fathers according to the proper rules, they follow the spreading line (of the progeny); and for the others is also the heaven, who have no progeny, (but) they are able to pay their debts to the givers.

7\. O Wife and Husband, begin, follow well and protect the uniform way (by sacrificing) with ghee. What is your given offering (to the fathers) and (what is your) oblation (sacrificed on the fire); for the sake of that alike to your Gotra (lineage) begin (the sacrifice).

[[P137]]

8\. What (sin have I made as) violating the mid-region, the earth, the heaven, the mother or the father; from that sin may the Household Fire take me up; and also from those which misdeeds I have committed (may the Household Fire take me up).

9\. The earth is the mother; Aditi, like the mother; the mid-region, the brother; the sin, the enemy; the heaven is our father, (O sacrificer), become fatherly, you will obtain the weal; you will not obtain the (heavenly) worlds dying without offspring.

10\. Where, in the heaven the good-hearted ones, the good workers become delighted leaving away the disease of the body, with the limbs that are not lame and (being) not wicked; may we see (my) father and (my) son there.

11\. What food we eat by the wrong way, O gods, giving, not giving or not doing, whatever there is the sin according to the views of the gods, whatever have we taken (from a lower one); may Fire set us free from that debt.

12\. Whatever bad food we eat, the clothes, the gold or the cow, the goat and sheep we use, whatever there is the sin according to the views of the gods, whatever have we taken (from a lower one); may Fire set us free from that debt.

13\. Whatever sin has been done by me by the mind, by the speech whenever; from that all set us free (O Fire) being worshipped by me; for you know everything in its real sense.

[[P138]]

### VII

1\. Vātaraśanas were the seers, the hard-working ones, who kept their semen up. The (other) seers came to them for their purpose. Vātaraśanas hided themselves; they entered the Kūṣmāṇḍa Mantras (i.e. the Mantras of the Anuvākas 3–6). The seers found them in those (Kūṣmāṇḍa Mantras) by faithfulness and austerity.

2\. The seers asked them, "why do you hide yourselves?" They replied the seers, "Salutation O prosperous ones, how may I serve you in this house?" The seers said to them, "Tell us the purifying (Mantras), by which we may become sinless." They saw these hymns (of the Kūṣmāṇḍa Mantras).

3\. By these (Mantras of the Anuvākas beginning with the words) "O gods, whatever anger to the gods", "whatever debt we have found, being unable", "May this fire put your life all round" you should pour ghee as oblation; by this, "we inform the Fire (Vaiśvānara)" you should present yourself. Whatever is the new sin of killing the embryo, you will become released from that.

4\. They sacrificed by these; they became sinless.

5\. In the performances (one) should sacrifice by these; he, becoming purified, enjoys the heavenly world.

### VIII

> *Note:* Origin of the Kusmanda Homa and the use of those Mantras. (From 1 to 5). Consecration of the Kusm and a Homa. (From 1 to 10).

1\. He should sacrifice by the Kusmanda Mantras, who thinks himself as impious.[^P139_1]

[^P139_1]: Anuvakas 3 to 6.

[[P139]]

2\. He, who sprinkles the semen in the improper vagina, becomes sinful as a thief, as a killer of the embryo.

3\. What is the new sin of killing of the embryo, he is released from that.

4\. According to the quantity of the sin (one) performs the preparation of that religious ceremony; after that he sacrifices without break.

5\. (One) becomes consecrated in a year; he purifies himself by the year.

6\. (One) becomes consecrated in a month; what is a month, that is the year (because the year is the collection of the months); (hence he) purifies himself by the year. (One) becomes consecrated in twenty-four nights; for there are twenty-four half months in a year; (hence he) purifies himself by the year. (One) becomes consecrated in twelve nights; for there are twelve months in a year; (hence he) purifies himself by the year. (One) becomes consecrated in six nights; for there are six seasons in a year, (hence he) purifies himself by the year. (One) becomes consecrated in three nights; for there are three feet in the Gayatri (Mantra); (hence he) purifies himself by the Gayatri (Mantra).

7\. (One) should not eat the meat; should not go to the woman, should not sit upon (anything other than the earth) and should hate the false.

8\. (Taking of) the milk is the rule for the Brahmana (caste); the rice-gruel, for the King (Ksatriya caste); the curd, for the Valaya (caste).

[[P140]]

9\. This rule is also for the Soma-sacrifice.

10\. If he thinks that he will become pale (by these rules) he may take in their place boiled rice, fried rice (or barley) and coarsely ground meal (Saktu) for not making himself pale (1).

### IX

> *Note:* Study of the Vedas. (From 1 to 2).

1\. Brahma, (who is) born himself, went to the white performers of the austerity, (who were) not born like ourselves. They became the Rsis (seers); that is the Rsitva (the etymological sense) of the Rsis. They worshipped that god. Desirous of the sacrifice, they saw this Brahmayajna (sacrifice in the form of the study); they took this and sacrificed by that.

2\. What Rces they read, they became the oblations of the milk for the gods; what Yajuses, (they read, they became) the oblations of ghee; what Samans (they read, they became) the oblations of the soma-juice; what Atharvangiras (-Mantras they read, they became) the oblations of the honey; what Brahmanas, Itihasas (histories), Puranas, Kalpas, Gathas, Narasamsins (praises of the heroes) (they read, they became) the oblations of the fat for the gods. By that they destroyed sinful hunger. The sinless gods came to the heavenly worlds, and the seers obtained the intimate union with the Brahman (1).

[[P141]]

### X

> *Note:* The five great sacrifices.

1\. These five great sacrifices are spread always, viz. the sacrifice of the gods, the sacrifice of the fathers, the sacrifice of the creatures, the sacrifice of the men, the sacrifice of the Brahman. Thus.[^P142_1]

[^P142_1]: The names of the sacrifices.

2\. What, even a fuel, he puts in the fire, that is finished the sacrifice of the gods.[^P142_r2_6]

[^P142_r2_6]: *(Mantras 2–6)* Description of each sacrifice.

3\. What he makes the svadha even by the waters, that is finished the sacrifice of the fathers.

4\. What he takes as oblation for the creatures, that is finished the sacrifice of the creatures.

5\. What food he gives to the Brahmanas, that is finished the sacrifice of the men.

6\. Whatever he reads either a Rc or a Yajus or a Saman, that is finished the sacrifice of the Brahman.

7\. When he reads the Rces, the streams of the milk carry svadha for the fathers; when he reads the Yajuses, the streams of the ghee (carry svadha for the fathers); when he reads the Samans, the soma-juice flows for them; when he reads the Atharvangiras (-Mantras), the streams of the honey flow for them; when he reads the Brahmanas, Itihasas (histories), Puranas, Kalpas, Gathas, Narasamsins (the praises of the heroes), the streams of the fat carry the svadha for the fathers.[^P142_r7_8]

[^P142_r7_8]: *(Mantras 7–8)* The study pleases the fathers and the gods.

[[P142]]

8\. He makes the gods satisfied by the oblations of the milk, when he reads the Rces; by the oblations of ghee, when he reads the Yajuses; by the oblations of Soma, when he reads the Samans; by the oblation of the honey, when he reads the Atharvangiras (-Mantras); by the oblations of the fat, when he reads the Brahmanas, Itihasas (histories), Puranas, Kalpas, Gathas, Narasamsins (the praises of the heroes); (yea) he makes the gods satisfied. They, being satisfied, make him (the sacrificer) satisfied with the shining, brilliance, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge and food etc. (1).

### XI

> *Note:* The description of the sacrifice of the Brahman (sacred study) with the quotation and the Brahmana-passage. (From 1 to 8).

1\. Who is to sacrifice by the sacrifice of the Brahman, (he should go) out of the village in the east, or in the midquarter between the east and the north or in the north so far that the roofs etc. of the village become not observable. After the rising of the sun wearing (the sacred thread, i.e. Yajnopavita) in the right side he should, after sitting (and) washing the hands, drink thrice (the water). Then he should touch the water twice by the hands. After washing (the lips) once he should touch the head, the eyes, the ears and the heart.

2\. When he drinks thrice he pleases the Rces by that; when he touches the water twice, he pleases the Yajuses by that; when he washes (the lips) once, he pleases the Samans by that; when he washes the left hand, feet and touches the head, the eyes, the ears and the heart, he pleases the Atharvangiras (-Mantras), Brahmanas, Itihasas, Puranas, Kalpas, Gathas and Narasamsins by that.

[[P143]]

3\. After spreading the Darbha-grass for sitting, he should sit on that by the Upastha Asana (putting the right foot upon the left foot) with his face to the east; (now he) should read. The Darbha-grass is, verily, the elixir of the waters and the plants, (hence) he makes the Brahman full of elixir.

4\. (He should utter) Om after putting his hands upon the feet (left hand upon the right foot and the right hand upon the left hand) and putting the Pavitras (Darbha-grass) in the hands. Yajus (Om) is the representative of knowledge of the three Vedas; this is the speech, this is the Great one, not to be destroyed.

5\. As to that it has been said by this Rc.

6\. The Rces are in the great protecting Aksara (not destroyable) Om, in which all the gods sit; who does not know that, what will he do by the Rces? Who, verily, know that they are sitting here.

7\. (One) uses only the three Vedas; he says Bhur, Bhuvah, Svar; this is the truth of the speech; which is the truth of the speech, he uses that.

8\. He should read thrice the Gayatri Mantra of Savitr-god, at first (stopping) on the words, then (stopping) on the half-Rces and then in a breath (not stopping anywhere in the middle). The god Savitr is the impeller of the prosperity; he obtains, indeed, the prosperity. He should begin the verses (on the next day) after the read words (1).

[[P144]]

### XII

1\. (One) should read his lesson silently in a village.

2\. Ahneys, the son of Suci has said thus.

3\. And in the forest, if he is unable (to read properly) he must read the lesson by uttering, standing, moving, sitting or laying; knowing thus, who reads the lesson, he becomes a pious ascetic.

4\. Salutation to Brahman, may salutation be for the fire, salutation to the earth, salutation to the plants, salutation to the Vac, salutation to the Vacaspati; I make salutation to Visnu, the great one (1).

### XIII

> *Note:* Rules for reading in the village and the forest and the salutations. (From 1 to 5). Other particulars of Brahma-Yajna, with quotation and Brahmana-passage.

1\. (One) should read powerfully at the mid-day; that Brahmana is, verily, the sun (Aditya); hence the sun has much heat at that time. As to that this has been said:—

[[P145]]

2\. Wonderful army of the divine ones, the eye of Mitra, Varuna and Agni has risen up. He fulfils the heaven, the earth and Surya (sun) is the soul of all the moving and the fixed ones.

3\. This sacrifice (Brahma-Yajna) is always spread freshly and finished freshly; at the evening is the finishing of that sacrifice (Brahma-Yajna), begun in the morning.

4\. He utters the concluding Mantra "Salutation to Brahman..." thrice.

5\. After touching the waters he comes to his home; whatever he gives there, that is the Daksina (sacrificial fee) (1).

### XIV

> *Note:* Study in the rain etc. and its fruit. (From 1 to 3).

1\. Of this sacrifice the cloud is the oblation oracle; the lightning, the fire; the rain, the oblation; the roaring of the cloud, the Vasatkara; the roaring of the lightning, the following Vasatkara; the wind, the soul; and the Amavasya, the offering of the right sacrifice.

[[P146]]

2\. He, who knowing this, reads his lesson in the rain, in the flash of the lightning, in the roaring of the cloud, in the roaring of the lightning, in the wind and in the Amavasya, that is performed, verily, an austerity; for the reading of one's lesson is, verily, an austerity.

3\. (He) ascends the supreme heaven, becomes supreme among his equals; (he) obtains that great and not returning world which a person obtains after donating this earth full of the wealth; he wins the death, which comes again and again; he obtains the intimate relation with the Brahman (1).

### XV

> *Note:* Intermissions of study and the details and importance of the Brahma-Yajna. (From 1 to 9).

1\. There are, verily, two intermissions of study when he is not pure and when the place is not pure.

2\. (In this sacrifice) the gods are the materials (of the sacrifice).

3\. Who, knowing thus, reads his lesson according to his power, in the midnight, at the time of the dawn, moving, standing, sitting, laying, in the forest or in the village: he wins all the worlds, he moves in all the worlds debtless. As to that this has been said:—

4\. May we be debtless in this, debtless in that, debtless in the third world. Whatever are the ways to the gods or to the fathers (Devayanas and Pitryanas) may we, the debtless, obtain them.

5\. The sin caught the born fire; the gods destroyed that sin by the oblations; they destroyed the sin of the oblations by the sacrifice; of the sacrifice, by the Daksina (sacrificial fee); of the Daksina, by the Brahmana; of the Brahmana, by the Mantras; of the Mantras, by study of one's own lesson; the study of one's own lesson is, verily, sinless, divinely pure; who leaves his study, he becomes shareless in the speech, shareless in the heaven; as to that this has been said:—

[[P147]]

6\. He, who abandoned his friend (the study), who knows friendship, has not a part even in the speech, whatever he hears, hears in vain; he does not know that path of the good deed.

7\. Therefore, one should read his lesson; whatever sacrifice he reads, by that all it is sacrificed for him; he obtains the intimate relation with Fire, Wind, and Sun; as to that this has been said:—

8\. Who abuse the new or old readers (learned ones), the knowers of the Vedas; they all abuse the sun, the fire, the second and the swan (Hamsa = Atman), the third.

9\. Whatever gods there are, they all live in the Brahmana, who knows the Vedas. Therefore, (one) should salute daily the Brahmanas, who know the Vedas; should not use bad words for them; (by this way) he pleases these gods (living in the Brahmanas) (1).

### XVI

1\. He, who causes (ones) sacrifice and who takes (sacrificial fee) from them, becomes empty in this and in that world. After causing one sacrifice and taking (fee) from him, he should read thrice his lesson of the Veda without taking the meal.

2\. Or he should read for three nights the Gayatri Mantra of the god Savitr; in this way he makes himself not empty.

3\. The cow (vara) is the Daksina (sacrificial fee); he pleases vara (Atman) by the vara (cow); for the Atman is the vara (1).

### XVII

> *Note:* Atonement by study. (From 1 to 3).

1\. He milks out the Mantras, who causes (one) sacrifice. By which Mantras of the sacrifice he causes (one) sacrifice, he should read that lesson going in the forest, sitting in a pure place.

[[P148]]

2\. Not eating is his consecration; the place, the Upasada (going near); the seat, the pressing of the Soma; the speech, the oblation; the mind, the Upabhrt (the sacrificial ladle); the resolution, the Dhruva (the sacrificial ladle); the breath (Prana), the sacrificing; the Saman, the Adhvaryu priest. This sacrifice is of the Daksina (fee) of the breaths, the eternal Daksina, which is more prosperous (1).

### XVIII

> *Note:* Atonement for the violation of the vow of chastity. (From 1 to 8).

1\. In how many ways (one), who has violated his vow of chastity, enters (the gods)? The speakers of the sacred knowledge say: in four ways, by the breaths to Maruts, by the vigours to Indra, by the splendor of the sacred knowledge to Brhaspati and by the all others to Fire. Sudeva, the son of Kasyapa, has found out this atonement of that.

2\. Which student of the sacred knowledge violates the vow of chastity, he sacrifices two separate oblations of ghee after bringing and keeping the fire (by the Mantra) "O Kama (god of the desire and love etc.), I have violated the vow of chastity, O Kama, I have violated the vow of chastity; hail for Kama; O Kama, I have committed the offence, O Kama; hail for Kama.

Nectar is, verily, the ghee; he puts in himself the ghee, the nectar.

3\. After sacrificing, putting together his palms of the hands, he should utter these Mantras standing in the sideway of the fire:—

[[P149]]

4\. May Maruts make me full again, may Indra make me full again, may Brhaspati make me full again, may this fire make me full again by life and vigour; may they make me full of life.

5\. Maruts put breaths (Pranas) in him, Indra puts vigour in him, Brhaspati puts the splendor of the sacred knowledge in him, the fire puts all others in him, becoming full in all the limbs of the body he obtains the whole (span of) life.

6\. He should utter the Mantras thrice, for the gods are thrice (fully) true.

7\. He, who thinks himself impious, should sacrifice thus, utter the Mantra thus; he makes (in this way) purified himself; he puts the life in himself.

8\. The cow (vara) is the Daksina (sacrificial fee); he pleases the vara (Atman) by vara; for Atman is the vara.

### XIX

> *Note:* Salutations and the description of a divine dolphin. (From 1 to 5).

1\. I obtain Bhuh (the earth), I obtain the Bhuvah (the mid-region), I obtain the Svar (the heaven), I obtain the Brahman, I obtain the sheath of the Brahman, I obtain the nectar, I obtain the sheath of the nectar, which the death does not see, I obtain that, I obtain the gods, I obtain the town of the gods by that Brahma, by that armour being covered and recovered by the brilliance of Kasyapa.

[[P150]]

2\. To whom we salute, he is the head; the religion, the skull; the Brahman, the upper jaw; the sacrifice, the lower jaw; Visnu, the heart; the Year, the generative organ; Asvins, the back feet; Atri, the middle body; Mitra and Varuna, the front feet; the fire, the first part of the tail: then Indra, the second, then Prajapati, the third and the not fearing one, the fourth part of the tail (these are the limbs of the divine dolphin).

3\. Such is that powerful divine dolphin; to that:-

4\. Who knows thus, he wins the death which comes again and again; he wins the heavenly world; he does not die in the way; he does not die in the fire; he does not die in the waters; he does not die without offsprings; he obtains the food easily.

5\. You are firm (polar star), you are the residing place of the firm (worlds), you are lord of the beings, you are supreme among the beings; the beings serve you from all sides; salutation to you, salutation, all salutation to you, salutation to Sisukumara (dolphin) (1).

### XX

> *Note:* Salutations. (From 1 to 3).

1\. Salutation to the eastern quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter.

2\. Salutation to the southern quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the western quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the northern quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the aboveward quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the belowward quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the intermediate quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter.

[[P151]]

3\. Salutation to those, who live between the Ganga and the Yamuna; may they, pleasant-minded ones increase our life; salutation to the seers, who reside between the Ganga and the Yamuna; salutation, salutation to the seers, who reside between the Ganga and the Yamuna (1).

Salutation to Brahman, may salutation be to Fire, salutation to earth; salutation to the plants, salutation to Vāc (speech), salutation to Vacaspati (lord of speech), I make salutation to great Visnu.

Om Peace!

Peace!

Peace!

[[P152]]

## III

[[P153]]

### I

> *Note:* Mantras for peace and propitiation.

Harih Om.

I pray for that happiness and welfare for the progress of the sacrifice (and) for the progress of the sacrificer. May the divine welfare be for us, (so that) no sufferings take place. May propitiation be for our two-footed (men); may propitiation be for the four-footed (cattle). Further, may the medicine (remover of all) take place.

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

1\. Om. The thinking is the Sruc (large wooden ladle); the heart, the ghee; the Vac (speech), the altar; the uttering, the Barhi-grass; the Hotṛ, the fire; knowledge, (also) the fire; the lord of speech (Vacaspati), the invoker; the mind, the Upavakṭr (the impeller); the breath, the oblation; the Samana, the Adhvaryu priest.

2\. O Vacaspati (lord of speech), O disposer, making others bend (to salute), we spread your fame; spread our fame; may Vacaspati drink Soma and may he put vigour in us; hail! (1)

### II

> *Note:* Mantras of the Caturhotra Citi. Identification of the priests with the earth etc.

1\. The earth is the Hotṛ priest; the heaven, the Adhvaryu priest; Rudra, verily, the fire; Bṛhaspati, the impeller.[^P153_r1_2]

[^P153_r1_2]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Typifications of the utensils and prayers. Identification of the priests with the earth etc.

2\. O Vacaspati, you are worshipped by the full power of the speech; grant the heaven, which is worth choosing, for the sacrificer; Vacaspati drinks the Soma-juice; may he generate Indra for the sake of the power of the senses (1).

[[P154]]

### III

1\. The fire is the Hotṛ priest; Aśvins, the Adhvaryu priest; Tvaṣṭr, verily, the fire; Mitra, the impeller.

2\. Soma is the first going one of the Soma-plant; the white Soma-juice is the first going one of the white (shining Mantras). Indra, Soma-juices have been prepared for you, who are of the speed of the wind and the hearer of the invokings; hail! (1)

### IV

1\. The sun is your eye; the wind, the breath; the heaven, the back; the mid-region, the soul; (obtain) the sacrifice by the limbs; the earth, by the bodies.

2\. O Vacaspati, by the full speech, by the unbroken ladle impel the sacrifice which increases the gods, in the heaven (1).

### V

> *Note:* Mantras describing the seven seers identified with the priests etc.

1\. (The seer) Mahāhavi is the Hotṛ priest; Satyasavi, the Adhvaryu priest; Acyutatejas, verily, the fire; Acyutamanas, the impeller; Anadhravis and Apratadhravis, the Abhigar priests (who call out approvingly to the other priests); Acyutasya, the Udgātṛ priest.[^P154_r1_2v]

[^P154_r1_2v]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Identification of the priests with the earth etc. and the seven seers.

[[P155]]

2\. O Vacaspati, O disposer of the heart, making others bend (to salute), I spread your fame; spread our fame. Vacaspati drank the Soma-juice. Do not cut the divine line, do not cut the manly line. Salutation to the heaven, salutation to the earth; hail! (1)

### VI

1\. The speech is the Hotṛ priest; the consecration, the wife; the wind, the Adhvaryu priest; the waters, the Abhigar priest; the mind, the oblation; I sacrifice in the austerity.

2\. Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ. Brahman is self-born, salutation to the Brahman, the self-born (1).

### VII

> *Note:* Bodily functions identified with the priests, etc. and prayer. Ten Mantras to be uttered after the Cāturmāsya-Yāga. (From 1 to 10).

1\. Brahman is the sacrificer of one (sacrificing by his maturity only): he is the sacrifice; may he grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may he become my sacrifice.[^P155_r1_2]

[^P155_r1_2]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Prayer and bodily functions identified with the priests, etc.

[[P156]]

2\. The fire is the sacrificer of the two (being useful in cooking and carrying the oblations); that is the nourisher; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become my nourisher.

3\. The earth is the sacrificer of three (being the place of the fire, food and sacrifice); that is the firm-standing (1); may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become my firm-standing.

4\. The mid-region is the sacrificer of the four (being the causer of speech, rain, space and place); that is the disposer in various ways; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become disposer in various ways for me.

5\. The wind is the sacrificer of five (five breaths); that is the breath; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become my breath (2).

6\. The moon is the sacrificer of six (i.e., the seasons); that causes the seasons; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may the seasons be for me.

7\. The food is the sacrificer of seven (seven holes of the head); that is the breath of the breath; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become the breath of my breath.

8\. The heaven is the sacrificer of eight (eight suns); that is one not to be injured (3); may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may I become one not to be injured.

9\. Aditya is the sacrificer of the nine (eight suns and Aditya); that is brilliant; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may I become brilliant.

[[P157]]

10\. Prajāpati is the sacrificer of ten (nine holes of the body and the navel), he is this all; may he grant me offspring, cattle, fame, and may all be mine (4).

### VIII

1\. The Fire (becomes satisfied) by the Yajuses; Savitṛ (sun), by the hymns; Indra, by the praises; Mitra and Varuṇa, by the prayers; Āṅgirases, by the Dhāniyāte (of side-altar) fires; Maruts, by the seat of the oblation-receptacles; the waters, by the prokṣaṇīs (vessels for putting the holy water); the plants, by the Barhi-grass; Aditi, by the altar; Soma, by the consecration (1); Tvaṣṭṛ, by the fuel; Viṣṇu, by the sacrifice; Vasus, by the ghee; Ādityas, by the Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee); All-gods (Viśvedevān), by the power; Pūṣan, by giving (their) own (oblations to the gods); Bṛhaspati, by the priest; Prajāpati, by the Udgītha (singing Samans in particular modes); the mid-region, by the Pavitra (fringed filtering cloth); the wind, by the pots; and I, by the faithfulness (2).

### IX

> *Note:* The Śambhāra Yajus Mantras. The wives of the gods.

1\. Senā (army) is (the wife) of Indra; Dhenu (speech), of Bṛhaspati; Pathyā (salutary one), of Pūṣan; Vāk (speech), of the wind; Dīkṣā (consecration), of Soma; the earth, of the fire; Gāyatrī (-Mantra), of Vasus; Triṣṭup (-Mantra), of Rudras; Jagatī (-Mantra), of the Ādityas; Anuṣṭup (-Mantra), of Viṣṇu (1); Virāṭ (-Mantra), of Varuṇa; Paṅkti (-Mantra), of the sacrifice; Anumati (assent), of Prajāpati; Śraddhā (faithfulness), of Mitra; Prasūti (Impelling), of Savitṛ; Marīci (ray), of Sūrya (sun); Rohiṇī (-constellation), of Candramas (moon); Arundhatī (-constellation), of the seers (Ṛṣis); the lightning, of Parjanya; the four quarters, the four sub-quarters, the day and the night, the agriculture and the rain, the light and the compensation, the waters and the plants; the vigour and the joy—these are the wives of the gods (2).

[[P158]]

### X

> *Note:* Mantras for taking Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee).

1\. O Goddess Dakṣiṇā (Sacrificial Fee), I take you by the impelling of the impeller Savitṛ, by the hands of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan; may Varuṇa, the king carry you to the fire as gold; by that may we obtain immortality; may this (Dakṣiṇā) be food for the donator and comfort for me, the obtainer. Kāma (desire or cupid) gave it for Kāma; Kāma is the donator (1); Kāma is the obtainer. (O Dakṣiṇā) enter Kāma; I take you by Kāma (desire); O Kāma, this is yours; O Kāma, this is your Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee). (O Dakṣiṇā), may Uttāna Āṅgirasa obtain you.

2\. O goddess Dakṣiṇā, I take you by the impelling of Savitṛ, by the hands of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan; may Varuṇa, the king carry you for Soma as clothes, for Rudra as cow, for Varuṇa as horse, for Prajāpati as Puruṣa (person) (2), for Manu as bed, for Tvaṣṭr as goat, for Pūṣan as sheep, for poverty as mule and ass, for the Himavant as elephant, for Gandharvas and Apsarases as garland and ornaments, for all gods as food, for speech as corn, for Brahman as boiled rice, for the ocean as waters (3), for Uttāna Āṅgirasa as carriage, for Vaiśvānara (fire) as chariot.

[[P159]]

3\. Vaiśvānara ascended in the heaven as ancient one; that upper place of the heaven is welfare-doing one by the good hymns; that Vaiśvānara, producing wealth for the beings moves all round uniformly as a career, an awaking one.

4\. O Dakṣiṇā (Sacrificial Fee), may the King Varuṇa carry you as the chariot for Vaiśvānara; by that may we obtain the immortality; may food be for the donator and comfort for me, the obtainer (4). Who gave it whom? Kāma (desire or cupid) gave it for Kāma; Kāma is the donator, Kāma is the obtainer; (O Dakṣiṇā) enter Kāma; I take you by Kāma (desire); O Kāma, this is yours; O Kāma, this is your Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee); may Uttāna Āṅgirasa obtain you (5).[^P159_r4]

[^P159_r4]: *(Mantra 4)* Mantras for taking Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee).

### XI

> *Note:* Hotṛ-hṛdaya Mantras.

1\. I know well the soul (Ātman) of Indra, which is (like) the gold, shining and longing, moving in ten ways; moving in the ocean (waters) by his mind (to create the worlds). Brahmā knew that in the ocean. He is the ruler of the men, entered their hearts; He is one going on in various forms. Where a hundred lights become one, where all the Vedas become one, where all the Hotṛs become one; that is the Self (Ātman) of the men living in their minds (1), the ruler of the men, entering their hearts. Where all born ones become one, where the four Hotṛs (priests) obtain the prosperity, that is the Self (Ātman) of the men, living in their minds.[^P160_r1]

[^P160_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Daśa-Hotṛ Mantras (referring to the Mantras of ten Hotṛs of the Anuvāka VII).

[[P160]]

2\. Brahmā knew by the austerity that (Ātman as) Indra, the firm-standing of the worlds, the soul of the heaven, Savitṛ and Bṛhaspati, the four Hotṛs pervading the quarters, the vigour of the speech.[^P160_r2_4]

[^P160_r2_4]: *(Mantras 2–4)* Catur-Hotṛ Mantras. See T.A. 3.7.2.

3\. The poets (sages) knew decidedly that Ātman, entered the inner heart, (in the form of) Tvaṣṭr, spreading the forms, the knowing one (2), the breath of the immortality, this sacrifice, and the soul of four Hotṛs (priests).

4\. The poets (sages) knew decidedly this Ātman, entered the inner heart, the brother of the gods, put in the minds and this sacrifice, performed by the immortality.

5\. This Ātman of Indra, the sacrificer of the five, living everywhere, worth choosing by all (in the form of the wind) knows all, hundred millions; he pervades all the creation; he is the nectar of the gods and the life of the men.[^P160_r5_7]

[^P160_r5_7]: *(Mantras 5–7)* Pañca-Hotṛ Mantras.

6\. The poets (sages) decidedly knew this Indra, the king, Savitṛ, the Self (Ātman) of the mind; the poets protect (see) always in the foot of the Ṛta (Settled Order) (that Ātman as) the ray, being heated in the rays.

[[P162]]

7\. Who holds the world in the sheath of the egg of the creation (Aṇḍakośa) having no difference with that; after that (breaking the sheath) who sees the worlds, whose power has been called this sheath of the egg of the creation; and whose membrane has been called breath; I have been formed by him, by that Immortality.

8\. (The learned ones) describe that Ātman as gold (moon),[^P162_1] sheath, covered by the regions, the treasure of the gods, shining well (4), a part of the sacrificer of the six (of the moon) full of the nectar; the (others) do not know that.[^P162_r8_10]

[^P162_1]: See T.A. 3.7.3.

[^P162_r8_10]: *(Mantras 8–10)* Ṣaḍ-Hotṛ Mantras.

9\. By whom the seasons have been made five-fold or even six-fold by the mind; the sages see that sacrificer of the six, causing the seasons in the foot of the Ṛta (Settled Order).

10\. The gods, though living with this, do not know this acting Ātman, entered the inner heart, moving in the moon by the mind, Self (Ātman) of Indra, moving in hundred ways (5).

11\. Indra, the king, who rules the world, is the sacrificer of the seven, caused seven-fold.[^P162_2][^P162_r11_34]

[^P162_2]: See T.A. 3.7.3.

[^P162_r11_34]: *(Mantras 11–34)* Sapta-Hotṛ Mantras.

12\. Brahmā knew the heart of the gods, being its line sprinkled by the Supreme One, moving in Āditya by the mind.

13\. Brahmā took up this (sacrificer of the seven) from Brahman as the sun, raining, in the midst of the waters (of the clouds).

14\. In whom (Āditya) seven protectors (rays) rain the abundant prosperity: O Indra (give us that prosperity) full of many horses and cows (6).

[[P163]]

15\. That Hari, the best obtainer of the wealth and the protector sprinkles that prosperity for Indra which is not destroyable, full of abundance.

16\. May Indra give us that prosperity which is abundant, not destroyable, full of many horses and cows.

17\. There are ten thousands of his (Āditya's) rays; may Indra (Āditya) as ray come to us being increased by our prayers; may he, the ray, Savitṛ, Indra grant us much wealth (7).

18\. May this fire put in me ghee, brilliance, honeyful sense-organ.

19\. Who moves in the sky as Hari, Pataga (sun), Patari (full of shining folds), Suparṇa (divine bird), dweller of the heaven; may that Indra (Āditya = sun) grant us the desired boon.

20\. There moves the great wheel (year) of five fellies (seasons); that golden light (Āditya) goes creeping slowly in the sky, in the middle of the waters of the clouds, as great light; may that Indra (Āditya) grant me the desired boon.

21\. Seven (rays) move the chariot of one (wheel) (8); (in reality) one horse, having seven names, carries that wheel of three navels (three main seasons, i.e., cool, hot and rainy), which is not destroyable and sinless and by which all these worlds live.

22\. The seers, the knowers of the heaven, seeing this auspicious (Āditya) performed austerity and consecration; then there was born the Kṣatriya (caste), power and vigour; may the gods give this sacrificer (that all).

23\. They decidedly knew this (Āditya) in the highest heaven as the white ray, being eaten by the beings again and again; as the carrier of the waters; as the protector and as Indra (ruler) (9).

[[P164]]

24\. The red and the yellow waters (become) one (-coloured yellow in the clouds); these yellow waters of one form falling (on the earth) become ten thousands and lakhs and of the needs of the boats.

25\. Which is this white Ray, that is all this world; may that give us offspring, cattle and wealth.

26\. The White Ray has become all; may that impel for me various cattle.

27\. By the power of the Almighty the seers see the clear sun[^P164_1] by their heart; the other poets see them in the midst of the ocean (sky); the wise ones wish the foot of the rays.

[^P164_1]: See T.A. 3.7.3.

28\. The sun (Āditya) holds the speech by the mind; the wind utters that in the body; the sages protect that shining, heaven-giving speech in the foot of the heaven.

29\. Whatever are the cattle of the village having many forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the fire release them for us first (11); and may Prajāpati, concordant by his progeny, (grant them for us).

30\. (O Agni and Prajāpati), grant producing power in our all cattle and increase this sacrificer.

31\. Whatever are the cattle of the village having all forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the play of them all seven be here for wealth, nourishing and good vitality.

32\. Whatever are the cattle of the forest having many forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the wind release them for us first; and may Prajāpati, concordant by his progeny, (grant them for us).

[[P165]]

33\. The gods knew the fixed and the moving world born of the food like the ghee put in the cave (of their heart).

34\. Whatever are the cattle of the forest, having many forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the play of them all seven be here for wealth, nourishing and good vitality (12).

### XII

> *Note:* The Puruṣa Sūkta. (From 1 to 18).

1\. Hundred-headed Puruṣa (Person) having hundred eyes, hundred feet, after covering all the worlds stood larger (than these) by ten fingers.

2\. That all is Puruṣa (only), whatever was (before) or whatever will be (further); He is the Lord of the immortality (gods) as well as of those, who are born (living on) by the food.

3\. This is His greatness; Puruṣa (Person) Himself is greater than this (1); all the beings are His (one) foot; His other three feet are in the heaven (in the form of) nectar.

4\. Puruṣa of three feet rises above; His feet is also here again; then He pervaded various ones who eat or who do not eat (as mountains etc.).

5\. Of Him Virāṭ was born; of Virāṭ a person was born; after being born he became (various) other than Virāṭ; after him the earth and the bodies were born (2).

6\. When the gods performed the sacrifice by the oblation of the person, then Vasant (spring season) was its ghee, Grīṣma (summer) was fuel and Śarad (autumn) was the oblation.

[[P166]]

7\. Seven were the Paridhis (enclosures) of this and twenty-one were the fuels, when the gods bound the person as cattle (for sacrificing).

8\. They sprinkled in Barhi-grass (by mind) that person, born before (the other beings) (3); by him the gods, the sages and the seers sacrificed.

9\. From that sacrifice, where all (in the form of the person) was sacrificed came forth the wonderful ghee and that produced those cattle who are related to the wind, who are wild and who are of the village (tame).

10\. From that sacrifice, where all (in the form of the person) was sacrificed, came forth the Ṛcas, the Sāmans, the verses came forth from that, the Yajus was produced from that (4).

11\. From that the horses were produced and also those, who have teeth in the sides of the mouth (as asses etc.) were born; the cows came forth from that; the goats and the sheep were born of him.

12\. In how many ways they caused the person, when they caused the person? What is called his mouth? What are the hands? What are the thighs? And what are called the legs?

13\. Brāhmaṇa (caste) was his mouth; Kṣatriya was his hands (5); Vaiśya was his thighs and Śūdra was born of the legs.

14\. The moon was born of (his) mind; the sun was born of the eye, Indra and the fire were born of the mouth and the wind was born of the breath.

15\. From the navel the mid-region was born; from the head moved round the heaven; from the legs the earth and from the ear the quarters came forth; in that way they caused all the world (6).

[[P168]]

16\. I know this Great Puruṣa, who has the colour of the sun, who is out of the darkness; He, the wise one, is merciful on the world after deciding all the forms and giving the names to them.

17\. Whom the disposer made famous, whom Indra, knowing all the quarters, made famous; by knowing Him one becomes immortal here; there is no other way for going to the (Supreme).

18\. The gods performed the sacrifice by the sacrifice (sacrificial person); these were the first holders (Dharma) of the creation; those great ones obtain the heaven where there are the ancient ages and the gods (7).

### XIII

> *Note:* Concluding portion of the Puruṣa-Sūkta. (From 1 to 5).

1\. He was born of the waters and the elixirs of the earth; he moved round in the rule of Viśvakarman (causer of the world); Tvaṣṭṛ makes the forms of him; I know that (greatness) of the Puruṣa (person) first.

2\. I know this great Puruṣa of the colour of the sun, out of the darkness; he, who knows him thus, becomes immortal here; there is no other way for reaching the (immortality).

3\. Prajāpati moves in the womb of the creation; he becomes born in various forms, (though really) being unborn (1). The wise ones know the cause of that; the sages wish the foot of the rays.

4\. Salutation to that shining Brahman, who shines for the sake of the gods, who is the priest of the gods and who was born before the gods.

[[P169]]

5\. The gods, while producing the lights of the Brahman (consciousness), said in the first time; "who knows thus of Brahman, the gods live according to his wish".

6\. (O Ātman:) the goddess of shyness and the goddess of prosperity are your wives; the days and the nights are the sides; the stars are the forms; Aśvins are the opened (mouth); grant the desired (thing); grant that (heaven); grant all (for me).

### XIV

> *Note:* Mantras to be used at the Upahoma after offering the oblations to Death-god. (From 1 to 10).

1\. The breath (Prāṇa) holds (the bodies) being held (by Brahman); that one god has entered variously (in the beings); when he becomes lazy, then he sets again leaving the burden of holding the body.

2\. (People) say him, indeed, mortal and also immortal, (they) say him holder, (they) say him protector; he holds (the body) being held (by Brahman), who knows this truly for keeping (in the heart).

3\. This (Prāṇa) leaves even a new-born one and does not leave even an old one (1); he leaves many in one day; he is an active god to be prayed always.

4\. Who knows from where this (breath) was born, and also who knows what relation this has with Brahman; in his old sleeping body this (breath) plays and does not leave in the gone days.

[[P170]]

5\. (O Prāṇa!), the waters follow you (i.e., they rain when the Prāṇa wind blows heavily) as the knowing cows follow the calves nourishing by the milk; you burn the fire, the carrier of the oblations; you are the holder Mātariśvan (wind) of the subjects (2).

6\. You are the sacrifice, you are, verily, Soma; all the gods come to your invoking. You are one, entered many beings. May salutation be for you; become one to be invoked easily.[^P169_r6]

[^P169_r6]: *(Mantra 6)* A Yajus Mantra.

7\. O Prāṇa (in-breath) and Apāna (out-breath), coming and going in the body, may salutation be to you both, hear my invoking: I invoke you by the praise; come soon; as young ones leave (kill) him, he hates me.

8\. O Prāṇa and Apāna, being concordant, leave (kill) him; do not go with his breath (3); O gods (Prāṇa and Apāna), being concordant by the praise, give him to us for killing, (may) I kill him.

9\. The 'non-being' (Asat) produced; the (Prāṇa breath) was born from that 'being'; whomever he (Prāṇa) produced, they all became his protector; when he becomes lazy in holding the burden, then leaving the burden he sets away again.

10\. Then you became as Prāṇa as the great enjoying of Prajāpati, when you caused to breathe well the nine gods (holes of the body).

### XV

> *Note:* The Mṛtyu-Sūkta to be uttered after Brahma-medha sacrifice. (From 1 to 6).

1\. The gods (senses) follow the carrying (death), who carries (the breaths), who is the lord of the world and the Bull among the wise ones; this Brahman (hymn) has come in my favour; do not destroy my way; make stride (for killing my enemies).

[[P171]]

2\. O Death, do not cut, do not kill; do not destroy my power, do not take away (anything) as thief; O strong one, do not kill my offspring and the span of my life; I serve you, the observer of the men, by the oblation.

3\. All the quarters hear well the fierce and trembling Death (1); again they produce by his desire.

4\. By the desire of the Death may my desire be fulfilled; by his heart may my heart (come to me); what is favourable of these senses, may that come to me.

5\. O Death, follow your other path, which is your other than the path of the gods; I say to you, who has eyes and ears, "Do not kill my offspring and brave ones".

6\. Saluting and praying, I worship the Death, born first of the Ṛta (Settled Order), who is the sprinkler of the beings and the one king of the progeny of the men (2).

### XVI

> *Note:* Mantras for Sūrya (sun).

1\. O Sūrya (sun), you are moving one, seer of the world and maker of the light; you cause the world shine splendourly; (O fluid of Soma etc.,); you have been taken in the Upāyama (sacrificial vessel); I (sacrifice) you for the shining sun; he (Sun) is your cause; I (sacrifice) you for the shining sun.

[[P172]]

### XVII

1\. O Soma, the exalted one, be satisfied by all protections; be increased for us.[^P172_xvii_r1]

[^P172_xvii_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Funeral Mantra to be uttered when sprinkling water on the excavation made for burning the dead one.

### XVIII

1\. Those men went to the Death, who saw the rising dawns in the old age, we have also to go to (Death) who are seeing (the dawns), and they will also go, who will see the dawns in future.[^P172_xviii_r1]

[^P172_xviii_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra for bathing after funeral.

### XIX

1\. (O Fuel), I put you as shining one (on the dead one), I put you as the maker of the light, I put you as the knower of the light, I put you as full of light, I put you as burning, I put you as being burnt with the 'Malmala' sounds of the flames, I put you as being shined, I put you as being lighted, I put you as many, I put you as of great light, I put you as awaking one, I put you as waking one.[^P172_xix_r1]

[^P172_xix_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantras addressing the fuel for a cremation.

[[P173]]

### XX

1\. Hail! for Prayāsa, Hail! for Āyāsa! Hail! for Viyāsa, Hail! for Saṃyāsa! Hail! for Udyāsa, Hail! for Avayāsa! Hail! for Śuce! Hail! for Śoka! Hail! for Tapyatva, Hail! for Tapat! Hail! for the (sin of) killing of the Brāhmaṇas, Hail! for all.[^P173_xx_r1]

[^P173_xx_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantras for offering oblations to the gods of the region of Yama (i.e., the gods of the region of Yama who inflict punishment).

### XXI

1\. (I sacrifice) for (the god) Citta (heart) by progeny; for Bhava by liver (Yakṛt), for Rudra by the flesh of the lower part, for Paśupati (lord of cattle) by the fat heart, for the fire by heart, for Rudra by the blood, for Śarva by the nerves, for Mahādeva (great god) by the inner sides, for Osiṣṭhahana by fleshes (named Śiṃśinikā, i.e., the parts of singing organs).[^P173_xxi_r1]

[^P173_xxi_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantras for offering oblations in the Rājagavīya Homa.

I pray for that happiness and welfare for the progress of the sacrifice and for the progress of the sacrificer. May divine welfare be for us. Further may the medicine (remover of all the sufferings) obtain place. May the propitiation be for our two-footed (men). May propitiation be for the four-footed (cattle).

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

[[P174]]

## IV

[[P175]]

### I

> *Section:* The Pravargya Sacrifice.

Hariḥ Oṃ.

1\. Salutation to the speech, which has been uttered, salutation to that speech which is yet unuttered; salutation to the speech, salutation to the lord of the speech (Vācaspati); salutation to the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras; may not the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras disobey me; may I not disobey the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras.[^P175_r1_7]

[^P175_r1_7]: *(Mantras 1–7)* Propitiatory Mantras. (From 1 to 7).

2\. May I speak the speech which is related to all the gods, auspicious, unbroken and dear to the gods.

3\. May the heaven be comfortable for me, may the earth be comfortable for me, may this all world be comfortable for me, may the moon and the sun be comfortable for me, may Brahman and Prajāpati be comfortable for me.

4\. I will speak the matter, the world, the light, the austerity, Brahman and the truth.

5\. I spread this bed (Pravargya) for that; may there be bed (shelter) for my offspring, cattle; may I become bed (shelter) for my cattle and offspring.

6\. May Prāṇa (in-breath) and Apāna (out-breath) protect me; may Prāṇa and Apāna do not leave me.

[[P176]]

7\. I will think the honey (Pravargya), I will produce the honey, I will speak the honey; may I speak the honeyful (sweet) speech for the gods, well-hearing for the men (fathers); may the gods protect me as such, may the fathers also please me for the sake of beauty.

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

### II

> *Section:* The construction of the Mahāvīra.

1\. The sacrificing priests of the learned, great and wise (sacrificer) concentrate their minds, concentrate their wisdom; Savitṛ, the knower of all the rules, alone, has created this all; great is the fame of the god Savitṛ.[^P176_r1]

[^P176_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Putting of the thorny fuel on the Pravargya-fire.

2\. I take you by the impelling of the god Savitṛ, by the arms of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan.[^P176_r2]

[^P176_r2]: *(Mantra 2)* Mantra for taking Abhri.

3\. You are Abhri (an instrument), a double-headed hoe of the wood of the Mimosa Catechu and woman, performer of the sacrifice for the gods.[^P176_r3]

[^P176_r3]: *(Mantra 3)* Mantra to be uttered over the Abhri.

4\. O Brahmaṇaspati, stand up (1), be increased; desirous of divine ones, pray you; may Maruts, well donators go near; O Indra, become swift-going one with us.[^P176_r4]

[^P176_r4]: *(Mantra 4)* Address to Brahmaṇaspati.

5\. May Brahmaṇaspati come well; may the goddess Sūnṛtā (true speech) come well; may the gods lead our sacrifice for obtaining the manly Vīra (Mahāvīra pot), the cooker of the five foods.[^P176_r5]

[^P176_r5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantra for carrying the skin of a black antelope, a goat, a horse and an ox to the place where the earth is dug.

[[P177]]

6\. May the goddesses heaven and earth assent me.[^P177_r6]

[^P177_r6]: *(Mantra 6)* Mantra for spreading the antelope-skin.

7\. May I prosper today, this is the head of the sacrifice (2).[^P177_r7]

[^P177_r7]: *(Mantra 7)* Mantra for striking the Abhri.

8\. (I take) you for the sacrifice, (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.[^P177_r8]

[^P177_r8]: *(Mantra 8)* Mantra for lifting the earth and putting that on the antelope-skin.

9\. You were this much before.[^P177_r9]

[^P177_r9]: *(Mantra 9)* Mantra for lifting the earth.

10\. May I prosper today, this is the head of the sacrifice; (I take) you for the sacrifice, (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.[^P177_r10]

[^P177_r10]: *(Mantra 10)* Mantra for bringing the earth.

11\. O divine white-ants, you are the first-born of this living one, observers of the Ṛta (Settled Order).[^P177_r11]

[^P177_r11]: *(Mantra 11)* Mantra for bringing the earth thrown up by white-ants.

12\. May I prosper today; this is the head of the sacrifice (3); (I take) you for the sacrifice; (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.[^P177_r12]

[^P177_r12]: *(Mantra 12)* Mantra for bringing that earth.

13\. You are the vigour of Indra.[^P177_r13_14]

[^P177_r13_14]: *(Mantras 13–14)* Mantra addressed to the Pūtīka tree (Caesalpinia Bonducella).

14\. May I prosper today; this is the head of the sacrifice; (I take) you for the sacrifice; (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.

15\. You are born of the fire, the semen of Prajāpati.[^P177_r15_16]

[^P177_r15_16]: *(Mantras 15–16)* Mantra to be uttered after spreading the hairs of a goat and a black antelope.

16\. May I prosper today; this is the head of the sacrifice (4); (I take) you for the sacrifice; (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.

[[P178]]

17\. Put the life, put the Prāṇa-breath, put the Apāna-breath, put the Vyāna-breath, put the eye, put the ears, put the mind, put the speech, put the self, put the firm-standing, put me, put in me.[^P178_r17]

[^P178_r17]: *(Mantra 17)* Mantra for making the horse smell the offering.

18\. May the honey make you honeyful.[^P178_r18]

[^P178_r18]: *(Mantra 18)* Mantra for sprinkling the hot water on the offering.

19\. You are the head of the sacrifice (5).[^P178_r19]

[^P178_r19]: *(Mantra 19)* Mantra for pounding earth and making balls.

20\. Be the feet of the sacrifice.[^P178_r20]

[^P178_r20]: *(Mantra 20)* Mantra for stamping the balls with the two thumbs.

21\. I perform you by the Gāyatrī-verse, I perform you by the Triṣṭubh-verse, I perform you by the Jagatī-verse.[^P178_r21_25]

[^P178_r21_25]: *(Mantras 21–25)* Mantras for making a figure of Mahāvīra (a sacrificial pot).

22\. You are the girdle of the sacrifice.

23\. May Aditi take your hole by the Paṅkti-verse.

24\. Be cooked by the heat of the sun.

25\. You are the sacrifice (6).

### III

> *Note:* Sanctification of Mahāvīra.

1\. You are the issue of the sprinkling horse; may Varuṇa, the holder of the rules heat you by the firm rules of Mitra and Varuṇa.[^P178_iii_r1]

[^P178_iii_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra for heating the Mahāvīra.

[[P179]]

2\. I give you for the (flames named) Arciṣ (shining), Śociṣ (burning), Jyotis (light) and Ātapa (heat).[^P179_r2]

[^P179_r2]: *(Mantra 2)* Mantra for placing the Mahāvīra on some burning wood of the household fire.

3\. Mitra becomes vast seeing this in the heaven with greatness and in the earth with the fame (1); (all say) the fame of the donation of the god Mitra, the holder of the beings; his donation is of wonderful fame.[^P179_r3]

[^P179_r3]: *(Mantra 3)* Mantra to be uttered over Mahāvīra.

4\. (I take) you for the performance.[^P179_r4]

[^P179_r4]: *(Mantra 4)* Mantra for wiping the ashes from the Mahāvīra.

5\. May the god Savitṛ take you above by the power having good hands, good fingers and good arms.[^P179_r5]

[^P179_r5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantra for lifting the Mahāvīra.

6\. Fulfill the quarters being not contained on the earth; stand up; be increased (2); stand firm above.[^P179_r6]

[^P179_r6]: *(Mantra 6)* Mantra for placing that on the sand.

7\. I see you by the eye of the sun for the straight (earth), for the good (mid-region), for the good residence (heaven) and for the prosperity (quarters).[^P179_r7]

[^P179_r7]: *(Mantra 7)* Mantra for looking at the Mahāvīra.

8\. This I make firm that, the son of that with the subjects, cattle and brilliance of sacred knowledge.[^P179_r8]

[^P179_r8]: *(Mantra 8)* Mantra for fixing it on the place.

9\. I sprinkle you by the Gāyatrī-verse; I sprinkle you by the Triṣṭubh-verse; I sprinkle you by the Jagatī-verse; may the speech sprinkle you, may the vigour sprinkle you, may the oblation sprinkle you; sprinkle the speech; sprinkle the vigour; sprinkle the oblation.[^P179_r9]

[^P179_r9]: *(Mantra 9)* Mantra for sprinkling the milk of the goat on it.

10\. O god of infront, I hang you above (3).[^P179_r10]

[^P179_r10]: *(Mantra 10)* Tying of the Mahāvīra in a skin of the black antelope.

[[P180]]

### IV

1\. O Brahman, I perform by the Pravargya (-sacrifice); O Hotṛ (priest), praise well the Gharma (Pravargya); O Āgnīdhra and Rauhiṇa (-priests) put the Puroḍāśa above; O Pratiprasthātṛ (-priest), bring; O Prastotṛ (-priest) sing the Sāmans.

2\. May the stobhas (hymns) carry you, who are full of Yajus-Mantras, of satisfied senses by the Sāmans, assented by the all-gods (Viśve-devas) and Maruts; made vast by the Dakṣiṇās (sacrificial fees), taking across and pleasant-minded one; may you put splendour for us being not injured; Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvar; perform (the Pravargya, O priests) (1).

### V

> *Note:* Exhortations (From 1 to 2). Placing of the Mahāvīra on the fire.

1\. O Brahman, we perform (Pravargya); O Hotṛ (-priest), praise the Gharma (Pravargya).[^P181_r1]

[^P181_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Exhortation to Brahman.

2\. (I sprinkle) you for the rule, for the sacrifice, for the brilliance of the sun.[^P181_r2]

[^P181_r2]: *(Mantra 2)* Throwing of the sacrificial pots on the fire.

3\. Hail for Prāṇa-breath! Hail for Vyāna-breath! Hail for Apāna-breath! Hail for the eye! Hail for the ear! Hail for the mind! Hail for Vāc and Sarasvatī! Hail for encouragement! Hail for the will! Hail for the vigour! Hail for the vitality! (1)[^P181_r3]

[^P181_r3]: *(Mantra 3)* Oblations to be poured on the Āhavanīya fire.

[[P181]]

4\. May the god Savitṛ mix you with the honey (1).[^P181_r4]

[^P181_r4]: *(Mantra 4)* Mantra for mixing the Mahāvīra with ghee.

5\. Protect the earth from the heat.[^P181_r5]

[^P181_r5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantra for throwing gold on another fire.

6\. You are the ray, you are the shining, you are the light.[^P181_r6]

[^P181_r6]: *(Mantra 6)* Mantra for placing the Muñja-grass on the gold.

7\. Sit well; you are great; shine becoming worth choosing by the gods; O pure and good fire, spread the smoke, which is mild and worth seeing.[^P181_r7]

[^P181_r7]: *(Mantra 7)* Putting of the Mahāvīra in its place.

8\. Whom the Brāhmaṇas, being heated by the fire, mix (with ghee) as making that vast and as one full of the fat; that Gharma (Pravargya) has come to the fire performing the Ṛta (sacrifice) like the dearest son near his father (2).[^P181_r8]

[^P181_r8]: *(Mantra 8)* Putting of the ghee on the Mahāvīra.

9\. (O goddess), not to be injured, in the east, in the rule of the fire, grant me life; as possessor of son, in the south, in the rule of Indra, grant me offspring; sitting well, in the west, in the rule of the god Savitṛ, grant me breath; famous, in the north (3), in the rule of Mitra-Varuṇa, grant me hearing; disposer, above, in the rule of Bṛhaspati, grant me Brahma (sacred knowledge), grant me Kṣatra (dominion), put brilliance in me, put splendour in me, put fame in me, put mind in me.[^P181_r9]

[^P181_r9]: *(Mantra 9)* Prayer to be uttered by the Yajamāna, holding the Kuśa-grass on the top of the Mahāvīra.

10\. You are the pervading of Manu (men), having many sons; protect me from all the violences (4); become well enjoyable for me; do not, do not kill.[^P181_r10]

[^P181_r10]: *(Mantra 10)* Mantra for touching the earth.

[[P182]]

11\. O fire, heat (destroy) the inner enemies; heat (destroy) the not praising and coming outer enemy; heat (destroy), O knowing Vasu (giver of residence), those who have no good heart (for us); for them may your (flames), not injured and not tired, stand firm.[^P182_r11]

[^P182_r11]: *(Mantra 11)* Mantra for throwing the charcoal.

12\. You are collected, you are collected well; Hail for Maruts; be spread all over.[^P182_r12]

[^P182_r12]: *(Mantra 12)* Mantra for throwing the charcoal round the Mahāvīra.

13\. You are measuring, you are good measuring, you are each measuring (5), you are well measuring, you are best measuring, you are above measuring, you are the inner holder of the mid-region.[^P182_r13]

[^P182_r13]: *(Mantra 13)* Mantra to be uttered while touching a sacrificial wood named 'Paridhi'.

14\. Protect the heaven from the heat.[^P182_r14]

[^P182_r14]: *(Mantra 14)* Mantra for placing the gold on Mahāvīra.

15\. O Indra (Mahāvīra), being increased by these praises, fulfill that which is little here; when you strike the great clouds for the praisers, may then we be your enjoyers.[^P182_r15_17]

[^P182_r15_17]: *(Mantras 15–17)* Mantras for sanctifying that.

16\. One is your white and one is your sacrificeable form (6); of different forms are the days; you are like the heaven; you protect all the measurings, O possessor of food; O Pūṣan (nourisher Mahāvīra), may your gift be auspicious here.

17\. O able Rudra (Mahāvīra), you hold the bow and the arrows; O able one, you wear various sacrificeable Niṣka (-ornament); O able one, you show mercy on this water and earth; there is none more powerful than you.

[[P183]]

18\. You are of Gāyatrī-verse, of Triṣṭup-verse and of Jagatī-verse.[^P183_r18]

[^P183_r18]: *(Mantra 18)* Mantra for taking a fan.

19\. Honey, honey, honey (7).[^P183_r19]

[^P183_r19]: *(Mantra 19)* Mantra for blazing the fire by that fan.

### VI

> *Note:* Mantras for sanctifying the Mahāvīra.

1\. You shine in the east by ten (rays); you shine in the south by ten (rays); you shine in the west by ten (rays); you shine in the north by ten (rays); you shine above by ten (rays); may you, pleasant-minded and not violated, put brilliance in me.[^P183_r6_1]

[^P183_r6_1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra to be uttered on the Mahāvīra.

2\. May the fire, with Vasus, make you shine in the east by the Gāyatrī-verse; you as such make us shine being shined.[^P183_r6_2_3]

[^P183_r6_2_3]: *(Mantras 2–3)* Mantras referring to the five quarters.

3\. May Indra, with Rudras, make you shine in the south by the Triṣṭup-verse; you as such make us shine being shined. May Varuṇa, with Ādityas, make you shine in the west by the Jagatī-verse; you as such make us shine being shined (1). May the heavenly Maruta (wind), with Maruts, make you shine in the north by the Anuṣṭup-verse; may you as such make us shine, being shined. May Bṛhaspati, with all-gods (Viśve-devas), make you shine above by the Paṅkti-verse. May you as such make us shine, being shined.

4\. O Gharma (Mahāvīra), you are shining among the gods; may I become shining among the men.[^P183_r6_4_7]

[^P183_r6_4_7]: *(Mantras 4–7)* Mantras to be uttered on the Mahāvīra.

[[P184]]

5\. You are among the gods emperor, shining, full of life, brilliant and having the splendour of the sacred knowledge; among the men may I become shining, full of life, brilliant and having splendour of the sacred knowledge.

6\. You are the shining, put the shining in me, (yea) the shining in me.

7\. You shine in the east by ten (rays); you shine in the south by ten (rays); you shine in the west by ten (rays); you shine in the north by ten (rays); you shine above by ten (rays), being pleasant-minded one; O emperor, you as such put food and vigour for us; as possessor of the food purifying me, the possessor of the food; the Gharma (Mahāvīra) has been shining by me; may I become shining (2).

### VII

> *Note:* Mantras for looking at the Mahāvīra.

1\. I have seen the protector (Mahāvīra in the form of the sun), which is not destroyable, moving in the ways infront and backward; that protects the (subjects), gathered or gone in different places; that comes again and again in the worlds.[^P184_r7_1]

[^P184_r7_1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra for looking at the Mahāvīra and praising as the protector.

2\. You protected us here by Madhu (spring season) and Mādhvī (wife of spring), (yea) by Madhu and Madhuci (wife of spring); (I see the Mahāvīra) following you for obtaining the gods.[^P184_r7_2]

[^P184_r7_2]: *(Mantra 2)* Mantra for looking and praising as the spring season.

3\. The fire has gone with the fire, with the god Savitṛ and shines with (the god) sun (1).[^P184_r7_3_4]

[^P184_r7_3_4]: *(Mantras 3–4)* Mantras for looking and praising as the summer season.

[[P185]]

4\. Hail! The fire has gone with the heat, with the god Savitṛ and shined with (the god) Sun.

5\. You shine as the holder of the heaven, the region, the holder of the earth, the holder of the vast mid-region, the holder god of the gods, the immortal one and born of the austerity.[^P185_r7_5]

[^P185_r7_5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantra for looking and praising as the rainy season.

6\. (I see you) for the sake of the heart; (I see) you for the sake of the mind; (I see) you for the sake of the heaven; (I see) you for the sake of the sun (2); make this sacrifice above, give these sacrificings to the gods in the heaven.[^P185_r7_6]

[^P185_r7_6]: *(Mantra 6)* Mantra for looking and praising as the autumn season.

7\. (I see you) O lord of all the earths, O lord of all the world; O lord of the mind of all; O lord of all the speech, O lord of all the austerity, O lord of all the Brahman (sacred knowledge).[^P185_r7_7]

[^P185_r7_7]: *(Mantra 7)* Mantra for looking and praising as the winter season.

8\. O Gharma (Pravargya), you are famous in the gods; protect the gods.[^P185_r7_8]

[^P185_r7_8]: *(Mantra 8)* Mantra for looking and praising as the dewy season.

9\. Grant us the speech, born of the austerity, going to the gods (3).[^P185_r7_9_17]

[^P185_r7_9_17]: *(Mantras 9–17)* Mantras for looking and praising as wives invested with many qualities.

10\. (This is) the womb of the gods.

11\. (This is) the father of the men.

12\. (This is) the lord of the subjects.

13\. (This is) the wisdom of the poets.

14\. This god has done effort with the god Savitṛ and shined with the sun.

15\. O Gharma (Mahāvīra), you are giver of life and brilliance to us.

[[P186]]

16\. You are our father, impel us as a father.

17\. You are the putter of the life, body, milk, brilliance, worship and wealth (4); O fulfiller of the mid-region, you are greater than the vast; may we obtain you, do not, do not kill.

18\. O fire, you are the lord of the house of the subjects, consisting of all the men; O youngest one, protect us from the sin by hundred fulfillings (of the wealth etc.); make us sacrificers for hundred winters, make us courageous who (were) lazy (first); may here, indeed, be the wealths.[^P186_r7_18]

[^P186_r7_18]: *(Mantra 18)* Praise of the fire.

19\. May I make sexual intercourse full of Tvaṣṭṛ (form-making god) by your protection; having good semen, keeping that semen, may I obtain the brave offspring in your presence; may I not be separated from wealth and nourishing (5).[^P186_r7_19]

[^P186_r7_19]: *(Mantra 19)* Mantra to be uttered by the wife of the sacrificer after looking at the Mahāvīra.

### VIII

> *Note:* Mantras for pouring milk on the Mahāvīra.

1\. I take you by the impelling of the god Savitṛ, by the arms of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan.[^P186_r8_1]

[^P186_r8_1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra for taking a rope.

2\. You are the bridle for Aditi.[^P186_r8_2]

[^P186_r8_2]: *(Mantra 2)* Mantra for purifying that rope.

3\. O Iḍā, come; O Aditi, come; O Sarasvatī, come.[^P186_r8_3_4]

[^P186_r8_3_4]: *(Mantras 3–4)* Mantras for calling the milch-cows.

4\. O that.....(name of the cow), come; O that.....come; O that.....come (1).

[[P187]]

5\. You are the turban of Aditi.[^P187_r8_5]

[^P187_r8_5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantra for tying the cows.

6\. You, the son of Iḍā, are the wind.[^P187_r8_6]

[^P187_r8_6]: *(Mantra 6)* Mantra for tying the calves.

7\. May Pūṣan release you; for Aśvins, cause to swell milk.[^P187_r8_7]

[^P187_r8_7]: *(Mantra 7)* Mantra for releasing the calves.

8\. O Sarasvatī, which is your udder, sleeping, comfortful, by which you nourish all the choosable ones, which keeps wealth, which is knower of the things, good donator; make that worth sucking for the gods.[^P187_r8_8]

[^P187_r8_8]: *(Mantra 8)* Mantra addressing to the cow.

9\. O calf, leave the Gharma (milk); O calf, protect the Gharma (2); leave for the Gharma (Pravargya).[^P187_r8_9]

[^P187_r8_9]: *(Mantra 9)* Mantra addressing to the calf.

10\. May Bṛhaspati go to you (for milking you).[^P187_r8_10]

[^P187_r8_10]: *(Mantra 10)* Mantra for going to milk the cow.

11\. (O udders!) you are donators, you are worth sucking by the calf and separated by the blood.[^P187_r8_11]

[^P187_r8_11]: *(Mantra 11)* Mantra for touching the udder.

12\. For Aśvins, cause to swell milk; for Sarasvatī cause to swell milk; for Bṛhaspati, cause to swell milk; for Indra, cause to swell milk; (yes) for Indra, cause to swell milk (3).[^P187_r8_12]

[^P187_r8_12]: *(Mantra 12)* Mantra for milking.

13\. You are of Gāyatrī-verse, you are of Triṣṭup-verse, you are of Jagatī-verse.[^P187_r8_13]

[^P187_r8_13]: *(Mantra 13)* Mantra addressing to the 'Śapha' and the 'Upayama' woods.

14\. Come to me with the part of the vigour.[^P187_r8_14]

[^P187_r8_14]: *(Mantra 14)* Mantra addressing to the milk.

15\. O Indra and Aśvins, drink the Gharma (milk with ghee) of the sweet honey.[^P187_r8_15]

[^P187_r8_15]: *(Mantra 15)* Mantra addressing to Indra and Aśvins.

[[P188]]

16\. Hail! I sacrifice you for the rays of the sun that cause rain.[^P188_r8_16]

[^P188_r8_16]: *(Mantra 16)* Mantra for offering the oblation to the sun.

17\. You are the sweet oblation.[^P188_r8_17]

[^P188_r8_17]: *(Mantra 17)* Mantra addressing to the milk of the goat.

18\. Heat even the heat of the sun.[^P188_r8_18]

[^P188_r8_18]: *(Mantra 18)* Mantra addressing to the light of the sun.

19\. I take you by the heaven and the earth (4).[^P188_r8_19]

[^P188_r8_19]: *(Mantra 19)* Mantra for taking the Mahāvīra by two Śapha woods.

20\. I raise you by the mid-region.[^P188_r8_20]

[^P188_r8_20]: *(Mantra 20)* Mantra for dusting away the ashes.

21\. May I become able to hold you being assented by the gods and the fathers.[^P188_r8_21]

[^P188_r8_21]: *(Mantra 21)* Mantra for lifting the Mahāvīra.

22\. You are the brilliance; follow the brilliance; touching the heaven, do not, do not violate; touching the mid-region, do not, do not violate; touching the earth, do not, do not violate; you are paradise, grant me paradise; grant me heaven, protect me from the heaven (5).[^P188_r8_22]

[^P188_r8_22]: *(Mantra 22)* Mantra for bringing the Mahāvīra.

### IX

> *Note:* Sacrificing of the oblations to the Āhavanīya-fire.

1\. Hail for you, the wind, the ocean! Hail for you, the wind, the water! Hail for you, the wind, the non-injurable! Hail for you, the wind, the protector! Hail for you, the wind, the moving one! Hail for you, the wind, the active! Hail for you, the fire, full of the things! Hail for you, Soma, accompanied with Rudras! Hail for you, Varuṇa, accompanied with Ādityas (1)! Hail for you, Bṛhaspati, accompanied with all the gods! Hail for you, Savitṛ, accompanied with Ṛbhus, accompanied with the pervading one, accompanied with the lord, accompanied with the food! Hail for you, Yama, accompanied with Aṅgiras, accompanied with the fathers.[^P188_r9_1]

[^P188_r9_1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra for sacrificing the oblations.

[[P189]]

2\. All the good-wishes are (in Brahman), sitting in the south.[^P189_r9_2]

[^P189_r9_2]: *(Mantra 2)* Mantra for circumambulating the Āhavanīya-fire.

3\. (He) worshipped all the gods.[^P189_r9_3]

[^P189_r9_3]: *(Mantra 3)* Mantra for looking at the Hotṛ-priest.

4\. O Aśvins, drink this sweet Gharma (milk with ghee) of which Hail has been done! Hail for the fire, the sacrificeable! May there propitiation be by the Yajus-Mantra.[^P189_r9_4]

[^P189_r9_4]: *(Mantra 4)* Mantra for sanctifying the sacrificial things.
