5\. O Aśvins, drink the Gharma (milk with ghee) which is good for the heart (2); (protect us) by your shining protections (in this sacrificing) day; may the heaven and earth assent you; Hail for Indra![^P189_r9_5_6]

[^P189_r9_5_6]: *(Mantras 5–6)* Mantras for sacrificing the oblations on the fire.

6\. Hail for Indra! Vāṭ (Hail)!

7\. Aśvins drank the Gharma (milk and ghee), which was good for the heart; the heaven and the earth assented you; that thing is as Vāṭ (Hail). Salutation to the heaven, salutation to the mid-region, salutation to the earth (3); put this sacrifice in the heaven; (yea) in the heaven put this sacrifice; go to the heaven, go to the mid-region, go to the earth, go to the five great quarters, go to the gods, who drink the Gharma (milk with ghee), go to the fathers, who drink the Gharma (milk with ghee) (4).[^P189_r9_7]

[^P189_r9_7]: *(Mantra 7)* Prayer after the sacrificing.

[[P190]]

### X

> *Note:* Agnihotra sacrifice from the remaining articles.

1\. Be increased for the food; be increased for the vigour; be increased for Brahman; be increased for the dominion; be increased for the waters; be increased for the plants; be increased for the vegetables; be increased for the heaven and the earth; be increased for the good beings; be increased for the brilliance of the sacred knowledge (1); be increased for the sacrificer; be increased for my greatness.[^P190_r10_1]

[^P190_r10_1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra to be uttered on the remaining article.

2\. (I sacrifice) you for the brilliance, for the shining wealth, for the senses and for the prosperity.[^P190_r10_2]

[^P190_r10_2]: *(Mantra 2)* Mantra addressed to the Pravargya with reference to the quarters.

3\. You are the holder; may you become my good holder; put the brilliance of the sacred knowledge in us, put the dominion, put the subject; may the wind not move you even (2).[^P190_r10_3]

[^P190_r10_3]: *(Mantra 3)* Mantra for replacing the Mahāvīra.

4\. I seat you in his breath, who hates us and whom we hate; go with him useless (making his works useless).[^P190_r10_4]

[^P190_r10_4]: *(Mantra 4)* Mantra for destroying the enemies.

5\. Hail for Pūṣan, the destroyer of the enemies! Hail for ...! Hail for the promoting ones! Hail for the stones! Hail for the heaven and the earth! Hail for the fathers, drinkers of the Gharma (milk with ghee)![^P190_r10_5]

[^P190_r10_5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantra for putting the fuels in the fire.

6\. Hail for Rudra, the chief Rudra.[^P190_r10_6]

[^P190_r10_6]: *(Mantra 6)* Mantra for throwing a wood behind the priest's back.

7\. May the light of the day be connected with the impelling (of the sun); the good light of the lights; Hail! May the light of the night be connected with the impelling (of the moon); the good light of the lights.[^P190_r10_7]

[^P190_r10_7]: *(Mantra 7)* Mantra for offering the oblation by the Pratiprasthātṛ-priest.

[[P191]]

8\. You have fulfilled (my desires); protect me from the day and the night; this is, O fire, your fuel; be increased by that; give me life; make me full of brilliance. You have fulfilled (my desires); protect me from the night and the day (4); this is, O fire, your fuel; be increased by that; give me life; make me full of brilliance.[^P191_r10_8]

[^P191_r10_8]: *(Mantra 8)* Mantra for morning and evening offering of fuel.

9\. The fire is the light; the light is the sun, the sun is the light; the light is the sun; Hail![^P191_r10_9]

[^P191_r10_9]: *(Mantra 9)* Mantra for morning and evening oblations.

10\. Bhūḥ (the earth or the being), Hail! The fire, Hail![^P191_r10_10]

[^P191_r10_10]: *(Mantra 10)* Mantra for offering prickly wood after touching the water.

11\. The oblation, the sweet oblation has been sacrificed in the most lordly fire; you are our father; do not, do not kill; I have seen your (essence), O divine Gharma (Pravargya), (the essence of) one full of honey, full of food, full of corn, full of the beauty of the limbs, full of the Svadhā (sacrificial oblation); we have enjoyed you as such; do not, do not kill.[^P191_r10_11]

[^P191_r10_11]: *(Mantra 11)* Mantra to be uttered after eating the remaining offering.

12\. Hail for you! Hail for the rays of the sun; Hail for you! (Hail) for the stars! (5)[^P191_r10_12]

[^P191_r10_12]: *(Mantra 12)* Mantra for filling the sacrificial vessels for morning and evening offerings, before the Āhavanīya fire.

### XI

> *Note:* Mantras for taking down the Pravargya.

1\. O Gharma (Pravargya), what is your sorrow in the heaven, what is in the Gāyatrī-verse, what is in the Brāhmaṇa, what is in the sacrificial place (Havirdhāna); all that I destroy by this sacrifice.[^P191_r11_1]

[^P191_r11_1]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra for making an oblation by the Adhvaryu-priest, while the Pratiprasthātṛ-priest, having lighted one of the three splinters of pointed wood, holds it high near the face.

[[P192]]

2\. O Gharma (Pravargya), what is your sorrow in the mid-region, what is in the Triṣṭup-verse, what is in the Rājanya (ruling caste Kṣatriya), what is in the Āgnīdhra-priest; I destroy all that by this sacrifice, Hail! O Gharma, what is your sorrow on the earth, in the Jagatī-verse, in the Vaiśya (caste); I destroy all that by this sacrifice, Hail![^P192_r11_2]

[^P192_r11_2]: *(Mantra 2)* Mantra while the lighted wood is held near the navel and the knee.

3\. "May the assent for us today" and "you, indeed, in assent" (these Mantras should be uttered).[^P192_1][^P192_r11_3]

[^P192_1]: T.S. 3.3.11.

[^P192_r11_3]: *(Mantra 3)* Mantra for throwing the splinters in the Āhavanīya fire and bringing the wife of the sacrificer there.

4\. We follow you, the protecting one of the heaven; protect (2) the bodies of the mid-region; we follow you, the holder of the earth for the new fruit.[^P192_r11_4_6]

[^P192_r11_4_6]: *(Mantras 4–6)* Mantra for making holes at three parts for carrying the Pravargya.

5\. We follow you, the protector of the Brahman (Brāhmaṇa caste); protect the bodies of the Kṣatra (Kṣatriya caste); we follow you, the holder of the Viśa (Vaiśya caste or the subject) for the new fruit.

6\. We follow you, for the protection of the breath; protect the bodies of the eye; I follow you, the holder of the ear for the new fruit.

7\. You are the beauty of the propitiation-holding (3), the son of the men-holding one.[^P192_3]

[^P192_3]: *(Mantras 7–8)* Mantras for Adhvaryu to circumambulate the Uttara Vedi sprinkling the water by a jar.

8\. You carry the propitiation, you carry (that) from all (the directions).

9\. The quadrangular (altar) is the navel of Ṛta (settled order).[^P192_r11_9]

[^P192_r11_9]: *(Mantra 9)* Mantra for touching the Uttara Vedi.

[[P193]]

10\. You are the seat; full of all the life and the vast comfort.[^P193_r11_10]

[^P193_r11_10]: *(Mantra 10)* Mantra for cleaning the Uttara Vedi.

11\. We have put away the haters and the wicked ones and obtained the opposite of these by the rule (of this sacrifice).[^P193_r11_11]

[^P193_r11_11]: *(Mantra 11)* Mantra for overcoming the enemies.

12\. O Gharma, this is your food, this is your fulfilling; be (4) increased and satisfied by that; may we become increased and satisfied.[^P193_r11_12]

[^P193_r11_12]: *(Mantra 12)* Mantra for filling some cups with curd and honey.

13\. You are the divine Gandharva named Ranti, your sacrificing roof is one having the feet; the fire is the president; Rudra is sovereign.[^P193_r11_13_21]

[^P193_r11_13_21]: *(Mantras 13–21)* Mantras addressed to the Pravargya while standing before it.

14\. May we be connected with the life, with the breath, with lordship of the cows, with the brilliance, with the milk, with the wealth and the nourishing (5).

15\. Who hates us and whom we hate, he has been destroyed.

16\. (This Pravargya, as) the sprinkler, the destroyer (of the sins), the great, the observable like Mitra, has shouted and shines with the sun.

17\. You are the knower, the cause of the ocean (cloud), the pleasing one, the courageous, the falcon, the observer of Ṛta (Settled Order), having the golden feathers, the bird, the nourisher, sitting firm in the great seat of the gathering (6); may salutation be to you, do not, do not kill.

18\. The waters saw Soma-stealing Viśvāvasu Gandharva; they went to him by the Ṛta (Settled Order); Indra also followed them hastily; he saw the encirclings of the sun (for the protection of Soma).

[[P194]]

19\. May Viśvāvasu, the divine Gandharva, the maker of the pleasing songs, tell us all that which we have performed (and) which we do not know (7); impelling our wisdoms may he protect our wisdoms. He found the purifying (Soma) in the feet of the rivers; opened the doors of (the rivers), closed by the stones; the Gandharva said about the nectar of these; then Indra knew him not less.

20\. O god Gharma (Pravargya), this you obtained the gods.

21\. This man, I obtained the men; O drinking of Soma, come to me.

22\. May the waters and the plants be good friends of us; may they become bad friends of him, who hates me or whom I hate.[^P194_r11_22]

[^P194_r11_22]: *(Mantra 22)* Mantra for turning Pravargya round the fire.

23\. "May we, above the darkness"[^P194_r11_23][^P194_1]

[^P194_r11_23]: *(Mantra 23)* Mantra for cleaning the Pravargya.

[^P194_1]: T.S. 4.1.7.

24\. and "Above, that, verily," "wonderful" (these Mantras should be uttered).[^P194_r11_24][^P194_2]

[^P194_r11_24]: *(Mantra 24)* Mantras for offering the oblations and inviting the sun.

[^P194_2]: T.S. 1.4.43.

25\. O fire, proclaim this new (sacrifice), connected with the Gāyatrī-verse for the fruit-giving to us among the gods (8).[^P194_r11_25]

[^P194_r11_25]: *(Mantra 25)* Mantras for making oblation to the Āhavanīya fire.

### XII

> *Note:* Collection of the milk, honey, and curd in the Mahāvīra pot.

1\. You are the milk of the great cows milked for the gods.[^P194_r1_3]

[^P194_r1_3]: *(Mantras 1–3)* Mantras for pouring the milk, the honey, and the curd in the Mahāvīra respectively.

2\. You are the shining of the light, the elixir of the vegetables and the plants.

3\. We, the possessors of the food, put you, the possessor of the food having a mind for obtaining the high heaven.

[[P195]]

### XIII

1\. The heaven sprinkled the earth; the young bull sprinkled the cows; all these worlds have been sprinkled; may the sprinkled sacrifice generate.

2\. The sprinkled one generated, (yea), generated well; from the sprinkled one the sprinkling one is generated; may we generate from our sprinkled one.

### XIV

1\. I destroy that lightning, which fell from the east by this your sacrifice; I destroy that lightning, which fell from the south, west, north, or above by this your sacrifice.

### XV

1\. Hail for Prāṇa-breath! Hail for Apāna-breath! Hail for the eye!

Hail for Vyāna-breath!

Hail!

Hail for the ear! Hail for the mind! Hail for the Vāc! Hail for Sarasvatī!

### XVI

> *Note:* Expiations for defects in the Pravargya rite.

1\. Hail for Pūṣan, the destroyer of the enemies! Hail for Pūṣan, the good and well way! Hail for Pūṣan, the pleaser of the men! Hail for Pūṣan, full of light! Hail for Pūṣan, the leader of the men; Hail for Pūṣan, full of knowledge![^P195_r1_2]

[^P195_r1_2]: *(Mantras 1–2)* Expiations for defects.

[[P196]]

### XVII

> *Note:* Consecration of the receptacle of the fire.

1\. The sun, rising by the power of this, did not get the trouble and holds all the burden like the earth; may the clean goddess Manīṣā (wisdom) come to us like a well-made, swift chariot. Some, worshipping that, decide that as the great Sāman; they held the sun by that; by that they made the sun shine; what is this Gharma (Pravargya), the head (of the sacrifice), that is this fire. You are the fulfilling, may that (Pravargya) be dear with offspring and cattle; may Prajāpati put you with that god; sit firm with Aṅgiras.

### XVIII

> *Note:* Prayer to Agni.

1\. O Fire, what are your wet causes (fuel) and what are your dried causes (fuels as nest); what are your prosperous causes and what are your novel causes and what is, O Fire, your body named vigour; by those two being concordant sit here with offspring and wealth (for us). May Prajāpati put you with that god; sit firm with Aṅgiras.[^P196_r1]

[^P196_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Prayer to Agni.

### XIX

> *Note:* Consecration of the receptacle of the fire.

1\. You are Agni (fire); you are Vaiśvānara (fire, belonging to all men); you are Saṁvatsara (year, the first in the cycle of six years); you are Parivatsara (the second in the cycle of six years); you are Idāvatsara (the third year); you are Iḍuvatsara (the fourth year); you are Idvatsara (the fifth year); you are Vatsara (the sixth year of the cycle of the six years); of you, as such, the spring season is the head; the summer, the right wing; the rains, the tail; the autumn, the left wing; the dewy season, the middle body; the first wings are the collections (of the fire); the next wings are the Purīṣa (the dung-fuel); the days and the nights are the bricks. Of you, as such, may the months and the half-months be (able for their work); may your seasons be (able for their work); may your year be (able for its work); may your days and nights be (able for their work); A-this, Pra-this, Vi-this, Sam-this, Ud-this — (by these the able-being is specified). May Prajāpati put you with that god; sit firm with Aṅgiras.[^P196_r1_ext]

[^P196_r1_ext]: *(Mantra 1)* Mantra for placing the hot bricks.

[[P197]]

Bhur, Bhuvah, Suvah.

### XX

1\. Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ.

2\. "Above, for our well-protection", "Protect us above from..." (these mantras should be uttered).[^P197_r2]

3\. The old age ate (the Mahāvīra), the rule-maker; respected one, being in the young age; see the greatness of the God that it died today which was similar (to young) yesterday.[^P197_r3]

Expiation for the breaking of the Mahāvīra.

[^P197_r2]: *(Mantra 2)* Expiation for all defects.

[^P197_r3]: *(Mantra 3)* Expiation for the fall of the Mahāvīra.

[[P198]]

4\. Which was broken before by decaying in absence of joining substance; the wealthy Indra will join that having the (joining) articles before him (1); he will perform that broken (sacrifice).[^P197_r4]

5\. Again with vigour and wealth.[^P197_r5]

6\. O Gharma, do not trouble us being troubled; do not make our high (work) low; do not bring us to the passion; do not put us, verily, in the darkness; may not the increasing (cruel) persons of Rudra come to us.[^P198_r6_9]

7\. O Gharma, do not check us from the good fruit hating us on account of our fault-ful sacrifice; may not Rudra and misfortune check us; and may not the heaven and the earth check us. (2)

8\. O Mitra-Varuṇa, protect us here coming near us; O friends, make us shining here respectively; may the good-binding weapon of Ādityas, which is violent, touching hundred ones and poisonous, protect us from every side.

9\. "O Varuṇa, my this", "That I beg you", "You for us, O Fire", "O Fire, you as such for us", "O Fire, you are obtainable"[^P198_1] (these Mantras should be uttered).

10\. "May we, above the darkness",[^P198_2] "Above, that wonderful", "Good-feathered birds"[^P198_3] (these Mantras should be uttered). (3)[^P198_r10]

[^P197_r4]: *(Mantra 4)* Expiation for the breaking of the Mahāvīra.

[^P197_r5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantra for not turning the contents of the Mahāvīra.

[^P198_r6_9]: *(Mantras 6–9)* Expiation for the oblation boiling over the Mahāvīra pot.

[^P198_r10]: *(Mantra 10)* Expiation if the sun sets before the complete cooking of the oblation.

[^P198_1]: T.A. 2.3.

[^P198_2]: T.S. 4.1.7.

[^P198_3]: T.Br. 2.5.8.

[[P199]]

### XXI

1\. Bhūḥ, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ. Put that great sense-organ in me; courage in me; the power in me; put good vigour in me; may the Gharma shine in three ways for me with will and mind, with food and light, with sacrifice and milk, with Brahman and brilliance, with Kṣatra and fame, with truth and austerity; may we enjoy the milking of that (Gharma); may we enjoy the comfort of that; may we enjoy the food of that; may we, being assented, drink your juice, which has been drunk by Indra first, assented and full of honey.[^P199_10]

[^P199_10]: *(Benedictory Mantra)* Benedictory Mantra at the end of the Pravargya.

### XXII

> *Note:* Addresses to the fire.

1\. O Fire, what are your cruel forms — hunger and thirst, not making out and not eating, great hunger and great thirst, laziness and ignorance — these are, O Fire, your cruel form; by these go to him, who hates us and whom we hate.[^P199_r1]

[^P199_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Addresses to the Fire.

### XXIII

> *Note:* Addresses to the fire.

1\. Longing, longing for improper things and moisture, hot and cool, cruel and fierceful, freezing, laziness and absence of food — these are, O Fire, your cruel forms; by these go to him, who hates us and whom we hate.[^P200_r1]

[^P200_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Addresses to the Fire.

[[P200]]

### XXIV

> *Note:* Naming of the cooking pans by the names of Maruts.

1\. Dhuni, Dhvanta, Dhvena, Dhvanayen, Niliṁpa, Vilimpa, and Vikṣipa (these are the names of Maruts).[^P200_r1_maruts]

[^P200_r1_maruts]: *(Mantra 1)* Naming of the cooking pans by the names of Maruts.

### XXV

1\. Ugra, Dhuni, Dhvanta, Dhvena, Dhvanayen, Sebeṣvan, Sehemana, Sahīyān, Etya, Pretya, Vikṣipa (these are the names of Maruts).

### XXVI

> *Note:* Imprecatory phrases.

1\. May the days and the nights impell you above; may the half-months win you, indeed; may the months be at you; may the seasons cook you; may the year kill you, O you (thief).[^P200_r1_imprec]

[^P200_r1_imprec]: *(Mantra 1)* Imprecatory phrases.

### XXVII

> *Note:* Expiations if the oblations are stolen.

1\. Khaat, Phat, Jahi, Chindhi, Bhindhi, Hendhi, Kat — these are the cruel speeches.[^P201_r1]

[^P201_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Expiations if the oblations are stolen.

[[P201]]

### XXVIII

1\. O Indra, moving set free the cows to move; make us, the last ones, possessors of the cattle; punish that ill-wishing one by the thunderbolt, strike on that sleeping one for (taking) his food.

2\. O Fire, be concordant with the fire; O death, be concordant with the death; O prosperous one, may salutation be to you.

3\. O Fire, salutation to you once, salutation to you twice, salutation to you thrice, salutation to you four times, salutation to you five times, salutation to you ten times, salutation to you hundred times, salutation to you thousand times, salutation to you innumerable times; may salutation be to you; do not, do not kill.

### XXIX

1\. You are the envoy of Yama and Rudra both sent (here, in the form) having bloody mouth, being unknown on account of being covered by the blood, having the feet like the dog, the envoy of Yama you run as a vulture, having good feathers you enjoy the corpse.

### XXX

> *Note:* Expiations if a vulture, jackal, or wolf is seen.

1\. O goddess of speech, which is this crying as a jackal; may that cry for my enemy; O Death, take that to the death; may that trouble by the trouble.[^P201_r30]

Expiations if the wolf is seen. Expiations if a vulture is seen. Expiations if a jackal is seen.

[^P201_r30]: *(Mantra 1)* Expiations if a jackal is seen (also wolf/vulture).

[[P202]]

### XXXI

1\. Which attacking ram cries this speech being sent by someone or coming by his own will, may Indra and Fire, being concordant with Brahman, make that speech auspicious for us.

### XXXII

1\. O long-faced and bad-jawed, do not cry in our right side; if you speak in my right side, then destroy our enemies.

### XXXIII

1\. The owl has come in this way having golden eye and iron-mouth, the envoy of the demons (Rākṣasas); O Fire, destroy that from here.

### XXXIV

> *Note:* Expiation if a man possessed by ghosts speaks.

1\. Which you are speaking superhuman speeches, entering the beings, speak that for defeating our enemies; O Death, take them to the death; may they obtain trouble by the trouble; may the Fire be concordant with the Fire.[^P202_r1]

[^P202_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Expiation if a man possessed by the ghosts speaks.

[[P203]]

### XXXV

1\. You fall here spreading your thighs, you shut again and again your left eye; may there not be any disease to anyone.

Expiations if a vulture falls.

### XXXVI

> *Note:* Expiations if the worms fall in the oblation.

1\. O worm, I kill you by (the help of) Atri, Kaṇva, and Jamadagni; by the sacred knowledge of Viśvāvasu the king of the worms has been killed, also the old one (of the worms) has been killed, also the father, the mother (have died), and then also the fat ones, the little ones, the black ones, the white ones, and the Asetikās (little-living ones) have been killed; all have been killed with the white ones.[^P203_r1]

[^P203_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Expiations if the worms fall in the oblation.

### XXXVII

> *Note:* Oblation for killing somebody by imprecation.

1\. Taking and killing that (enemy) I put that in the jaws of Yama like the cooked oblation; what is true, may this (speech) be like that; Khan, Phan, Mrasi (die being broken and destroyed).[^P203_r37]

[^P203_r37]: *(Mantra 1)* Oblation for killing somebody by imprecation.

[[P204]]

### XXXVIII

1\. I curse you by sacred knowledge, I curse you by the oath of the sacred knowledge; I see you by the cruel eyes of Bhṛgus; I meditate you by the cruel mind of Āṅgirasas; I injure by the edge of the cruel weapon; O you named such, fall beneath me.

### XXXIX

> *Note:* Siṃījavarī-herb should be plucked in the cow-shed of him who is to be killed by imprecation.

1\. O Siṃījavarī-herb, trouble (the enemy); O herb, born on his bed, trouble him on the bed; make him enter the mountains; kill there near the rays of the death; destroy that enemy, who hates us or whom we hate, in the time by which the sun rises in the east.[^P204_r1]

[^P204_r1]: *(Mantra 1)* Siṃījavarī-herb should be plucked in the cow-shed of him who is to be killed by imprecation.

### XL

> *Note:* Imprecation for killing the enemies / Sāma hymn.

1\. Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ; Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ; Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ; from the earth may be performed, from the earth may be performed; from the earth may be performed; may happiness be in happiness (in us), may happiness be in happiness (in us), may happiness be in happiness (in us); obtainable thing has been obtained, obtainable thing has been obtained, obtainable thing has been obtained. (O goddess) in us, in us may the light like the sun be.[^P204_r1_xl]

[^P204_r1_xl]: *(Mantra 1)* Imprecation for killing the enemies (with Sāma hymn uttered loudly).

[[P205]]

### XLI

> *Note:* Offering of the Udumbara wood to the fire while making consecration.

1\. The earth is the fuel; the fire burned that, that (earth) burned the fire; I burned that (earth), may that, being burnt, burn me (make me shine) with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge, food etc., Hail![^P205_r1_3]

2\. The mid-region is the fuel; the wind burned that; that burned the wind; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge, food etc., Hail! The heaven is the fuel; the sun burned that; that burned the sun; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge and food etc., Hail![^P205_r2]

3\. You are my fuel, related to Prajāpati, destroyer of the enemies, killer of the enemy, Hail!

4\. O Fire, the lord of the rules, I will follow the rules; (3) may I do so; may that be increased. O wind, the lord of the rules, O Āditya, the lord of the rules, O Vratapati (lord of the rules), the ruling lord of the rules, I will follow the rules; may I do so; may that be increased.[^P205_r4]

5\. The heaven is the fuel; the sun burned that; that burned the sun; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance[^P205_4], splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge, food etc., Hail! The mid-region is the fuel; the wind burned that; that burned the wind; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame[^P206_5], brilliance of sacred knowledge, food etc., Hail! The earth is the fuel; the fire burned that; that burned the fire; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge[^P206_6], food etc., Hail! You are my fuel, related to Prajāpati, destroyer of the enemies, killer of the enemies. Aditya, lord of the rules, I followed the rules; I could follow that, that was increased for me. O wind, the lord of the rules, O fire, the lord of the rules, O Vratapati (lord of the rules), the ruling lord of the rules, I followed the rules; I could follow that, that was increased for me (7).[^P205_r5]

[^P205_r1_3]: *(Mantras 1–3)* Mantras for offering the Udumbara wood.

[^P205_r2]: *(Mantra 2)* Offering of Udumbara wood.

[^P205_4]: Brilliance: shining light of sacred knowledge.

[^P205_r4]: *(Mantra 4)* Invitation of the gods.

[^P205_r5]: *(Mantra 5)* Repetitions of the first three Mantras.

[[P206]]

[^P206_5]: Fame: widespread renown.

[^P206_6]: Brilliance of sacred knowledge: divine intuition.

### XLII

1\. May the wind, living in the mid-region, blow being auspicious for us; may the sun shine being auspicious for us; may the days be auspicious for us; may the night be performed auspicious for us.

2\. May the dawn come being auspicious for us; may the sun shine being auspicious for us; O well merciful Sarasvatī, be auspicious and most wealful for us; may we not see any lack being in your view.[^P206_r_benedict]

[^P206_r_benedict]: *(Benedictory Mantras)* Benedictory Mantras to be uttered in the end taking some hot water in the hand (from 1 to 36).

[[P207]]

3\. (O earth!) you are the residence for Iḍā (food), all possess residence by you; may we become the possessors of the residences; may he become without residence, who hates us and whom we hate.

4\. You are the firm-standing; may we become the possessors of the firm-standing; may we not be cut from the firm-standing; may he become without any firm-standing, who hates us and whom we hate.

5\. O wind, bring the medicine, remove away the sin; you are the possessor of all the medicines; you move as the envoy of the gods.

6\. These two winds blow from the ocean to the distant lands (1); may one bring us courage and may the other remove away whatever is sin.

7\. O wind, which is that nectar put in your home, from that give us for living, from that put in us as medicine and from that carry greatness for us.

8\. May the wind blow as medicine being auspicious and comfortable for our heart; may that increase our lives.

9\. You are the home of Indra; I obtain you with cows, with horses and with that, whatever is ours.

10\. I obtain Bhūr (earth); I obtain Bhuvaḥ (mid-region); I obtain Suvaḥ (heaven); I obtain the wind; I obtain the untroubled god; I obtain the unbreakable stone; I obtain Brahma-Kośa (sheath of Brahman) of Prajāpati; I obtain Brahman; I obtain OṂ.

11\. May the mid-region be vast for us; (in that) may the fires and the mountains be great; by which welfare the wind makes welfare for me, by that may my inner welfare be.

[[P208]]

12\. May Prāṇa-breath and Apāna-breath protect me from the death; may Prāṇa and Apāna not leave me.

13\. May the fire put wisdom in me, offspring in me, brilliance in me; may Indra put wisdom in me, offspring in me, sense-organ in me; may the sun put wisdom in me, offspring in me and the shining in me.[^P208_20]

14\. O Aśvins, protect us in the days and the nights by the propitiation and good fortune; may Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, seas, earth, and heaven please me.

15\. By the protection of Prajāpati has been performed this wonderful (sacrifice), a friend, covered by the protection of Prajāpati.

16\. May Prajāpati, the true and best increaser of the happiness and food, make you happy; may he make the wealth firm for destroying the evils.

17\. Become the good protector of us, the praisers and the friends by your hundred protections.

18\. The seers (in the form of) the good-feathered birds, favouring the wisdoms, went to Indra begging: Remove away the darkness; fulfill the eye; set us free who are bound as treasure.

19\. May the goddesses, waters, be for our propitiation and drinking, may they flow being auspicious and destroyers of the troubles.

20\. I ask the medicine from the waters which are able to grant choosable things and the residences of the moving ones.

21\. May the waters and the plants be good friends of us; may they become bad friends of those who hate us and whom we hate.

[^P208_20]: Shining: divine lustre.

[[P209]]

22\. O Waters, you are full of comforts; may you as such put us in the vigour for seeing the great beautiful ones.

23\. What is your most auspicious elixir, make us shareholders of that like the wishing mothers.

24\. May we obtain well that elixir by the residence of which you become pleased (4); O Waters, make us powerful to generate.

25\. The earth is peaceful, it has become peaceful by the fire, may that destroy my sorrow being peaceful.

26\. The mid-region is peaceful, that has become peaceful by the wind; may that destroy my sorrow being peaceful. The heaven is peaceful, that is peaceful by the sun; may that destroy my sorrow being peaceful.

27\. The earth is the peace, the mid-region is the peace, the heaven is the peace, the quarters are the peace, the intermediate quarters are the peace, the fire is the peace, the wind is the peace, the sun is the peace, the moon is the peace, the stars are the peace, the waters are the peace, the plants are the peace, the cow is the peace, the goat is the peace, the horse is the peace, the man is the peace, Brahma is the peace, the Brāhmaṇa is the peace, the peace is the peace, may the peace be for me, the peace.

28\. I make peace by that peace, the all-peace for my men and cattle; may the peace be for me, the peace.

29\. May the prosperity, shyness, holding power, austerity, wisdom, firm-standing, faith, truth, religion, these stand following me when I stand; may not the prosperity, shyness, holding power, austerity, wisdom, firm-standing, faith, truth, religion, these leave me.

[[P210]]

30\. May I stand up by life, by my own life, by the elixir of the plants and also by the power of the rain for the immortality.

31\. May we see for hundred autumns that eye (orb of the sun), rising brilliantly, assigned by the gods; may we live for hundred autumns; may we be happy for hundred autumns, may we be delighted for hundred autumns, may we be for hundred autumns, may we hear for hundred autumns, may we speak well for hundred autumns, may we be not defeated for hundred autumns, may we be able to see the sun for long time.

32\. May that sprinkling sun purify me, which has come from the great ocean from the midst of the waters, who is of red eyes and wise.

33\. (O Veda!) you are the flowing of Brahman, you are the two pins of Brahman, you are the holding place of Brahman; by this Brahman this great earth has been held, by this great mid-region has been held; this held the great heaven full of the gods; may I hold that which I know; may the Veda not be forgotten by me.

34\. May the wisdom and the thinking power enter me; for accepting the past and future may I live the whole span of life; may I live the whole span of life.

35\. By these praising songs fulfill that which is our little (limb). O Indra, when you break the great clouds for the sake of the praisers, may then we be your great share-holders.

36\. I have proclaimed Brahma; may not that leave me.[^P210_51]

Om Peace, Peace, Peace.

[^P210_51]: Pañcāti marker or section end note.

[[P211]]

Salutation to the speech, which has been uttered; salutation to that speech which is yet unuttered; salutation to the speech, salutation to the lord of the speech (Vācaspati), salutation to the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras; may not the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras disobey me; may I not disobey the seers, the makers of the Mantra, the lords of the Mantras. May I speak the speech which is related to all the gods, auspicious, unbroken and dear to the gods. May the heaven be comfortable for me, may the earth be comfortable for me, may this all world be comfortable for me, may the moon and the sun be comfortable for me, may Brahman and Prajāpati be comfortable for me. I will speak the matter, the world, the light, the austerity, Brahman and the truth. I spread this bed for that; may there be bed (shelter) for my offspring, cattle; may I become bed (shelter) for my cattle and offspring. May Prāṇa-breath and Apāna-breath protect me from the death; may Prāṇa and Apana not leave me. I will think the honey, I will produce the honey, may I speak the honeyful (sweet) speech for the gods, well-hearing for the men (fathers); may the gods protect me as such; may the fathers also please me for the sake of beauty.

Om Peace!

Peace!

Peace!

[[P212]]

## V

> *Note:* Brāhmaṇas of the Mantras of the Prapāṭhaka IV.[^P213_1]

[[P213]]

### I

> *Note:* An anecdote about the Pravargya.

Auspicious for us... may that not leave us. Om Peace! Peace! Peace![^P213_2]

[^P213_1]: *(Prapāṭhaka V)* Brāhmaṇas of the Mantras of the Prapāṭhaka IV.

[^P213_2]: The last Anuvāka of the Prapāṭhaka IV should be uttered for the propitiation.

1\. The gods began a prosperous Satra (a great Soma-sacrifice or session) desirous of fame. They said, "What fame Kurukṣetra has comes to us first, may that be of all us together." Their altar, Khāṇḍava was the right half of that; Tūrghna was the left half, Pariṇat was the hinder part, the Maru-country was the (middle part); of them the Utkara (anything dug out for throwing grass etc.) (1); sacrifice of Viṣṇu obtained the fame.[^P213_r1_6]

[^P213_r1_6]: *(Kaṇḍikās 1–6)* The anecdote.

2\. Viṣṇu wished that very much (not to distribute among the gods); he escaped away with that; the gods followed him to check the fame; of him, being followed, the bow was born from the left and the arrows were born from the right; therefore, arrow and bow have pious birth, for they are born of the sacrifice (2); many gods could not defeat Viṣṇu, who was alone; therefore, many men, having no arrow and bow do not defeat one who has arrow and bow.

3\. He laughed, "Many gods could not defeat me alone"; from him, laughing in pride, the brilliance ran away; the gods hided that in the plants; they became the Śyāmāka-plants having the name Smayakas (caused by the laughter) (3); therefore, Smayakatva (etymological essence of Smayaka) is of the Smayaka-plants; therefore, a consecrated one should laugh covering his mouth by the hands for holding the brilliance.

[[P214]]

4\. He stood holding firmly his bow on the earth. Those white-ants spoke (to the gods), "We pray for the boon, we will perform this for you; wherever we dig, may we wet that by the waters"; therefore, wherever the white-ants dig, they wet that by the waters (4); for that is chosen by them; they ate even the rope of his (Viṣṇu's) bow; his bow, going forcefully above out and put his head above; that moved following the heaven and the earth; for it moved (Pravartat); hence there is the Pravargyatva (etymological essence) of the 'Pravargya'; for it fell making the noise 'Ghram', hence there is the Gharmatva of the Gharma; the semen (head) of a great one fell, hence there is the Mahāviratva of the Mahāvīra (5); for the gods put it again, hence there is the Samrāṭtva of the Samrāṭ.

5\. The gods took that cut (body) in three ways: the fire took that as Prātaḥ-Savana (pressing of Soma in the morning); Indra as midday Savana; All-gods (Viśvedevas) as third Savana. By that headless sacrifice the sacrificers could not obtain the benedictions, they could not win the heavenly world.

6\. Those gods said to Aśvins, "You are the physicians; put this head of the sacrifice"; they replied, "We pray for the boon; make our share also here", they made this Āśvina-Graha for them; they (Aśvins) put again this head of the sacrifice, which is the Pravargya. By that headful sacrifice the gods obtained the benedictions and won the heavenly world.

[[P215]]

7\. If one begins the Pravargya sacrifice, then he puts the head of the sacrifice; by that headful sacrifice the sacrificer obtains the benedictions and wins the heavenly world; for this cause this Pravargya sacrifice has Aśvins as chief ones.

### II

> *Note:* The importance of the Pravargya-sacrifice. Brāhmaṇa-passages of the Mantras T.A. 4.2. (From 1 to 35).[^P215_r1_35]

[^P215_r1_35]: *(Mantras 1–35)* Brāhmaṇa-passages of the Mantras T.A. 4.2.

1\. One sacrifices the oblation for Savitṛ for impelling.

2\. He sacrifices by taking (ghee etc.) four times; the cattle are four-footed; hence he obtains the cattle; four are the quarters; hence he becomes firm in the quarters.

3\. The metres flew away from the gods, "We will not carry your oblations, having no share in them"; they (the gods) held for them this oblation-taking of the four times for invitatory verse (Puro'nuvākya) and sacrificeable verse (Yājyā) (1), for the goddess and the Vaṣaṭ-making (Haviḥ); which he sacrifices taking (the oblation) four times, by that he pleases the metres; those (metres) being pleased, carry his oblations to the gods.

4\. The speakers of the sacred knowledge discuss whether it is to be sacrificed in the house of a consecrated one or not. The consecrated one is, verily, the oblation; if one sacrifices, then he puts the sacrificer in the fire as the oblation; and if he does not sacrifices (2):—

[[P216]]

5\. Then the definition of the sacrifice is left; (therefore, one should utter only Yajus-Mantra); by this he does not put the sacrificer in the fire as the oblation, nor the definition of the sacrifice is left.

6\. The Gāyatrī thought the other metres crossed by it; the Vaṣaṭ-making (god), reaching there cut the head of it; from that came forth the juice of two kinds; the half of that entered the earth and the other half entered the cattle. Which entered the earth (3), that became the Khaḍira tree (Acacia catechu); which entered the cattle, that became the goat. Which is the Abhri (digging instrument), made of the Khaḍira, that fulfills the head of the sacrifice, indeed, by the juice of the metres.

7\. Which is of the Udumbara (Ficus Glomerata)—vigour, verily, is the Udumbara—that fulfills the head of the sacrifice, indeed, by the vigour; which is of the Veṇu (bamboo)—brilliance, verily, is the Veṇu (4)—by the brilliance, indeed, that fulfills the head of the sacrifice. Which is of the Vikankata tree, by that he obtains the shining.

8\. He takes the Abhri (digging instrument) (by the Mantra:) "By the impelling of the god Savitṛ", for the sake of impelling; he says, "By the arms of Aśvins", for Aśvins are the Adhvaryu-priests of the gods; he says, "By the hands of Pūṣan", for going (-capacity).

9\. This is like the thunderbolt, which is this Abhri; he says, "You are Abhri, you are woman" for the peace (5).

10\. He says, "For the gods, the makers of the Adhvara"; the sacrifice is, verily, the Adhvara, hence he says, verily, "For the gods, the makers of the sacrifice."

[[P217]]

11\. He says, "O Brahmanaspati, stand up"; he goes to obtain the head of the sacrifice by Brahman, indeed.

12\. He says, "May Brahmanaspati come well"; he obtains the head of the sacrifice going first, indeed.

13\. He says, "May the goddess Speech come"; for the sacrifice is, verily, the speech.

14\. He says, "For obtaining the manly Mahāvīra pot, the cooker of the five foods" (6); for the sacrifice is fivefold.

15\. He says, "May the gods carry our sacrifice"; he makes the gods the leaders of the sacrifice.

16\. He says, "May the goddesses heaven and earth assent me"; he fulfills the head of the sacrifice being assented by these.

17\. He says, "May I prosper today, this is the head of the Makha"; the sacrifice is, verily, the Makha; hence he says, verily, "May I prosper today, this is the head of the sacrifice."

18\. He says, "I take you for the sacrifice, I put you on the head of the sacrifice"; he takes that decidedly (7).

19\. He takes thrice; three are these worlds; he fulfills the head of the sacrifice for these worlds, verily. He takes silently at the fourth time; he fulfills the head of the sacrifice by the innumerable (objects).

20\. He takes further from the pit; therefore, the pit is most merciful.

21\. He says, "You were this much before"; he fulfills the head of the sacrifice fully in this earth, indeed.

22\. What is this ant-hill, that which the white-ants take out (8); which is the preparation of the fat of the white-ants; by that he obtains the vigour, the juice of the earth; and also the ear; for this is the ear of the earth, which is the ant-hill.

[[P218]]

23\. He becomes not deaf, who knows thus.

24\. Indra raised the thunderbolt for Vṛtra; wherever he showed bravery (9), therever he (Vṛtra) was not caught; he made effort near the Pūtīkastamba (Caesalpinia Bonducella); he (Vṛtra) was caught. He (Indra) said, "You kept my protection"; that is the Pūtīkatva (etymological essence) of the Pūtīkas (Pūtīkas). What are the Pūtīkas (Pūtīka trees)? They keep the protection of the sacrifice.

25\. He says, "You are born of the fire, the semen of Prajāpati"; he obtains that juice, verily, which entered the cattle (10).

26\. Five are the preparations; for the sacrifice is, verily, fivefold; he fulfills the head of that, whatever is this sacrifice.

27\. If he prepares by the skin of the village-cattle, he troubles the village-cattle; if he prepares by the skin of the black antelope; he troubles, verily, the wild animals; therefore, among the born animals of the same time (11) the wild ones are less; for they have been troubled.

28\. He fulfills in the place of the hairs, hence this (skin of black antelope) is pure for the sacrifice.

29\. They come holding for destroying the Rākṣasas (demons). Many men bring, by that they put honour in that. They put that on the sandy and encircled place for the sake of the peace.

30\. He mixes with the hot waters (12); i.e., he puts brilliance in that.

[[P219]]

31\. He says, "May the honey make you sweet"; i.e., he puts brilliance in that by Brahman.

32\. If he touches by the village-pots, then he troubles the village-pots; he makes that with the Armakapāla (old earth-vessels of the destroyed villages); these are not worth earning, so he troubles only these.

33\. He makes that with the sugars for the firmness and for the propitiation.

34\. He makes that with the hairs of the goat; this is the dear body of the fire; he makes that with its dear body, indeed, and also with the brilliance.

35\. He makes that with the hairs of the skin of the black antelope; the sacrifice is, verily, the skin of the black antelope; he makes the sacrifice, indeed, with the sacrifice.

### III

> *Note:* Preparation of the Mahāvīra-pot has been described in this Anuvāka with the details.

1\. He makes in an encircled place for holding the brilliance of the sacred knowledge.

2\. Making that he should not breathe; if he breathes he troubles the Prāṇas (breaths); he breathes while making that, moving in the side for the protection of his breaths.

3\. One should not go between the Pravargya and the sun; if he goes between them, he may become of bad skin (1); therefore, one should not be between them, for his own protection.

[[P220]]

4\. One should go by the bamboo; the bamboo is, verily, the brilliance; the Pravargya is (also) the brilliance; he increases the brilliance by the brilliance.

5\. He says, "You are the head of the Makha"; the sacrifice is, verily, the Makha; this is the head of that, which is this Pravargya (2); so he says thus.

6\. He says, "You are the feet of the sacrifice"; for these are the feet of the sacrifice; and (he says this) for the firm-standing.

7\. He says, "I perform you by the Gāyatrī-verse"; he performs that by the verses.

8\. He performs that by thrice giving; three are, verily, these worlds; for obtaining these worlds.

9\. He performs by the verses (3); the verses are, verily, the vigour; he performs that by the vigour.

10\. He makes the hole by the Yajus-Mantra for the opening.

11\. He makes this much like the sacrificial mouth of Prajāpati.

12\. He makes this much like the articles of the sacrifice.

13\. He makes this much; this much is the vigour in the person; like the power (he makes) (4).

14\. He makes non-measurable for obtaining the non-measurable.

15\. He makes round (like the neck) for holding.

16\. He says, "Be heated by the heat of the sun"; this is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

17\. He heats by the excrements of the horse; the horse is, verily, related to Prajāpati; (he does so) for equal cause.

[[P221]]

18\. He says, "You are the issue of the sprinkling horse"; that sun is, verily, the sprinkling horse; of him metres are the issues (5); he heats that by the metres.

19\. He says, "For the shining, for the burning"; he puts the shining in it.

20\. He says, "May you be shined by Varuna and gone with Mitra" for the peace.

21\. He says, "I take you for the performance"; this is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

22\. He says, "May the god Savitṛ take you above"; he puts that above, which is caused by Savitṛ, with Brahman and with the gods.

23\. He says, "Fulfill the quarters being not contained on the earth" (6); therefore, the fire shines in all the quarters.

24\. He says, "Stand up, be increased and stand firm above" for the firm-standing.

25\. He is sufficient to become blind, who sees the Pravargya; so he says, "I see you by the eye of the sun" for the protection of the eye.

26\. He says, "I see you for the straight, good and well-residing (places)"; this earth is straight; the mid-region is good and that heaven is well-residing (7); the quarters are the prosperity; he makes these worlds for him; and also (he does so) for the firm-standing.

27\. He says, "This I make firm that the son of that with the subject, cattle and brilliance of the sacred knowledge"; he makes firm with the subject, cattle and brilliance of the sacred knowledge.

[[P222]]

28\. He should say, "With the subject" for the ruling caste; he makes him firm with the subject, indeed; he should say, "With cattle" for the Vaiśya caste; he makes him firm with the cattle, indeed.

29\. The pot of the Asuras is not sprinkled (8); he makes that sprinkled for the gods.

30\. He sprinkles with the milk of the goat; this is the great milk, which is the milk of the goat; he sprinkles that by the great milk.

31\. By the Yajus-Mantra for the peace.

32\. He sprinkles with the metres; this is, verily, done by the metres; he sprinkles the metres with the metres.

33\. He says, "Sprinkle the speech", he obtains the speech; he says, "Sprinkle the vigour", he obtains the vigour; he says, "Sprinkle the oblation", he makes the oblation.

34\. He says, "O god of infront, I hang you above"; this is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

### IV

> *Note:* Use of the Mantras for placing the Mahāvīra. (From 1 to 43).[^P222_r1_43]

[^P222_r1_43]: *(Mantras 1–43)* Use of the Mantras for placing the Mahāvīra.

1\. He says, "O Brahman, we perform the Pravargya; Hotṛ (priest), praise the Gharma"; who is the Hotṛ-priest, he is, verily, now Bṛhaspati; he performs the Pravargya addressing in this way for not being troubled himself.

2\. He says, "I sprinkle you for Yama and for Makha"; these are the gods of this; he increases this by these (gods), indeed.

[[P223]]

3\. He sprinkles by the hot waters; (by this) he puts the brilliance in him (in the sacrificer) (1).

4\. He sprinkles respectively; hence he puts brilliance in him respectively.

5\. He sprinkles thrice; for the sacrifice is threefold (having three Savanas); and (he does so) for the purity.

6\. The Hotṛ-priest repeats the saying for the killing of the Rākṣasas.

7\. He (does so) without taking the breath for the continuation of the life of the sacrificer.

8\. He repeats the metres as the Gāyatrī (though) being (really) Triṣṭups (2); for the Prāṇa-breath is related to the Gāyatrī-metre; hence he puts Prāṇa, indeed, in the sacrificer.

9\. He repeats (the metres) linked together; for the continuation of the Prāṇas (breaths) and the food etc. and also for killing the Rākṣasas.

10\. If he repeats the measured (metres), then he obtains the measured (objects); hence he repeats non-measured (metres) for obtaining non-measured (objects).

11\. This is the head of the sacrifice (3), which is the Pravargya; the Muñja-grasses are the vigour, which is the Veda of the Muñja-grasses; by that one increases the head of the sacrifice as by the vigour.

12\. He sacrifices the oblations for the breaths; hence he puts the Prāṇas in the sacrificer.

13\. He sacrifices seven oblations; seven are, verily, the breaths of the head; hence he puts the breaths in the sacrificer.

14\. He says, "May the god Savitṛ mix you with the honey" (4); he mixes him with the brilliance.

[[P224]]

15\. By "Protect the earth from the heat" he puts the gold for not making the earth much heated.

16\. Which is the Pravargya-sacrifice, that is the head of the sacrifice; the fire is the all-gods; burning the scattered Muñja-grasses he puts (them beneath the Mahāvīra-pot); hence he puts the head of the sacrifice, verily, among the gods.

17\. The fore-parts of the Muñja-grasses should not be scattered; for they are in the place of the Darbha-grasses here.

18\. He says, "You are the ray, you are the shining"; he puts in him the splendour and the brilliance of the sacred knowledge.

19\. He says, "Sit well, you are great", for it is great.

20\. The speakers of the sacred knowledge say that same are the priests as in the Darśapūrṇamāsa-sacrifices, then why the Hotṛ-priest does not utter benedictions for the sacrificer. (In reply they find that) some sacrifices have the benedictions in the beginning and some have the benedictions in the following parts.

21\. What Yajus-mantras he says, "Not injurable in the east"; (by those) the sacrificer obtains the benedictions, indeed, from the head (the Pravargya).

22\. He mentioned life in the east, offspring in the south, breath in the west, ear in the north, holding above; (by these) he puts the breaths in his favour.

23\. He is able to be mad, whom they move in the quarters (5); hence by "You are the pervading of Manu (men), having many sons" he touches the earth; this earth is, verily, the pervading of Manu (men), having many sons; he stands firm in this earth not to be mad.

[[P225]]

24\. He says, "Be well enjoyable for me; do not, do not kill" for non-violence.

25\. He says, "You are collected, you are collected well"; (by this) he puts the worship, indeed, in him.

26\. Which is the Pravargya-sacrifice, that is the head of the sacrifice. That sun is, verily, the Pravargya; Maruts are the rays of that (6). He says, "Hail for Maruts, be spread all over"; (by this) he makes the sun pervade all over with the rays. Therefore, that is pervading in that world with the rays. Therefore, the king is pervading with the subject. Therefore, the chief of the village is pervading with the co-born ones.

27\. For, the shining of the burnt fire reached to the Vikaṅkata tree; so, what are the encirclings of the Vikaṅkata tree, (by those) he obtains the shining.

28\. These (encirclings) are twelve (7); for there are twelve months in a year; he obtains, indeed, the year.

29\. Some say that there is a thirteenth month; which is the thirteenth encircling, by that he obtains the thirteenth months.

30\. He says, "You are the inner holder of the mid-region" for the discrimination.

31\. Saying, "Protect the heaven from the heat" he puts the gold above for not making that heaven much heated.

32\. He says, "O able one, you hold the bow and the arrows" (8); (by this) he praises this.

33\. Saying, "You are related to the Gāyatrī, Triṣṭup and Jagati metres" he takes the fan; he takes these by the metres.

34\. He fans saying, "Honey, honey"; Prāṇa (breath) is, verily, the honey; he puts the Prāṇa in the sacrificer.

[[P226]]

35\. They move thrice round that; for the Prāṇa (breath) is threefold.

36\. They move thrice round that; for the sacrifice is threefold (9); and also (they do so) for the killing of the Rākṣasas (demons).

37\. They move thrice again round that; six movings become performed; six are, verily, the seasons; they stand firmly in the seasons.

38\. He, who attacks the dear body of the Gharma, becomes of bad skin. He attacks the dear body of this who, after making three rounds makes the fourth round. Rajani, the lord of the cruel (priests) attacked this (in this way) (10); then he became of bad skin; therefore, after the third round one should not make the fourth one for his protection.

39\. Prāṇas (breaths) are, verily, the fans.

40\. They fan separately for not making the non-separation of the Prāṇas (breaths).

41\. They fan sitting variously; hence they stand firm in the quarters.

42\. They fan above for obtaining well the heavenly world.

43\. They fan all round; hence this wind blows all round.

### V

1\. He says, "May the fire, with Vasus, make you shine in the east by the Gāyatrī metre"; the fire, indeed, makes him shine in the east with Vasus by the Gāyatrī metre.

> *Note:* Use of the Mantras of the Prapāṭhaka IV.[^P226_r1]

[^P226_r1]: *(Mantras)* Use of the Mantras of the Prapāṭhaka IV.

[[P227]]

2\. He says, "May Indra, with Rudras, make you shine in the south by the Triṣṭup-metre"; Indra, indeed, with Rudras, makes him shine in the south by the Triṣṭup metre. He says, "May you make me shine being shined"; he asks the benediction in this way. He says, "May Varuna, with Ādityas, make you shine in the west by the Jagatī-metre"; Varuṇa, indeed, with Ādityas, makes him shine in the west by the Jagatī-metre (1). He says, "May you make me shine being shined"; he asks the benediction in this way. He says, "May the heavenly Maruts, with Maruts, make you shine in the north by the Anukṣṭup-metre", the heavenly Maruts, with Maruts, indeed, make him shine in the north by the Anustup-metre. He says, "May you make me shine being shined"; he asks the benediction in this way. He says, "May Bṛhaspati, with All-gods (Viśvedevas), make you shine above by the Paṅkti-metre". Bṛhaspati, with All-gods (Viśvedevas), indeed, makes him shine above by the Paṅkti-metre. He says, "May you make me shine being shined"; he asks benediction in this way (2).

3\. He says, "O god, Gharma, you are shining among the gods"; for this is shining in the gods. He says, "May I be shining among the men"; he, indeed, shines among the men.

4\. He says, "You are among the gods emperor, shining, full of life, brilliant and having the splendour of the sacred knowledge"; for this is shining, full of life, brilliant and having the splendour of the sacred knowledge among the gods. He says, "May I become shining, full of life, brilliant and having the splendour of the sacred knowledge among the men"; he becomes, indeed, shining, full of life, brilliant and having the splendour of the sacred knowledge among the men.

[[P228]]

5\. He says, "You are the shining, put the shining in me, (yea) the shining in me"; (by this) he asks this benediction.

6\. If he says, "O Gharma, you are shining" without making him shine by these Yajus-Mantras, then the Adhvaryu becomes not shining and also the sacrificer becomes not shining. If he says, "O Gharma, you are shining" making him shine by these Yajus-Mantras; then the Adhvaryu-priest becomes shining, and also the sacrificer becomes shining (3).

### VI

1\. Which is this Pravargya, that is the head of the sacrifice, the neck are the Upasad (-sacrifices); he begins (puts milk in the hot ghee) the Pravargya before the Upasads; hence he puts the head on the necks.

2\. He puts the milk in the hot ghee thrice; three are these worlds; he obtains the head of the sacrifice from these worlds.

3\. Six become performed; six are, verily, the seasons (1); he obtains the head of the sacrifice from the seasons.

4\. He puts the ghee in the hot milk twelve times; twelve are the months in a year; he obtains the head of the sacrifice from the year.

5\. Twenty-four become performed; twenty-four are the half-months in a year; he obtains the head of the sacrifice from the half-months.

6\. And some say that only once should be poured the milk in the hot ghee; for one is the head (2).

[[P229]]

7\. He performs the Pravargya (pours the milk in the hot ghee) in the Agniṣṭoma-sacrifice. Whatever is the Agniṣṭoma, that is the sacrifice; hence he puts the head of that whatever is the sacrifice.

8\. One should not perform the Pravargya in the Ukthya; progeny and cattle are the Ukthyas; if he performs the Pravargya in the Ukthya, he burns the progeny and cattle of the sacrificer.

9\. He performs the Pravargya in Viśvajit and Sarvapratha sacrifices (3); the Pṛṣṭhas destroy the non-destroyed (sins); by the Pṛṣṭhas, verily, he obtains the fruit of the Pravargya for him destroying the non-destroyed (sins).

10\. He says, "I have seen the protector"; the Prāṇa is, verily, the protector; he spreads the Prāṇa in the progeny.

11\. He says, "I have seen the protector"; that is, verily, the protector; he protects these subjects; he makes that sun protector of the subjects.

12\. He says, "Non-destroyable" (4); for that is not destroyed.

13\. He says, "Moving in the ways infront and backward", for this (sun) moves infront and backward.

14\. He says, "That (protects the subjects), gathered or gone in different places"; for this (sun) protects the subjects, gathered or gone in different places.

15\. He says, "That comes again and again in the worlds"; for this (sun) comes again and again in the worlds.

16\. He says, "You protected here by Madhu and Madhvi; Madhu and Madhuci"; (by this) he makes the two months of the spring for him.

[[P230]]

17\. He says, "The fire has gone with the fire" (5); (by this) he makes the two months of the summer season for him.

18\. He says, "The fire has gone with the fire"; for this fire (Mahāvīra) goes with the fire (sun).

19\. He says, "Hail! The fire has gone with the heat"; it has been said before (about this), by this next he praises.

20\. He says, "You shine as the holder of the heaven, the region, the holder of the earth"; (by this) he makes the two months of the rainy season for him.

21\. He says, "(I see you) for the heart, for the mind", (by this) he makes the two months of the autumn for him (6).

22\. He says, "Give the sacrificings among the gods in the heaven"; by the sacrificings, verily, he holds together these worlds.

23\. He says, "O lord of all the earths"; (by this) he makes the two months of the dewy season for him.

24\. He says, "O Gharma, you are famous in the gods; protect the gods"; (by this) he makes the two months of the winter season for him.

25\. He says, "Grant us the speech, which is born of the austerity, going to the gods"; which is the pure speech, that is born of the austerity; he obtains that, indeed (7).

26\. He says, "The womb of the gods"; for this is the womb of the gods.

27\. He says, "The father of the men"; the subjects are the men; which is the Pravargya, that is the father of them; therefore, he says thus.

28\. He says, "The lord of the subjects"; for this is the lord of the subjects.

[[P231]]

29\. He says, "The wisdom of the poets" (8); for this is the wisdom of the subjects.

30\. He says, "This god has done effort with the god Savitṛ and shined with the sun"; he praises that sun and this Pravargya.

31\. He says, "O Gharma, you are the giver of life and brilliance to us"; he asks this benediction.

32\. He says, "You are our father; impell us as a father"; it impells him, indeed.

33\. Nine are, verily, these Avakāśas (Mantras); the tenth is for the wife; nine are, verily, the Prāṇas (breaths); the navel is the tenth; he puts the breaths in the sacrificer; and the Virāṭ-Mantra is of ten syllables; the food is the Virāṭ; he obtains the food etc. by the Virāṭ, indeed.

34\. The head of the sacrifice was cut; the gods put that by the sacrificings; the priests see; this is, verily, the sacrificings; by the sacrificings, verily, he puts the head of the sacrifice (9).

35\. They see shiningly; for Prajāpati created the offsprings from the shining; (he does so) for the creation of the offsprings.

36\. They see shiningly; for Parjanya (rain-god) rains by the shining; (by doing so) Parjanya becomes desirous of raining and the offsprings increase.

37\. They see shiningly, for the shining is the brilliance of the sacred knowledge; they become full of the brilliance of the sacred knowledge (10).

38\. They see readingly; they go to the whole span of the life.

[[P232]]

39\. The wife should not see; if she sees, she gives birth and (the Mahāvīra) burns that born one; if she does not see, she does not give birth and (the Mahāvīra) does not kill the born one; hence he says the Yajus-Mantras covering her (by anything); she gives birth and (the Mahāvīra) does not kill the born one of her.

40\. One says, "May I make sexual intercourse full of the form-making god"; by the intercourse, verily, the offsprings are born.

### VII

1\. Saying, "by the impelling of the god Savitr I take you" he takes the girdle for the impelling. He says, "by the arms of Asvinas; for Asvinas are the Adhvaryu-priests of the gods." He says, "by the hands of Pusan" for the going (-capacity).

2\. He says, "I take you, you are the girdle of Aditi" for making the use of the Yajus-Mantras.

3\. He says, "O Ida, come; O Aditi, come; O Sarasvati, come"; these are, verily, her divine names; by the divine names he calls her.

4\. He says, "O that... come; O that... come; O that... come"; these are the names of this given by the men (1); he calls her by the names given by the men.

5\. Six are performed; six are, verily, the seasons; he calls her by the seasons.

6\. He says, "you are the turban for Aditi"; this is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

[[P233]]

7\. He says, "you, the son of Ida, are the wind"; for the calf is related to the god wind.

8\. He says, "May Pusan release you"; for the cattle are related to the god Pusan (2); he makes them released by their own god.

9\. He says, "for Asvinas, cause to swell milk"; Asvinas are, verily, the physicians of the gods; he makes them, indeed, physician for him.

10\. He says, "which is your sleeping udder", (by this) he praises that (cow).

11\. He says, "O calf, leave the Gharma, protect the Charma, leave for the Gharma"; this is similar to the saying, "give to him".

12\. He says, "May Brhaspati go to you" (3); Brhaspati is, verily, Brahman of the gods; he goes to her (cow) with Brahman, indeed.

13\. He says, "you are donators, you are worth sucking"; (by this) he makes them sacrificeable.

14\. He says, "separated by the blood" for the discrimination.

15\. He says, "cause to swell milk for Asvinas, cause to swell milk for Sarasvati, cause to swell milk for Pusan; cause to swell milk for Brhaspati"; he causes, verily, to swell these gods.

16\. He says, "cause to swell for Indra, cause to swell for Indra"; (by this) he increases Indra by his share.

17\. He says twice for Indra (4); hence Indra is the great share-holder among the gods.

[[P234]]

18\. Saying, "you are of Gayatri-metre, of Tristup-metre, of Jagati-metre" he takes the Sapha and Upayama woods; he takes these, indeed, by the metres.

19\. He says, "come to me with the part of the vigour"; he makes, indeed, his share of the vigour.

20\. Asvinas had said, verily, while putting the head of the sacrifice, "the Vasat (Hail) should be made for us first"; hence he says, "O Indra and Asvinas, drink this sweet honey"; he makes, indeed, Vasat (Hail) for Asvinas first and increases Asvinas by the share (5).

21\. He says, "O Vasus, sacrifice, Vāṭ (Hail!)"; he increases Vasus by the share.

22\. If he makes Vasat (Hail), his Vasat may become without essence; if he does not make Vasat, then the Rakṣasas may destroy the sacrifice; so he says Vāṭ; i.e., he makes Vasat imperceptibly; hence his Vasat does not become without essence and nor the Rakṣasas destroy the sacrifice (6).

23\. He says, "Hail! I sacrifice you for the ray of the sun that causes the rain"; which is his pious ray, that is the causer of the rain; he sacrifices this for that.

24\. He says, "you are the sweet oblation", (by this) he makes that sweet.

25\. He says, "heat even the heat of the sun"; this is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

26\. He says, "I take by the heaven and earth"; he takes that, indeed, by the heaven and the earth (7).

27\. He says, "I raise you by the mid-region"; he raises, indeed, by the mid-region; no man is able to hold that.

[[P235]]

28\. He says, "may I become able to hold you being assented by the gods and the fathers"; he holds, indeed, being assented by the gods and the fathers.

29\. He makes that improsperous who performs the Pravargya in the west and offers the oblations in the east; hence he says, "you are brilliance, follow the brilliance"; he puts brilliance in this.

30\. He says, for non-violence, "O touching the heaven do not kill that; touching the mid-region do not kill that; touching the earth do not kill that" (8).

31\. He says, "you are the paradise, grant me the paradise, grant me the heaven, protect me from the heaven"; (by this) he asks the benediction.

32\. Which is the Pravargya, that is the head of the sacrifice; the wind is the self; he says taking the names of the wind; hence he puts in himself the head of the sacrifice.

33\. He utters without taking breath for the continuation of the breath.

34\. He says five (Mantras) (9); the sacrifice is, verily, fivefold; whatever is the sacrifice, he puts the head of that.

35\. He says, "Hail for you, O fire, accompanied with Vasus!" The sun is, verily, the fire, accompanied with Vasus; for him he sacrifices this.

36\. He says, "Hail for you, Soma, accompanied with Rudras"; the moon is, verily, Soma, accompanied with Rudras; for him he sacrifices this. He says, "Hail for you, Varuna, accompanied with Adityas" (10); in the waters, verily, Varuna is accompanied with Adityas; he sacrifices for him by this. He says, "Hail for you, Brhaspati, accompanied with Visvedevas"; Brhaspati is, verily, Brahman of the gods; he sacrifices for Brahman by this.

[[P236]]

37\. He says, "Hail for you, Savitr, accompanied with Rbhus, pervading one and lord"; the year is, verily, Savitr, accompanied with Rbhus, pervading one and lord; he sacrifices this for him.

38\. He says, "Hail for you, Yama, accompanied with Angirases and fathers"; the breath, verily, is Yama, accompanied with Angirases and fathers (11); he sacrifices this for him.

39\. For these gods he sacrifices this.

40\. Ten become performed; Virat-Mantra is of ten syllables; the food is the Virat; by the Virat he obtains the food etc.

41\. The gods went to the heavenly world by the sacrificed Purodas-oblations, that is the Rauhinsîva (etymological sense, ascending) of the Rauhinas. What are the Rauhinas (the sacrificed Purodasas); by those the sacrificer goes to the heavenly world.

42\. He says, "may the light of the day be connected with the impelling (of the sun); the good light of the lights; Hail! may the light of the night be connected with the impelling (of the moon); the good light of the lights; Hail!" By this he holds that sun above by the day and below by the night; therefore, that sun has been held in that heaven by the days and the nights (12).

[[P237]]

### VIII

1\. He says, "All the good-wishes are (in Brahman), sitting in the south"; (by this) he pleases all the gods; and also protects him from all the faults.

2\. He says, "he worshipped all the gods here"; he increases all the gods by the shares.

3\. He says, "O Asvinas, drink this sweet Gharma, of which Hail has been done"; he increases Asvinas by the share.

4\. He says, "Hail for the fire, the sacrificeable one! May there propitiation be by the Yajus-Mantra"; (by this) he mingles that indeed, and makes that oblation, indeed (1).

5\. He says, "O Asvinas, drink this sweet Gharma (milk mingled with ghee), which is good to the heart; (protect us) by your shining protections (in this sacrificing) day"; (by this) he increases Asvinas by the share.

6\. He says, "May the heaven and the earth assent you" for the assent.

7\. He says, "Hail for Indra, Vat (Hail)"; for it is sacrificed first for Indra.

8\. After calling he says, "make sacrifice of the Gharma"; he sacrifices making Vasat (Hail) for killing the Rakṣasas (demons).

9\. He says, "Asvinas drank the Gharma" (2); about this it has been said before; by the next one he praises.

10\. He said, "the heaven and the earth assented you" for the assent.

11\. He says, "that thing is as Vat (Hail); salutation to the heaven, salutation to the earth"; this is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

[[P238]]

12\. He says, "put this sacrifice in the heaven, (yea) in the heaven put this sacrifice"; he makes him go to the heavenly world.

13\. He says, "go to the heaven, go to the mid-region, go to the earth"; he makes him firm in these worlds, indeed.

14\. He says, "go to the five quarters" (3); (by this) he makes him firm in the quarters. He says, "Go to the gods, drinkers of the Gharma, go to the fathers, drinkers of the Gharma"; he makes him firm in both worlds, indeed.

15\. Which increases, (by that) Parjanya (rain-god) becomes desirous to rain; therefore, increasing one is pious.

16\. Which increases in the east, that is of the gods; which increases in the south, that is of the fathers (4); which increases in the west, that is of the men; which increases in the north, that is of Rudras.

17\. He increases between the east and the north; (by that) he makes (this sacrifice) for the gods.

18\. He increases in all the quarters and sub-quarters; all the quarters become increased.

19\. He increases within the encircling (5) for not falling of the brilliance.

20\. He says, "be increased for the food, be increased for the vigour"; (by this) he puts the food and the vigour in the sacrificer.

21\. He says, "be increased for the sacrificer"; he asks this benediction for the sacrificer.

22\. He says, "be increased for my greatness"; he asks this benediction for himself.

[[P239]]

23\. He says, "(I sacrifice) you for the brilliance, for the shining, for the senses, for the prosperity", this is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

24\. He says, "you are the holder, may you become my good holder; put the brilliance of the sacred knowledge in me" (6); he puts Brahman in him, indeed.

25\. He says, "may the wind not move you".

26\. If he wishes to bewitch, he should say for him, whom he hates, "I seat you in his breath; go useless with him"; whomever he hates, makes him go useless with this.

27\. He says, "Hail for Pusan, the destroyer of the enemies", whatever are the gods share-holders of the sacrifice; he sacrifices this for them.

28\. He says, "Hail for the stones"; whatever are the speeches in the mid-region, by those he sacrifices this.

29\. He says, "Hail for the promoting ones"; the breaths are, verily, the promoting gods; he sacrifices this for them.

30\. He says, "Hail for the heaven and the earth", he sacrifices this for the heaven and the earth.

31\. He says, "Hail for the fathers, the drinkers of the Gharma"; whoever are the sacrificers, they are the fathers, the drinkers of the Gharma; he sacrifices this for them (8).

32\. He says, "Hail for Rudra, the Chief Rudra"; he increases Rudra, indeed, by the share.

33\. He anoints all round; he makes away cruel Rudra from all round.

34\. He makes away (Rudra) in the north; this is the quarter of Rudra; he makes away Rudra in his own quarter.

35\. He touches the water for the purity.

[[P240]]

36\. He does not see; if he sees (9), his eye may be destroyed; therefore, he should not see.

37\. He says, "you have fulfilled (my desires); protect me from the day and the night; this is, O fire, your fuel; be increased by that; give me life; make me full of brilliance"; (by this) he puts in him, indeed, life and brilliance. He says, "you have fulfilled (my desires); protect me from the night and the day; this is, O fire, your fuel; be increased by that; give me life; make me full of brilliance"; (by this) he puts in him, indeed, life and brilliance.

38\. He says, "the fire is the light; the light is the fire, Hail! The sun is the light; the light is the sun, Hail!" This is according to the Yajus-Mantra.

39\. The speakers of the sacred knowledge discuss whether Agnihotra should be performed or not (10); if one sacrifices by the Yajus-Mantra, then he sacrifices unlike the past; and if he does not sacrifice, then the fire may become defeated; by "Bhuh, Svaha" he should sacrifice; hence he sacrifices like the past and the fire is also not defeated.

40\. He says, "the oblation, the sweet oblation has been sacrificed"; (by this) he makes that sweet.

41\. He says, "in the most lordly fire"; the breath is, verily, the most lordly fire; he sacrifices this in the breath, the most lordly fire.

42\. He says, "you are my father, do not, do not kill" for the non-violence.

43\. He says, "O good Gharma, I have seen your (essence, the essence of one) full of honey, full of food, full of corn"; (by this) he asks this benediction.

[[P241]]

44\. He says, "may we enjoy you full of Svadha (food); do not, do not kill" for the non-violence.

45\. They become without brilliance, who sacrifice by the Pravargya; they enjoy; (by that) they put the brilliance in themselves (12).

46\. One should not eat meat for one year, he should not go to the woman, he should not drink by the earthly vessels, he should not drink his left water; (in this way) he sharpens the brilliance.

47\. The gods and the Asuras (demons) were equipped for the battle; the gods, wishing the victory, put Brahman in Vibhrat (shining), the son of the sun; he protected all that is readable in the day by this consecration; therefore, this consecration should be performed; for the protection of the brilliance; therefore, these Yajus-Mantras are of Vibhrat, the son of the sun.

48\. By "Hail for you, the rays of the sun" he seats together (fills) in the morning and by "Hail for you, the stars" he (does so) in the evening. These are the gods of these; by those he increases this (13).

### IX

1\. By "O Gharma, what is your sorrow in the heaven" he sacrifices oblations thrice; by the metres, verily, he destroys the sorrow from these worlds.

2\. He sacrifices first holding up to this (face), then up to this (navel) and then up to this (knee); three are, verily, these worlds; from these worlds he makes the sorrow away.

[[P242]]

3\. He says, "the assent for us today" for obtaining the assent.

4\. He says, "to you, the protector of the heaven"; by the heaven, indeed, these worlds are held.

5\. He says, "to you, the protector of Brahman" (1); in these worlds, indeed, the subjects are held.

6\. He says, "to you, the protector of the breath"; the breaths are held, indeed, in the subjects.

7\. Which is this Pravargya, that is the head of the sacrifice; that sun, verily, is the Pravargya; if one makes (Pravargya) in the south, west and north, then the head of the sacrifice becomes destroyed wickedly; hence he puts that in the east; he puts the head of the sacrifice in the east; he puts in the east, hence this sun rises in the east.

8\. He brings the Sapha and Upayama woods, fans and the Dhrstia (fire-taking objects); by this he makes the (Pravargya) accompanied with the body, which is accompanied with the soul.

9\. He, who knows thus, becomes accompanied with the soul in that world.

10\. Those are made of the Udumbara wood; Udumbara is, verily, the vigour; he obtains the vigour, indeed.

11\. Following, verily, the way (3). The Rakṣasas (demons) destroy the sacrifice; hence the Prastotr-priest follows with the Samans; for the Saman is the destroyer of the Rakṣasas; (he does so) for destroying the Rakṣasas.

12\. He reads the Nidhana (the last part of the fivefold Saman) thrice; three are, verily, these worlds; for these worlds, indeed, he destroys the Rakṣasas.

[[P243]]

13\. Every person repeats the Nidhana (Saman); for every person is the protector; (this is) for destroying the Rakṣasas (4).

14\. If he dismisses on the earth, he makes the earth sorrowful; if in the waters, he makes the waters sorrowful; if in the plants, he makes the plants sorrowful; if in the vegetables, he makes the vegetables sorrowful; hence he dismisses putting the gold; for the gold is nectar (5); he makes that firm in the nectar, indeed.

15\. Saying "you are the beauty of the propitiation" he makes a round sprinkling; for the fire is, verily, threefold; whatever is the fire, of that he destroys the sorrow.

16\. He makes round thrice again; six become performed; of this by six are, verily, the seasons; he destroys the sorrow of the seasons, indeed.

17\. He says, "the quadrangular (altar) is the navel of Rta (Settled Order)" (6); this earth is, verily, the Rta; of that earth this Pravargya is, verily, the navel; therefore, he says thus.

18\. He says, "you are the seat, full of all life"; for this earth is the seat.

19\. He says, "we have put away the haters and wicked ones" for destroying the enemies.

20\. Saying "O Gharma, this is your food, this is your fulfilling" he fills with the curd mingled with honey; the food etc. and curd are, verily, the vigour; he increases that by the vigour and the food etc. (7).

21\. He, who knows this, becomes satisfied.

22\. He says, "you are the divine Gandharva named Ranti"; by this form of the Mantra he clears the greatness and the good relation of the Pravargya.

[[P244]]

23\. He says, "May I be connected with life, with breath"; he asks this benediction, indeed.

24\. He says, "he has been destroyed who hates us and whom we hate"; this is, indeed, the bewitching of this.

25\. He says, "the sprinkling and destroying one has shouted"; for this is the sprinkling one (8), sprinkling and destroying one.

26\. He says, "the observable like Mitra", by this he praises this.

27\. He says, "you are the knower, the cause of the ocean"; he makes that go to its cause, indeed.

28\. He says, "may salutation be to you; do not, do not kill" for the non-violence.

29\. He says, "to Visvavasu, the Soma-stealing Gandharva"; whatever of this sacrifice is left, that becomes fulfilled by this.

30\. He says, "may Visvavasu tell us that all" (9); it has been said before; by the next one he praises.

31\. He says, "impelling our wisdoms may he protect our wisdoms"; by this he makes the seasons for him.

32\. He says, "the Gandharva said about the nectar of these"; the breaths are, verily, the nectars; he makes breaths, indeed, for him.

33\. He says, "O god Gharma, this you obtained the gods"; for he goes to the gods being the god.

34\. He says, "this I obtained the men" (10); for this goes to the men being man.

35\. While dismissing the Pravargya he is able to dismiss the offspring, cattle, drinking of Soma; hence he says, "may Soma-drinking come to me with the offspring, wealth and nourishing"; (by this) he puts in himself the offspring, the cattle and the drinking of Soma.

[[P245]]

36\. He says, "may the waters and the plants be good friends to us"; he asks, indeed, this benediction.

37\. He says, "may they become bad friends to those who hate me and whom we hate"; this is, verily, the bewitching of this.

38\.
