He, who dismisses the Pravargya, he falls from the heavenly world; hence after coming he sacrifices again in the household fire by "above, that wonderful", the Rcst related to the sun; the household fire is, verily, this world; he becomes firm in this world; that sun is, verily, the heavenly world; what are the Rcs of the sun, by that he goes to the heavenly world (11).

### X

1\. The gods milked from Prajapati, (the cow) this white milk (Pravargya-sacrifice). That was not various (enough) for them. The fire made that various. They became the white Samans; what drops fell from them they became the white Yajuses; these are, verily, of the white Samans. Of these two, one is the milk of the Samans and the next is dear to the gods. Which is the milk of the cow (1), that is the milk of the Samans. Which is the milk of the goat, that is the milk of the gods. Therefore, where they perform by the Yajuses, there they perform by the milk. In this way they increase Prajapati and gods by the milk and food etc.

2\. He, verily, enjoys (the fruits of) Pravargya perceptibly, who knowing thus pours the milk in the hot ghee (i.e., performs the Pravargya sacrifice).

[[P246]]

3\. One should dismiss in the Uttaravedi (northern altar) for him who is desirous of the brilliance; for the Uttaravedi is, verily, the brilliance (2); Pravargya is (also) the brilliance; hence he increases the brilliance by the brilliance.

4\. One should dismiss in the Uttaravedi for him, who is desirous of the food; for the Pravargya is, verily, the head of the sacrifice; the Uttaravedi is the mouth; he puts the mouth together for food etc.; he becomes, indeed, the enjoyer of the food.

5\. When around the dismissed (articles of the Pravargya sacrifice) the birds sit, in that year the birds sit around (the corpses of) the offsprings (3); therefore, he should dismiss only in the Uttaravedi.

6\. One should dismiss in the east or in the west (of the Uttaravedi); for this light rises from the east and that sits in the west; hence he dismisses that in its own cause.

7\. He should dismiss that in the waters; for this light rose from the midst of the waters; the Pravargya is also light; he puts that firm in its own light (4).

8\. One should dismiss in that quarter, in which he lives, that is the fire Vaisvanara (related to all men), whom he hates; which is this Pravargya; by the fire Vaisvanara he moves (burns) him.

9\. One should dismiss in the branch of the Udumbara tree; the Udumbara is, verily, the vigour; the food is the breath; the Gharma is the burning (5). He says, "this I burn even the breath of that, the son of that"; by the burning he burns even the breath of him; he obtains sorrow at once.

10\. One should dismiss in a place where the Darbha-grasses are surrounded by the continuous white-ants for him who is desirous of the rain. These Darbha-grasses are, verily, the unbroken flow of the waters; that sun showers the rain from above (if one dismisses) here. That sun, verily, gives the rain for him; those waters go by the settled lands (6).

[[P247]]

### XI

1\. When this (Pravargya) is being fulfilled, then this is Prajapati; when this is fulfilled, then this is Samrat; when milk is poured in the hot water, then this is Gharma; when this is dismissed, then this is Mahavira; which is the Pravargya, that is the sun; he made these names.

2\. Who knows thus, people know his name.

3\. The speakers of the sacred knowledge discuss (1); who calls by the real name of a prosperous one, he wishes the trouble of his good deeds. They wish the trouble of his good deeds, who know thus. Therefore, a learned one should call this 'Gharma' in the day and 'Samrat' in the night; these are the dear bodies of this; these are the dear names of this; by the dear body, indeed, and by the dear name he increases this; his vast fame goes before him in the man.

4\. The Gayatri (-metre) ran away from the gods; by the Pravargya, indeed, the gods became able to search it; by the Pravargya they obtained it. He, who performs the Pravargya twenty-four times, becomes able to follow the Gayatri, obtains the Gayatri, while going to the gathering of men, his fame goes before him.

5\. While sitting well this (Pravargya) is called Valavadeva (2); while the milk is poured in the hot ghee, then this is Vasus; this is Soma while being scattered; this is Asvinas while milk is carried; this is Maruta being boiled; this is Pausna when it goes above (while boiling); this is Sarasvata when it goes above the pot; this is Maitra, when it is taken; this is Teja and Vayu while being lifted; this is Prajapati, while this is being brought; being sacrificed and after the sacrificing this is Vac (3). Which is the Pravargya, that is, verily, that sun; he made these names.

[[P248]]

6\. Who knows thus, people know his name.

7\. The speakers of the sacred knowledge discuss: why this is eaten when the earthly oblation is not eaten; in reply to this one should say that this is Vac (speech); hence he puts the speech in the speech (4) and therefore, one eats it.

8\. Which is the Pravargya, that is, verily, twelvefold Prajapati; what Mantras are before the Avakasa-Mantras, by those he created the offsprings; by the Avakasa-Mantras he created the gods and the Asuras (demons); what Mantras are after the Avakasas, by them he created the food; this is Prajapati; this (Pravargya) is, verily, Prajapati (5).

### XII

1\. This (Pravargya) is performed being Savitr in the first day; by that this obtains the desires. When in the second day this is performed, then this goes to the gods being fire. When this is performed in the third day, then this goes to the breaths being the wind. When this is performed in the fourth day, then this goes to the rays being sun. When this is performed in the fifth day, then this goes to the stars being moon (1). When this is performed in the sixth day, then this goes to the year being season. When this is performed in the seventh day, then this goes to the Sakvari hymn being disposer. When this is performed in the eighth day, then this goes to the Gayatri (-metre) being Brhaspati. When this is performed in the ninth day, then this goes to these threefold worlds being Mitra. When this is performed in the tenth day, then this goes to the Virat (-metre) being Varuna (2). When this is performed in the eleventh day, then this goes to the Tristup (-metre) being Indra. When this is performed in the twelfth day, then this goes to the pressing of Soma being Soma.

[[P249]]

2\. For, this is performed before the Upasad (-sacrifices), hence this goes above from here heating those worlds. For, this is performed after the Upasads, hence this goes from there heating those worlds.

3\. He, who knows thus, shines, indeed.

Auspicious to us... may that not leave us.

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

[[P250]]

## VI

[[P251]]

### I

Harih Om.

I sprinkle well offspring, life and wealth for you by the Yajus-Mantra.[^P251_intro]

[^P251_intro]: Mantra for offering Homa to the household fire after the death of someone.

Om Peace !

Peace !

Peace !

1\. Make pleased by oblation Yama, the king, the son of the sun, the going place of the men, discriminating the good men and observing the paths of many.[^P251_1]

[^P251_1]: Mantra for offering Homa to the household fire after the death of someone.

2\. (O dead one), this cloth has come for you at first.[^P251_2]

[^P251_2]: Mantra for clothing and putting the dead one on the cart.

3\. Put off that cloth which you wore before; remember what sacrificial rite you have stored up and the Dakṣina (sacrificial fee), which you have given to your brothers.[^P251_3]

[^P251_3]: Mantra for clothing and putting the dead one on the cart.

4\. I yoke these fires (bulls) to carry your body (as) the breath, by whom you may go to the place of Yama or the place of the sacred ones.[^P251_4]

[^P251_4]: Mantra for clothing and putting the dead one on the cart.

5\. May Pusan, the knower, one whose cattle are not destroyed, the protector drive you from here; may he as fire give you to the fathers, to the gods, who know well your deeds.[^P251_5]

[^P251_5]: Mantra addressing the dead body.

6\. Pusan knows well all the quarters; may he carry us by the unfierce way; may he, the welfare-granter, not heated, fully brave, not swerving come to us (1).[^P251_6]

[^P251_6]: Mantra addressing the dead body.

> *Note:* Pitṛmedha or the rites for the fathers. Cremationery rites.

[[P252]]

7\. May the moving and all-pervading Pusan protect you; may he protect you in the good path going forward; may Savitr put you there where the sacred ones live and where they have gone.[^P252_7]

[^P252_7]: Mantra addressing the dead body.

8\. Lord of the world, this Rajagavi (is) the oblation (for you).[^P252_8]

[^P252_8]: Mantras for the Rajagavi.

9\. For wealthy fire, all (is oblation)![^P252_9]

[^P252_9]: Mantras for the Rajagavi.

10\. (O Rajagavi), going with the (dead) man, we purify ourselves removing the sins so that there may not come any other (sin) before the end of the span of the life.[^P252_10]

[^P252_10]: Mantras for the Rajagavi.

11\. O dear (Rajagavi), do not think (that you are killed), for you will go to the world of the fathers by the way of the sky, being a goddess, worth choosing by all; covering our both worlds make them full of milk (2).[^P252_11]

[^P252_11]: Mantras for the Rajagavi.

12\. (O dead one), this woman (your wife), choosing the world of her husband, observing all the ancient rules, comes to your put offspring and wealth here for her.[^P252_12]

[^P252_12]: Mantras for the wife of the dead one.

13\. O woman, stand up and come in the world of the living ones, you sleep with this dead one; become the wife of him who holds your hand and is willing to marry you.[^P252_13]

[^P252_13]: Mantras for the wife of the dead one.

14\. Taking the gold from the hand of the dead one live here, verily, for prosperity, sacred knowledge, brilliance and vigour; and may we also, having comfort, win all the competing enemies.[^P252_14]

[^P252_14]: Mantras for the wife of the dead one.

15\. Taking the bow from the hand of the dead one live here, verily, for prosperity, dominion, vitality and vigour; may we also, having comfort, win all the competing enemies. Taking the gem from the hand of the dead one live here, verily, for prosperity, subjects, nourishing and vigour; may we also, having comfort, win all the competing enemies (3).[^P252_15]

[^P252_15]: Mantras for the wife of the dead one.

[[P253]]

16\. O fire, do not destroy this ladle, which is dear to the gods and the performers of the Soma rites; may the immortal gods make us happy by drinking in the ladle.[^P253_16]

[^P253_16]: Address to the fire while putting the sacrificial vessel, different parts of the slaughtered cow, skin, fire on the dead one and burning that.

17\. O Cuirass, protect carefully this body from the heat of the fire; enclose it by your thick fat and marrow; holding this impudent fire, desirous of seeing and consuming it by its (fire's) vigour, do not allow him to go astray.[^P253_17]

[^P253_17]: Address to the fire while putting different parts of the slaughtered cow.

18\. O fire, do not burn this; do not give him pain; do not scatter his skin or body; O fire (Jatavedas), when you burned fairly, convey him (the spirit) to the fathers.[^P253_18]

[^P253_18]: Address to the fire while putting skin on the dead one.

19\. O fire (Jatavedas), when you burned him fairly, give the spirit to the fathers; when he obtains the carrying of the breath (by the fire) then he becomes one under the wish of the gods.[^P253_19]

[^P253_19]: Address to the fire while putting fire on the dead one.

20\. May your eye go to the sun, the soul to the wind; go to the heaven, to the earth by your good deeds or go to the waters if there is comfort for you, and live in the plants by the bodies.[^P253_20]

[^P253_20]: Address to the fire while burning the dead one.

21\. (This) goat (is your) share; heat that by your austerity: may your flame and light heat that; and what are his auspicious bodies, take him by those to the worlds of the sacred ones.[^P253_21]

[^P253_21]: Address to the fire while burning the dead one.

22\. This (dead one) is you (fire); you are on him (burning in) this (way); you are the seat of him as fire (Vaisanara), the son gives always the world for the father; O fire (Jatavedas), take him to the worlds of the sacred ones (4).[^P253_22]

[^P253_22]: Address to the fire while burning the dead one.

[[P254]]

### II

1\. Hail for them who are the preservers of his way : Hail for them who are the protectors of his way! Hail for them who are all-round protectors of him! Hail for the maker of the same! Hail for the abuser! Hail for him who says his fame to the gods! Hail for him who says his bad deeds to the gods! Hail for the fire! Hail for the acting one! Hail for him whom we are not reading here![^P254_1]

[^P254_1]: Address to fire for taking the dead body in the heaven.

2\. (O fire), who steals your fuel being perspirated or who heats his head; from them all, the sinners or the checkers of the heaven, this (dead one) is to be protected.[^P254_2]

[^P254_2]: Address to fire for taking the dead body in the heaven.

3\. You are born of him; may he be born of you again. Hail Vaisanara and the heavenly world (1) for the fire.[^P254_3]

[^P254_3]: Address to fire for taking the dead body in the heaven.

### III

1\. This Fire is burning with great flames; he sounds all the worlds being born as a bull; he pervaded my (sacrificer) from the near-place of the heaven; he has increased at the place of the waters as a great one.[^P254_4]

[^P254_4]: Address to fire for taking the dead body in the heaven.

2\. This is your one (body) and that is the second (being burnt); obtain the third, full of light; be increased in the supreme place of the gods where they live together, becoming full of the body and dear to the gods.[^P254_5]

[^P254_5]: Address to fire for taking the dead body in the heaven.

> *Note:* Homa to be performed after the cremation of the corpse sacrificing nine oblations. Ceremonies after the Homa.

[[P255]]

3\. (O fire! the sacrificers) make you famous, wishing by their heart, in the form of the divine bird flying in the heaven, having golden feathers, the envoy of Varuna and a nourishing bird in the seat of Yama.[^P255_3]

[^P255_3]: Address to fire for taking the dead body in the heaven.

4\. Fly away by the good path leaving the two dogs of Yama, who are born of Sarama and who have four eyes, who are of various colours; and obtain the fathers who live with Yama and who are well-knowers.[^P255_4]

[^P255_4]: Address to fire for taking the dead body in the heaven.

5\. O king Yama, who are your two dogs, having four eyes, protectors of the ways, famous in men; by those give this (dead one) welfare and grant absence of disease (1).[^P255_5]

[^P255_5]: Addresses to Yama and his two envoys, the two four-eyed dogs to take care of the dead one.

6\. Envoys of Yama, moving everywhere around the fettered men, having long noses and being pleased by the breaths of the men, having great power; grant us auspicious breath again for seeing the sun.[^P255_6]

[^P255_6]: Addresses to Yama and his two envoys, the two four-eyed dogs to take care of the dead one.

7\. Soma is purified by some and some worship the ghee, (for these gods and) for those, for whom honey flows; may this (dead one) go to them, verily. May this go to those, verily, who leave their bodies fighting in the battles or who have given thousand Dakṣinas (sacrificial fees).[^P255_7]

[^P255_7]: Prayers for the attainment of the good region by the dead one.

8\. [Omitted/Merged in flow]

9\. May this go to them who are not injurable on account of their austerity, who have gone to heaven by their austerity and who have performed great austerity.[^P255_9]

[^P255_9]: Prayers for the attainment of the good region by the dead one.

[[P256]]

10\. Friends, stand up, enter and swim the (three pits) having stones and wealth (waters); we leave all the troubles here; and may we pass to the auspicious vigours (2).[^P256_10]

[^P256_10]: Addresses to relatives to take bath in the three pits dug in the north of the cremation and filled with the stones and waters brought in odd number of jars.

11\. What is, verily, thousand-streamed, purifying (ray) of the divine Savitr, spread in the mid-region, by which he purified Indra making him trouble-less from the trouble; I purify all limbs of myself by that.[^P256_11]

[^P256_11]: Mantra for passing through two Palasa sticks planted over the pits.

12\. May those branches, being purified by the wind of the disposer (sun) make us full with offspring, wealth and brilliance; which are being plucked out by the relative ones of the dead one, desirous of a king (master) from this obtained nation.[^P256_12]

[^P256_12]: Mantra for plucking out the sticks.

13\. May we go to the supreme light seeing the light above the darkness and the better light of the divine sun.[^P256_13]

[^P256_13]: Mantra addressing the sun.

14\. May Dhatr (Disposer) and Savitr (Impeller) purify by the splendour of the fire and light of the sun (3).[^P256_14]

[^P256_14]: Mantras for taking bath not seeing each other.

> *Note:* Ceremonies after cremation.

[[P257]]

### IV

1\. We now blow out that fire by milk and water, which we burnt for cooking you as (cooking) a bull.[^P257_1]

[^P257_1]: Mantras for blowing out the fire and striking on the burnt bones on the third, fifth or seventh day.

2\. O fire, make him away, whom you have burnt; may here grow by some water any plant, having little grass and many branches.[^P257_2]

[^P257_2]: Mantras for blowing out the fire and striking on the burnt bones on the third, fifth or seventh day.

3\. O earth, sprinkled by the water; and O earth, sprinkled by the milk: take this to the place of the water where there are the frogs; make this fire blown out well.[^P257_3]

[^P257_3]: Mantras for blowing out the fire and striking on the burnt bones on the third, fifth or seventh day.

4\. May the water of the deserts be auspicious for you; may the waters of the wonderful countries be auspicious for you; may the waters of the oceans be auspicious for you; may the waters of the rain be auspicious for you.[^P257_4]

[^P257_4]: Mantras for blowing out the fire and striking on the burnt bones on the third, fifth or seventh day.

5\. May the flowing waters be auspicious for your body, may the waters of the wells be auspicious; may the snow fall suspiciously on you; may dew fall suspiciously (1).[^P257_5]

[^P257_5]: Mantras for blowing out the fire and striking on the burnt bones on the third, fifth or seventh day.

6\. O fire, impel him again for going to the fathers by Hails, who moves still even being burnt; enjoying his rest life there may he obtain his body.[^P257_6]

[^P257_6]: Mantras for offering three oblations after throwing the cinders in the south.

7\. Go with the feathers, with Hails, with stored up sacrifices in the highest heaven; which earth you choose to be born, go there; may the god Savitr put you there.[^P257_7]

[^P257_7]: Mantras for offering three oblations after throwing the cinders in the south.

8\. Whatever the black bird has harmed you or an ant, snake or jackal etc. has harmed; may that fire and soma, which entered the Brahmana make you debtless from that all.[^P257_8]

[^P257_8]: Mantras for offering three oblations after throwing the cinders in the south.

9\. (O dead one!) stand up, obtain the body, do not leave here any limb or body; which earth you choose, go there; may the god Savitr take you there.[^P257_9]

[^P257_9]: Mantra to be uttered by the principal wife when taking up the bones.

10\. This is your one (bone) and that is the second; obtain the third, full of light; be increased in the supreme place of the gods, where they live together, becoming full of the body and dear to the gods.[^P257_10]

[^P257_10]: Mantra to be uttered while collecting the bones in a jar.

[[P258]]

11\. Stand up, go, haste; make your house in the highest heaven; being concordant with Yama and Yami (wife of Yama) ascend this highest heaven.[^P258_11]

[^P258_11]: Mantra for taking the jar near the branch of Palasa or Sami tree.

12\. Having stones and wealth (water), "whatever is the purifying (ray) of the god Savitr", which is from this obtained nation", "say we, above, the darkness", "may Dhatr purify you" (the Mantras beginning with these words should be uttered).[^P258_12]

[^P258_12]: Mantra for hanging the jar on that branch.

13\. You are born of him, may he be born of you again. 2. Hail for the fire, Vaisvanara and the heavenly world (2).[^P258_13]

[^P258_13]: Mantra for burning the bone again in the case of soma-sacrificers. Last five Mantras of the Anuvaka III of this Prapathaka. Last Mantra of the Anuvaka II has been repeated here.

> *Note:* Last five Mantras of the Anuvaka III of this Prapathaka. Last Mantra of the Anuvaka II has been repeated here.

[[P259]]

### V

1\. May well-known Yama come by pleasant-minded protections, may Yami (wife of Yama) also come accompanied by settled (protection); may they sit on the well-settled Darbha-grass, verily, for vigour, good caste and destruction of the enemies.[^P259_1]

[^P259_1]: Invitation of Yama and his wife.

2\. When you come acting as Yemes (Yama and Yami); may then the men, desirous of divine ones, nourish you well; may you sit knowing your places and on that comfortable seat be for our happiness.[^P259_2]

[^P259_2]: Invitation of Yama and his wife.

3\. Press soma for Yama; sacrifice oblation for Yama; the sacrifice, having fire as envoy, full of ornament, goes to Yama.[^P259_3]

[^P259_3]: Offering of soma.

4\. Sacrifice the oblation, full of ghee for Yama and stand firm; may he, one among the gods, grant us long age for living well.[^P259_4]

[^P259_4]: Offering of ghee.

5\. Sacrifice the oblation, full of most (sweet) honey for Yama, the king. This is salutation for the seers, ancestors, born before and maker of the paths (1).[^P259_5]

[^P259_5]: Offering of honey.

6\. Who is wealth-wishing one in this earthly region, he is the one lord of this; for that Yama sing the pleasant-hearing modes, who is a king not to be checked.[^P259_6]

[^P259_6]: Praises of Yama.

7\. Sing the pleasant-hearing modes for Yama, who is a king not to be checked, who has made fire, the waters, the rivers, the lands, the heaven and the earth.[^P259_7]

[^P259_7]: Praises of Yama.

8\. Yama has held the earth; Yama has held all this world; all this exists for Yama whatever breathes being protected by the mind.[^P259_8]

[^P259_8]: Praises of Yama.

9\. He, who knows Yama, becomes able to speak like that one Seer (God) who knows all, how (run) the five castes, six seasons, fifteen dates (of a half-month) and the seers (2).[^P259_9]

[^P259_9]: Mantras for taking up the ghee.

10\. By Trikedrukasa (three sacrifices named Jyotis, Gaurus, Ayu) whatever falls on the six earths (the heaven, the earth, the waters, the plants, the food and the gladness) and the Great one, and what are the verses, Gayatri, Tristup (etc.); all these are kept in Yama.[^P259_10]

[^P259_10]: Mantras for taking up the ghee.

> *Note:* Offerings to Yama.

[[P260]]

11\. Yama, the son of the sun, does not satisfy taking away day-by-day the cow, the horse, the man, the moving one and the five castes.[^P260_11]

[^P260_11]: Mantras for taking up the ghee.

12\. Those men are described before Yama, the king, the son of the sun, whoever desire the truth and whoever are false-speakers.[^P260_12]

[^P260_12]: Mantras for taking up the ghee.

13\. Those men are described before, and go to you, O king, here (in your place) who salute the gods and who worship the Brahmanas.[^P260_13]

[^P260_13]: Mantras for taking up the ghee.

14\. In which tree of good leaves Yama drinks (soma) with the gods, there follows our ancient father, the lord of the subjects.[^P260_14]

[^P260_14]: Mantras for taking up the ghee.

### VI

1\. I sacrifice for the fire this oblation of thousand flows and hundred streams; may this fire hold increasingly in that my father, grandfather and great-grandfather.[^P260_1]

[^P260_1]: After putting the bones in the jar, that is covered with the grass, curds and honey.

2\. The drop has fallen on the earth and moves in the heaven (this mid-region), that earth on which it fell first and the third seat; I sacrifice the drop in seven quarters respectively.[^P260_2]

[^P260_2]: Addresses while dropping the curd and the honey.

3\. O fire, do not destroy in the highest heaven for the dead man this ocean (jar) of hundred streams, being served in the world, milking ghee and which is not to be broken.[^P260_3]

[^P260_3]: Addresses while dropping the curd and the honey.

4\. Recipe, be separated and go away from here (O you envoys of Yama!) who are old as well as new ones; may Yama grant him this place connected with the days, nights and the waters.[^P260_4]

[^P260_4]: Burning ground is swept in the morning.

> *Note:* Ceremony for raising a tumulus over the relic jar.

[[P261]]

5\. Savitr has put these bodies in the lap of the earth, the mother; by those may the (bulls), not destroyable ones be yoked (1).[^P261_5]

[^P261_5]: Yoking of the bulls for ploughing the ground.

6\. May the bulls comfortably carry the plough, may the men comfortably (cause them plough); may the ropes be bound comfortably and may the stick be upraised comfortably: O Sunasiras (Indra and Sun), put comfort in us.[^P261_6]

[^P261_6]: Furrows to be ploughed from east to west.

7\. Sunasiras (Indra and Sun), hearing this speech, sprinkle (this ground) by the water which you have gathered in the heaven.[^P261_7]

[^P261_7]: Furrows to be ploughed from east to west.

8\. O furrow, I salute you; O fortunate one, become belowward so that you may become full of fortune and fruits for us.[^P261_8]

[^P261_8]: Mantra for looking at the furrows.

9\. Savitr has put these bodies in the lap of the earth, the mother; O Aditi, become auspicious by that.[^P261_9]

[^P261_9]: Mantra for placing the jar in the middle furrow.

10\. O (bulls), who are ones not to be killed, be unyoked; we, going the divine ones, have come across this darkness, we have obtained the light and have reached the heaven (2).[^P261_10]

[^P261_10]: Unyoking of the bulls.

11\. The winds blow well, the lightnings fell, the plants go above, the heaven increases; the food becomes born for all the world, when Parjanya (rain) protects the earth by the semen (water).[^P261_11]

[^P261_11]: Mantra for sprinkling water with an Udumbara branch or a pitcher.

12\. As the five castes have sown household corns for Yama, so I sow the household corn so that we may become many in this world of the living ones.[^P261_12]

[^P261_12]: Mantra for placing the plants in the jar.

13\. (O sugars!) be performed as he says, be put as heaps all round, be put as heaps over, (in this way be put here: the gods are your fathers; may Prajapati put you with that god.[^P261_13]

[^P261_13]: Mantra for putting sugar.

14\. "Be increased", "Well your" (these two Mantras should be uttered).[^P263_14]

[^P263_14]: *(Mantra 14)* Mantras for putting the sands.

[[P263]]

### VII

1\. I make the earth firm for you; may I not be violated putting this world (clod) upon you; may the fathers hold this pillar for you; may Yama make place for you.[^P263_1]

[^P263_1]: *(Mantras 1–4)* Mantras for putting clods.

2\. Go to this earth, the mother, having great space, vast and full of good comfort; may this (earth), as a clever young lady protect you in the place of sin being soft as a rug.[^P263_2]

[^P263_2]: *(Mantras 1–4)* Mantras for putting clods.

3\. O earth, make him comfortful; do not check him; become full of good residence for him, become full of good space for his moving; cover this, O earth, as a mother covers her son.[^P263_3]

[^P263_3]: *(Mantras 1–4)* Mantras for putting clods.

4\. O earth, you are comfort-giving one, may thousand measured ones (sugars) depend upon you; may they all become here shelter for him always as honeyful homes.[^P263_4]

[^P263_4]: *(Mantras 1–4)* Mantras for putting clods.

5\. May the various-coloured, green and white corn-foods (fried barley) become cows for him having Tila (Sesamum Indicum) as their calf, milking vigour always and not lashing out (1).[^P263_5]

[^P263_5]: Mantra for throwing Tila and fried barley.

6\. This Svadha (food) is being put for you in the house of you...(name of the dead one)![^P263_6]

[^P263_6]: Mantra for putting the butter.

> *Note:* Yama which is not destroying. Ceremony for raising a tumulus over the relic jar.

[[P264]]

7\. I perform this offering of Darbha-grass for the fathers and the gods; I offer the best (Darbha), desirous of knowing the truth in my life-time; (O dead one) become pure being concordant with Yama and Yami.[^P264_7]

[^P264_7]: Mantra for spreading the Darbha-grass.

8\. May not the trees check you; may you not, O mother earth, (check him); (O dead one!) go to the fathers in the place of Yama and be increased.[^P264_8]

[^P264_8]: Mantra for surrounding the place with Palasa-branches.

9\. May not the trees check you, may not you, O mother earth, (check him); (O dead one!) go to the son of sun and shine in the world of Yama.[^P264_9]

[^P264_9]: Mantra for surrounding the place with Palasa-branches.

10\. Ascend on the boat of the nala-plant (reed); by that Nala obtain the way respectively; you as such, becoming one, who floats on sale cross well; cross fairly, cross excellently (2).[^P264_10]

[^P264_10]: Mantra for placing Nala (reed).

11\. Savitr has put these bodies in the lap of the earth, the mother; O earth, become auspicious for them.[^P264_11]

[^P264_11]: Mantra for placing the relic jar on the grass without looking on it.

12\. Sacrificer of six (sadhota): May your eye go to the sun; self, to the wind; go to the heaven and go to the earth by holding the rules (religion); go to the waters, if there is comfort for you and stand in the plants with the bodies.[^P264_12]

[^P264_12]: Addresses to the manes.

13\. Death, go by another way, which is your own, other than the way of the gods; I say to you, who have eyes and ears, "Do not kill my offspring and brave ones".[^P264_13]

[^P264_13]: Mantra for wiping the jar with old rag.

[[P265]]

14\. May wind be auspicious, may the shining sun be auspicious, may the plants be auspicious for you; may the quarters become auspicious for me.[^P265_14]

[^P265_14]: Mantra for sprinkling water after covering the body with an old cloth.

15\. I put you in the region of the earth, you are comfortable for that (dead one); the gods are your fathers; may Prajapati put you with that god.[^P265_15]

[^P265_15]: Mantras for putting on bricks.

16\. (I put you in the region) of the mid-region, of the heaven, of the quarters, on the back of the paradise, on the place of the sun; you are comfortable for that (dead one); the gods are your fathers; may Prajapati put you with that god (3).[^P265_16]

[^P265_16]: Mantras for putting on bricks.

> *Note:* Ceremony for raising a tumulus over the relic jar.

[[P266]]

### VIII

1\. May the Caru (food as oblation) with Apupa (particular bread) and ghee take place here, making the earth and the heaven firm above. Whoever are here the share-holders of ghee among the gods, may they serve as makers of seat and way. This is put Svadha (food) for you in the home of Yama, O...(dead one!). (That is) ten-syllabled (virat-Mantra); protect that, preserve that; I give you that; may not the fathers and the gods injure you in that; may Prajapati put you with that god.[^P266_1]

[^P266_1]: Mantras for putting the Caru.

2\. May the Caru, full of Apupa, well-cooked, full of milk, full of curd, full of honey take place here, making the earth and the heaven firm above. Whoever are here the share-holders of the well-cooked Caru, the milk, the curd, the honey among the gods, may they serve as makers of seat and way. This is put Svadha (food) for you in the home of Yama, (dead one!). (That is) hundred-syllabled, thousand-syllabled, ten-thousand-syllabled, undestroyable-syllabled, immovable-syllabled; protect that, preserve that; I give that to you; may not the fathers and the gods injure you in that; may Prajapati put you with that god (1).[^P266_2]

[^P266_2]: Mantras for putting the Caru.

[[P267]]

### IX

1\. Whatever fried corns I scatter here, by those I make immortal Svadha (food) for you; may Yama, concordant with the gods, make them desire-milking cows for you here.[^P267_1]

[^P267_1]: Mantra for throwing Tila and fried barley.

2\. O Arjuna (-plant), the Brahmanas know you, verily, as the milk of the plants; from the midst of them I take you for covering the carus (foods for oblation).[^P267_2]

[^P267_2]: Mantras for putting some plants looking at every side.

3\. Take the clump of the Durva (-grass), which is my dearest; may that grow abundant in this quarter of the men.[^P267_3]

[^P267_3]: Mantras for putting some plants looking at every side.

4\. Take the clump of the Kasa (-grass) for killing the Raksasas, for letting them not be again, who, the sinners, defeated this quarter.[^P267_4]

[^P267_4]: Mantras for putting some plants looking at every side.

5\. Take the clump of the Darbha (-grass), which is the dear of the fathers, so that the root, the joint and the fruit of this may be living (green) (2).[^P267_5]

[^P267_5]: Mantras for putting some plants looking at every side.

6\. "Fulfill the hole", "Those milk-destroyers of this" (these Mantras should be repeated).[^P267_6]

[^P267_6]: Mantras for putting some bricks.

7\. May the wind be auspicious; may the shining sun be auspicious for you; may the plants be auspicious for you; may all the quarters be for you.[^P267_7]

[^P267_7]: Mantra for sprinkling water.

8\. This only (is our sorrow for the dead one); may not any other sorrow other than this come to us; this (absence of sorrow) has been done by Asvine, Mitra and Varuna.[^P267_8]

[^P267_8]: Prayers.

9\. May Varana-plant, the divine one, destroy away this (trouble) and (may protect) from the sorrow, poverty or sin and hate.[^P267_9]

[^P267_9]: Mantra for placing the Varana tree.

10\. You are a holder, take from us the sins and the hates.[^P267_10]

[^P267_10]: Mantra for placing the clods.

11\. O Sami tree, blow out the sins and the hates.[^P267_11]

[^P267_11]: Mantra for placing the Sami tree.

12\. O Yava (barley), destroy the sins and the hates.[^P267_12]

[^P267_12]: Mantra for placing the barley.

13\. Go to the earth, go to the mid-region, go to the heaven, go to the quarters, go to the paradise, (yes) go to the paradise, go to the quarters, go to the heaven, go to the mid-region, go to the earth, or go to the waters, if there is comfort for you and stand in the plants with the bodies.[^P267_13]

[^P267_13]: Prayers for taking the dead one in good regions.

14\. "Full of stones and wealth (water)", "What, indeed, is the purifying of the god Savitr", "That is from the obtained nation", "May we, above the darkness", "May Dhatr purify" (these Mantras should be uttered) (3).[^P267_14]

[^P267_14]: Initial words of the Mantras for digging holes near the burning ground. Last Mantras of the Anuvaka III of this Prapathaka.

> *Note:* Ceremony for raising a tumulus over the relic jar.

[[P268]]

### X

1\. (O relative ones:) ascend this life (skin), praising to live up to the old age; try (to ascend respectively); may Dhatr, of good birth and having good gems make your life long to live well.[^P268_1]

[^P268_1]: Addresses to the relatives for sitting on bull-skin.

2\. As the days come in successive order from the preceding; as the seasons go with the seasons, caused (in the succession); the posteriority does not leave the anteriority; so, Dhatr (Disposer), cause the lives of these.[^P268_2]

[^P268_2]: Addresses to the relatives for sitting on bull-skin.

3\. O fire, mortal one has not done anything cruel for your body; he (acting) as a monkey burns you shiningly again as a cow (covers the womb by) the membrane. May you, o fire, shine taking away our sin, may you shine wealth; may you shine taking away our sin; for the Death, Hail![^P268_3]

[^P268_3]: Oblation of ghee.

4\. We touch the bull for our welfare; may you (o bull), like Indra, (as) fire, become leading us across to the gods (1).[^P268_4]

[^P268_4]: Mantra to be uttered while touching a red bull.

5\. These living ones, being separated by the dead one, have performed (the ceremonies), invoking of the gods has become auspicious today, now we go eastwardly for the sake of enjoyment and happiness, having long and good life.[^P268_5]

[^P268_5]: *(Mantra 5)* Mantras for proceeding to the east.

6\. Then we are going, rubbing out the foot-prints of the death and having long and good life; then, o sacrificing ones, be satisfied with offspring and wealth, sinless and purified.[^P268_6]

[^P268_6]: The last man rubs out, with a Sami branch, the foot-prints of those that precede.

> *Note:* Ceremonies to be performed on the tenth day after cremation.

[[P269]]

7\. I put this enclosure for the living ones; may none of us obtain only his half life; may all live a vast life of hundred autumns; we hide the death by this mountain.[^P269_7]

[^P269_7]: The Adhvaryu priest places a stone on the ground.

8\. May these women, unwidowed, good wives, touch their eyes by the collyrium-gee; without tears, without disease, worthy of every attention, may these wives enter the house first.[^P269_8]

[^P269_8]: Mantra for putting collyrium by the women.

9\. Which is the collyrium of the Trikakut (peak), born on the Anañjat (mountain), by that root of the immortality we destroy the enemies.[^P269_9]

[^P269_9]: 9. entre for the application of the collyrium, by the leaves of the Kuss-gress.

10\. O plant, as you grow on the earth, so may these also grow by fame, name and brilliance of sacred knowledge.[^P269_10]

[^P269_10]: Mantra for throwing the leaves on the bundle of the Kusa-grass.

11\. You are goat (Aja), destroy (Aja) our sins and hates.[^P269_11]

[^P269_11]: Mantra for eating goat's flesh.

12\. You are barley (Yava), destroy (Yava) our sins and hates (2).[^P269_12]

[^P269_12]: Mantra for eating barley.

> *Note:* Ceremonies to be performed on the tenth day after the cremation.

[[P270]]

### XI

1\. O fire, may you shine taking away our sin; may you shine wealth; may you shine taking away our sin.[^P270_1]

[^P270_1]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

2\. We sacrifice by the oblation of good field, good singing and good wealth; may (the fire) shine taking away our sin.[^P270_2]

[^P270_2]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

3\. When there comes the most auspicious (power) of these relatives and the brave offsprings of ours; then may (the fire) shine taking away our sin.[^P270_3]

[^P270_3]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

4\. To which powerful fire the brave ones go; may that shine taking away our sin.[^P270_4]

[^P270_4]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

5\. O fire, may we, who are your praisers, obtain the offsprings; may you shine taking away our sin (1).[^P270_5]

[^P270_5]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

6\. O fire, having mouth all round, you are the defeating one; may you shine taking away our sin.[^P270_6]

[^P270_6]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

7\. O fire, having mouth all round, make our enemies to be away as by boat; may you shine taking away our sin.[^P270_7]

[^P270_7]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

8\. You, as such, for our welfare make us cross the troubles as (one crosses) the ocean by the boat; may you shine taking away our sin.[^P270_8]

[^P270_8]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

9\. May our sin flow away as the waters flow while going belowward; may (the fire) shine taking away our sin.[^P270_9]

[^P270_9]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

10\. May our sin flow away as the waters flow from the high place; may (the fire) shine taking away our sin.[^P270_10]

[^P270_10]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

11\. We have come again to our homes for happiness and delight; may (the fire) shine taking away our sin.[^P270_11]

[^P270_11]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

12\. Where this prayer is done as an enclosure for living happily; there does not die a cow, man, horse or cattle; may (the fire) shine taking away our sin.[^P270_12]

[^P270_12]: Mantras for offering the oblations.

### XII

1\. We have seen the Rajagavi, living one, being carried for the sake of the dead, acting as young, which has been covered by the blind darkness (of the fear of being slaughtered); we know that releasing of the Rajagavi (cow) which is led with a corpse.[^P271_1]

[^P271_1]: Rajagavi is either killed or set free; the first has been discussed in the Anuvaka I; the next is dealt with here.

[[P271]]

> *Note:* for non-violence having her face to the east and the west.

2\. May she think that she is being nourished by me; for she will go to the region of the gods, by the way of the sky, worth choosing by all, covering the both worlds; may she cover our both worlds by milk.[^P271_2]

[^P271_2]: Rajagavi is either killed or set free; the first has been discussed in the Anuvaka I; the next is dealt with here.

3\. We reach with milk to the wealthy fire, full of honey, vigour and food; make us accompanied with wealth and with brilliance for our welfare.[^P271_3]

[^P271_3]: Mantra for sanctifying the cow.

4\. Who are alive, who are dead, who are born, who will be born, for their nourishing in this flowing stream of honey (the Rajagavi).[^P271_4]

[^P271_4]: Mantra for sanctifying the cow.

5\. (She is the mother of Rudras, the daughter of Vasus, the sister of Adityas, the centre of the nectar; I say, solemnly to the knowing one:) "Do not kill the sinless cow, which is not to be cut."[^P271_5]

[^P271_5]: Releasing of the cow.

6\. May she drink water, eat grasses. Om, release (1).[^P271_6]

[^P271_6]: Releasing of the cow.

Harih Om.

I sprinkle well offspring, life and wealth for you by the Yajus-Mantra.

Om Peace !

Peace !

Peace !

[[P272]]

## VII

[[P273]]

(The Taittiriya Upanisad, Sikṣa-valli)

### I (Prayer for Auspices and Peace)

Om. Auspicious for us (may) Mitra (be), auspicious (may) Varuna (be); auspicious for us may Aryaman be; auspicious for us (may) Indra (and) Bṛhaspati (be); auspicious for us (may) wide-strider Viṣṇu (be).[^P273_r1]

[^P273_r1]: *(Mantras)* Prayer for peace and auspices at the commencement of the Upanisad.

Salutation to Brahman, salutation to you, O Vayu; you, indeed, are the perceptible Brahman; I will declare you, indeed, the perceptible Brahman; I will declare (you) settled order (rta), I will declare (you) truth. May That protect me, may That protect the speaker; may That protect me, may That protect the speaker.

Om. Peace, peace, peace. (1)

### II (Lesson on Orthoepy)

Om. We will explain orthoepy: sound (varṇa), accent (svara), quantity (mātrā), force (bala), modulation (sāma), combination (saṃhitā). Thus has been declared the lesson on orthoepy. (1)

### III (Prayer and mysticism of five combinations)

(May) glory (be) with us two;[^P273_2] (may) splendour of sacred knowledge (brahma-varcas) (be) with us two.[^P273_r3]

> *Note:* Prapathaka V according to Commentator Bhaṭṭa Bhāskara. He gives all the three Prapathakas VII, VIII & IX as one; hence the numbers of Anuvākas are continuous in his commentary.

[^P273_2]: Teacher and pupil.

[^P273_r3]: *(Mantra)* Prayer for mutual brilliance and retention of sacred knowledge.

[[P274]]

Now next, we will explain the mystic meaning (upaniṣad) of combination (saṃhitā) under five headings: concerning the luminaries, concerning the worlds, concerning knowledge, concerning progeny, (and) concerning the self. These are the great combinations, they say.

Now, concerning the worlds: earth is the prior form, heaven, the latter form, space, the conjunction (1), wind, the connection; this much concerning the worlds. Now, concerning the luminaries: fire (is) the prior form, sun, the latter form, water, the conjunction, lightning, the connection; this much concerning the luminaries.

Now, concerning knowledge: teacher is the prior form (2), pupil, the latter form, knowledge, the conjunction, instruction, the connection; this much concerning knowledge.

Now, concerning progeny: mother is the prior form, father, the latter form, progeny, the conjunction, procreation, the connection; this much concerning progeny. (3)

Now, concerning the self: lower jaw is the prior form, upper jaw, the latter form, speech, the conjunction, tongue, the connection; this much concerning the self.

Thus these are the great combinations.

He who knows these great combinations thus explained, is conjoined with progeny, cattle, splendour of sacred knowledge (brahma-varcas), eatable food and heavenly world. (4)

[[P275]]

### IV (Prayer)

Who is the bull (pre-eminent) in the vedic chants (chandas), (who) is the all-formed (viśvarūpa); has sprung up from immortality above the vedic chants; may that Indra cheer me with intelligence; O God, may I be the possessor of immortality.

May my body be able, my tongue sweetest; may I hear abundantly with ears. You are the sheath of Brahman, veiled by intelligence. Protect that which I have heard.

Bringing, increasing (1), making herself long, my clothes, cows, food and drink always—(such is the prosperity). Therefore, bring that prosperity to me with wool[^P275_1] (and) with cattle. Hail!

May students of sacred knowledge (brahmacārin) come to me from all sides. Hail! May students of sacred knowledge come to me variously. Hail! May students of sacred knowledge come to me excellently. Hail! May students of sacred knowledge be self-controlled, be peaceful. Hail!

[^P275_1]: Wool means here the cattle possessing wool.

Glorious among men may I become. Hail!

Best among the wealthy may I become. Hail!

May I enter you as such, Lord. Hail!

You as such enter me, O Lord. Hail! In That (who is) of thousand branches, in you, O Lord, do I wash myself. Hail!

As waters go downward; as months into the year; so to me may students of sacred knowledge, O Disposer (Dhātṛ), come from all sides. Hail!

[[P276]]

You are a refuge; shine upon me, come to me. (3)

### V (Contemplation of four mystic utterances)

Bhūr, Bhuvas, Suvar: verily, these (are) the three utterances (vyāhṛtis).

Of them, verily, the fourth, namely Mahas, Mahācamasya discovered. That (is) Brahman; That is self (ātman); (His) limbs (are) the other deities.

Bhūr, verily, (is) this world; Bhuvas, the mid-region; Suvar, the other world (1); Mahas, the sun; by the sun, indeed, all the worlds are glorified. Bhūr, verily, (is) Fire (Agni); Bhuvas, the wind; Suvar, the sun (Āditya); Mahas, the Moon (Candramas); by Moon, indeed, all the luminaries are glorified. Bhūr, verily, (is) the Ṛks; Bhuvas, the Sāmans; Suvar, the Yajuses (2); Mahas, the Brahman; by Brahman, indeed, all the Vedas are glorified.

Bhūr, verily, (is) the vital breath (prāṇa); Bhuvas, the out-breath (apāna); Suvar, the pervading breath (vyāna); Mahas, the food (anna); by food, indeed, all the vital breaths are glorified.

They, verily, these four (are) fourfold; four, four (are) the utterances (vyāhṛtis).

Who knows these, he knows Brahman; all the gods bring offering to him. (3)

[[P277]]

### VI (Relation of the utterances with Brahman)

This space that is within the heart, therein is this Person (Puruṣa), consisting of mind (manomaya), immortal (and) bright.

Within the palate, that which hangs down like a nipple, that is the birthplace of Indra[^P277_1], where that hair-end splits dividing the skull.

[^P277_1]: Indra-Ātman.

(As) Bhūr he resides in Fire (Agni); (as) Bhuvas in wind (Vāyu); (as) Suvar in the Sun; (as) Mahas in Brahman. He obtains self-leadership (svārājya); he obtains the lord of mind, lord of the voice, lord of the eye, lord of the ear, lord of knowledge; (and) then becomes this Brahman whose body is the space (ākāśa-śarīra), whose soul is the truth (satyātmā), whose vital breath is delight (prāṇārāma), whose mind is bliss (manānanda), who is prosperous with peace (and who is) immortal.[^P277_2]

[^P277_2]: In two parts right and left.

Thus, O Prācīnayogya, worship.

### VII (Brahman as fivefold)

Earth, mid-region, heaven, quarters, intermediate quarters; Fire (Agni), Wind (Vāyu), Sun (Āditya), Moon (Candramas), Stars (Nakṣatras); waters, plants, trees, space (ākāśa) and self (ātman): this much concerning material existence (ādhibhūta).

Now concerning the self (adhyātma): prāṇa, vyāna, apāna, udāna, samāna; eye, ear, mind (manas), speech, touch; skin, flesh, muscle, bone, marrow.

Having ordained this, the seer said: 'Fivefold (paṅkta), verily, is this all; with the fivefold, indeed, one strengthens the fivefold.' (1)

[[P278]]

### VIII (Contemplation of Om)

Om is Brahman.

Om is this all (world).

Om, verily, this is compliance; and (on uttering) 'O recite' they recite. With Om they sing Sāmans. With Om 'Śaṃs' they recite the prayers (śastras). With Om the Adhvaryu (priest) utters the encouraging word (pratigara). With Om the Brahman (priest) impels. With Om (one) assents to the oblation to Fire (Agnihotra). 'Om' says the Brāhmaṇa about to recite 'May I obtain Brahman.' He does obtain the Brahman. (1)

### IX (Study and teaching with all other duties)

The settled order (ṛta), as well as study and teaching; the truth, as well as study and teaching; penance, as well as study and teaching; self-control, as well as study and teaching; peace, as well as study and teaching; the (sacrificial) fires, as well as study and teaching; Agnihotra (sacrifice), as well as study and teaching; guests, as well as study and teaching; humanity, as well as study and teaching; offspring, as well as study and teaching; begetting, as well as study and teaching; procreation, as well as study and teaching.

'The truth'—(says) Satyavacas, the son of Puruṣiṣṭa.

'Study and teaching only'—says Naka, the son of Mudgala, for that is penance, for that is penance.

### X (Ātma-Meditation)

I am the mover of the tree (worldly illusion); (my) fame (is) like the mountain's peak. I am highly pure like the excellent Immortal in the sun. (I am) shining wealth, (I am) wise, immortal and undecaying. This is Triśaṅku's recitation of Vedic knowledge. (1)

### XI (Exhortation after teaching)

Having taught the Veda, the teacher exhorts the pupils:

Speak the truth, follow the religion.

Do not swerve from study. Having brought the acceptable wealth to the teacher, do not cut off the line of progeny. From truth (you) should not swerve. From religion (you) should not swerve. From welfare (you) should not swerve. From prosperity (you) should not swerve. From study and teaching (you) should not swerve. From the duties to the gods and to the fathers (you) should not swerve. (1)

[[P279]]

Be one to whom mother is god; be one to whom father is god; be one to whom teacher is god; be one to whom guest is god.

What acts are irreproachable, they should be practiced (and) not others.

What are good deeds of ours, they should be practiced by you (and) not others. (2)

Whatever Brāhmaṇas are superior to us, their refreshment should be done by you with a seat.

With faith (śraddhā) should be given (any gift); without faith should not be given. With liberality (śrī) should be given (any gift); with modesty should be given; with fear should be given; with sympathy should be given.

Now, if there occurs any doubt concerning an act or a doubt concerning conduct (3), then, as the Brāhmaṇas there, who are thoughtful, zealous, devoted, not harsh, desirous of religion, may behave in such matters, so should you behave in such matters.

Now, with regard to the accused: as the Brāhmaṇas there, who are thoughtful, zealous, devoted, not harsh, desirous of religion, may behave in such matters, so should you behave in such matters.

This is the direction. This is the advice. This is the mystic doctrine of the Veda. This is the instruction. Thus you should worship. Thus, indeed, (you) should worship. (4)

[[P280]]

### XII (Prayer for auspices and peace)

Om. Auspicious for us (may) Mitra (be), auspicious (may) Varuna (be), auspicious for us may Aryaman be; auspicious for us (may) Indra (and) Bṛhaspati (be); auspicious for us (may) wide-strider Viṣṇu (be).[^P280_1]

[^P280_1]: *(Mantras)* Identical with the First Anuvaka except for certain changes of tense.

Salutation to Brahman, salutation to you, O Vayu; you, indeed, are the perceptible Brahman; I declared you, indeed, the perceptible Brahman; I declared (you) settled order, I declared (you) truth. That protected me, That protected the speaker; That protected me, That protected the speaker.

Om. Peace, peace, peace.[^P280_2]

[^P280_2]: *(Mantras)* Peace chanted once more in the Ānandāśrama edition.

[[P281]]

[[P282]]

## VIII

(The Taittiriyopanisad, Brahmananda-Valli)

### I (Prayer for good-feeling between teacher and disciple. Knowledge of Brahman)

Harih Om. May (Brahman) protect us both together.[^P282_2] May (He) nourish us both together. May we both show strength together. May the learning of us both be brilliant. May we not hate each other. Om. Peace, peace, peace.[^P282_3]

[^P282_1]: Sāyaṇa gives only two Anuvakas in the whole Prapathaka, separating line being the prayer-Mantras. Prayer is given in one Anuvaka by Bhaṭṭa Bhāskara and then other Anuvakas; but, according to tradition Śaṅkara combines both First and Second Anuvakas in one. R.L. Mitra also combines in one.

[^P282_2]: Both teacher and disciple.

[^P282_3]: Regular number of the kaṇḍikās have not been given in this Prapathaka except in the VIII Anuvaka, but there also in the end that has not been marked.

Om. The knower of the Brahman attains the Supreme.[^P282_1]

As to that this has been said: Real, Knowledge, Infinite is Brahman; he who knows (That) set down in the cave (of heart) (and) in the highest heaven, obtains all the desires together with the Brahman.

From That, verily, from this Soul the space (ākāśa) was born; from space, wind (vāyu); from wind, fire; from fire, waters; from waters, earth; from earth, plants; from plants, food; from food, man. He, verily, this man consists of food-essence. Thus, indeed, is his head; this, the right side; this, the tail; this, the left side; this, the self; the support.

As to that there is also this verse:

[[P283]]

### II (Food as Brahman. From food to breath)

From food, verily, creatures are born, whatever (creatures) dwell on earth. Moreover, by food, surely, they live; and then again they go to the food in the end. For food is, truly, the eldest of beings; therefore, it is called the medicament of all. All food, verily, they obtain, who worship food as Brahman. For food is, truly, the eldest of beings; therefore, it is called the medicament of all. From food are beings born; by food, when born, they grow. It is eaten and it eats beings; thence it is called food.

Then that, verily—than this one consisting of food-essence, there is another self within, consisting of breath (prāṇa); by him this is filled.

He, verily, this one, is just like man. After his human shape, this one (consisting of breath) is like man. Of him the in-breath (prāṇa) is itself head; the diffused breath (vyāna), the right wing; the out-breath (apāna), the left wing; space, the self; earth, the tail, the support.

As to that there is also this verse:

### III (Prāṇa as Brahman. From prāṇa to manas)

The gods breathe with breath (prāṇa), as also men and beasts. For breath, verily, is the life-duration of all beings; therefore, it is called the life-duration of all. The whole life-duration do they obtain, who worship breath as Brahman. For breath, verily, is the life-duration of beings; therefore, it is called the life-duration of all. Of that, of the former, this one, verily, is the self embodied.

[[P284]]

Than that, verily—than this one consisting of breath, there is another self within, consisting of mind (manas); by him this is filled.

He, verily, this one, is just like man. After his human shape, this one (consisting of mind) is like man. Of him the yajus itself is the head; ṛk, the right wing; sāman, the left wing; ordinance, the self; the Atharvanas and the Āṅgirasas, the tail, the support.

As to that there is also this verse:

### IV (Brahman beyond speech and thought. From mind to knowledge)

Whence words turn back together with the mind without reaching, he who knows that bliss of Brahman fears not at any time. Of that, of the former, verily, this is the self embodied.

Than that, verily—than this one consisting of mind, there is another self within, consisting of knowledge (vijñāna); by him this is filled.

He, verily, this one, is just like man. After his human shape, this one (consisting of knowledge) is like man. Of him, faith itself is the head; settled order (ṛta), the right wing; truth, the left wing; contemplation (yoga), the self; might (mahas), the tail, the support.

As to that there is this verse:

[[P285]]

### V (Knowledge as Brahman. From knowledge to bliss)

Knowledge accomplishes sacrifice; and also accomplishes deeds; all gods worship knowledge as Brahman, the Eldest. If (one) understands knowledge as Brahman (and) if from That he does not swerve, he, leaving sin in the body, obtains all desires. Of that, of the former, verily, this is the self embodied.

Than that, verily—than this one consisting of knowledge, there is another self within, consisting of bliss (ānanda); by him this is filled.

He, verily, this one, is just like man. After his human shape, this one consisting of bliss is like man. Of him pleasure (priya) itself is the head; delight (moda), the right wing; great delight (pramoda), the left wing; bliss, the self; Brahman, the tail, the support.

As to that there is also this verse:

### VI (Brahman)

Non-existent (asat), verily, he becomes, if he knows Brahman non-existent. If he knows Brahman existent, then (people) know him existent. Of that, of the former, verily, this is the self embodied.

Now next, the appurtenant questions:

Does any one, who knows not, go to the yonder world after departure (from this world)? Or does anyone, who knows, obtain the yonder world after departure (from this world)?

He desired: 'Let me be many, let me procreate myself.'

[[P286]]

He performed penance; after performing penance he created all this whatsoever. Having created it, into it, indeed, he entered.

Having entered it, he became existent and tyat; defined and undefined; support and non-support; conscious and unconscious; true (real) and false; whatever there is here, that all they call real.

As to that there is also this verse:

### VII (Creation)

This (world) was, verily, non-existent in the beginning; thence, verily, existence was produced. That made its soul itself; therefore it is called the well-done (or self-done).

Verily, what, that is well-done (or self-done), that, verily, is essence (rasa). For on getting the essence, truly, (one) becomes blissful.

For who would breathe out (or) who would breathe in, if there were not this bliss in space? For this, certainly, causes bliss. For, when, truly, this (ātman) finds fearlessness as a support in this invisible, bodiless, undefined (and) unsupported, then he has reached fearlessness.

When, however, this (ātman) makes any little difference in this (Brahman), then there is fear for him.

That (Brahman) himself is (the cause of) fear for the knower (of the difference) (who is) not thinker.

As to that there is also this verse:

[[P287]]

### VIII (Supreme Brahman and Bliss)

Through fear of Him the wind (vāyu) blows; through fear the sun (sūrya) rises; through fear of Him Fire (Agni) and Indra (and) Death (mṛtyu) as fifth speed.

This is that consideration (mīmāṃsā) of bliss:

Let there be a youth, a good youth (sadā-yuva), studious, most quick, most firm and most vigorous; let this whole earth be full of wealth for him. That is one human bliss.

Such hundred human blisses (1) are one bliss of the human Gandharvas as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten with desires.

Such hundred blisses of the human Gandharvas are one bliss of the divine Gandharvas as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten with desires. Such hundred blisses of the divine Gandharvas are one bliss of the fathers of long-enduring world as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten with desires.

Such hundred blisses of the fathers of the long-enduring world are one bliss of the gods born in the divine world (2) as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten by desires.

Such hundred blisses of the gods born in the divine world are one bliss of the gods divine by work—who go to gods by their work, as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten by the desires.

Such hundred blisses of the gods divine by work are one bliss of gods as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten by the desires.

[[P288]]

Such hundred blisses of gods are one bliss of Indra (3) as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten by the desires.

Such hundred blisses of Indra are one bliss of Bṛhaspati as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten by the desires.

Such hundred blisses of Bṛhaspati are one bliss of Prajāpati as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten by the desires.

Such hundred blisses of Prajāpati are one bliss of Brahman as well as of one well-versed in the Vedas and not smitten by the desires (4).

He who is in the man and who is in the sun—he is one.

He who knowing this, after departing from this world, proceeds on to this self, consisting of food; proceeds on to this self, consisting of breath; proceeds on to this self, consisting of mind; proceeds on to this self, consisting of knowledge; proceeds on to this self, consisting of bliss.

As to that there is also this verse:

### IX (Brahman beyond speech and mind)

Whence words turn back together with the mind without reaching, he who knows that bliss of Brahman fears not from any side.

Him, certainly, does not torment (the thought), "Why have I not done good and why have I done sin."

He who knows thus, regards these two as Ātman. This is the mystic doctrine (upaniṣat).

[[P289]]

May (Brahman) protect us both together. May (He) nourish us both together. May we both show strength together. May the learning of us both be brilliant. May we not hate each other.

Peace, peace, peace.[^P289_1]

[^P289_1]: Not given in Śaṅkara, Bhaṭṭa Bhāskara, Hume. Given by Sāyaṇa, Sitarama Sastri, B.A., S.C. & M.L.

[[P290]]

[[P291]]

## IX

(The Taittirīya Upaniṣad: Bhṛgu-Valli)[^P291_1]

[^P291_1]: Sāyaṇa considers this Prapathaka as the third Anuvaka of the VIII Prapathaka. Regular number of the kaṇḍikās have not been given except in the X Anuvaka.

### I (Attainment of the Knower of Brahman)

Harih Om. May (Brahman) protect us both together. May (He) nourish us both together. May we both show strength together. May the learning of us both be brilliant. May we not hate each other. Om. Peace, peace, peace.[^P291_2]

[^P291_2]: Hume does not give the Prayer-Mantra.

Bhṛgu, verily, the son of Varuṇa, went to his father Varuṇa (and said): "Teach (me) Brahman, Sir."

He said to him: "Food, breath, eye, ear, mind, speech."

He said again: "Whence, verily, these beings are born; by whom, when born, they live; to whom they go and merge (thereinto); be desirous to know That; That is Brahman."

He (Bhṛgu) performed penance. Having performed penance:—

### II (Food as Brahman)

He understood food as Brahman. For from food, verily, these beings are born; by food, when born, they live; to food they go and merge (thereinto).

Having understood that, he went again to (his) father Varuṇa (and said): "Teach (me) Brahman, Sir."

To him he said: "By penance desire to know Brahman; penance is Brahman."

He performed penance. Having performed penance:—

[[P292]]

### III (Prāṇa as Brahman)

He understood breath (prāṇa) as Brahman. For from breath, verily, these beings are born; by breath, when born, they live; to breath they go and merge (thereinto).

Having understood that, he went again to (his) father Varuṇa (and said): "Teach (me) Brahman, Sir."

To him he said: "By penance desire to know Brahman; penance is Brahman."

He performed penance. Having performed penance:—

### IV (Mind as Brahman)

He understood mind as Brahman. For from mind, verily, these beings are born; by mind, when born, they live; to mind they go and merge (thereinto).

Having understood that, he went again to (his) father Varuṇa (and said): "Teach (me) Brahman, Sir."

To him he said: "By penance desire to know Brahman; penance is Brahman."

He performed penance. Having performed penance:—

### V (Knowledge as Brahman)

He understood knowledge as Brahman. For from knowledge, verily, these beings are born; by knowledge, when born, they live; to knowledge they go and merge (thereinto).

Having understood that, he went again to (his) father Varuṇa (and said): "Teach (me) Brahman, Sir."

To him he said: "By penance desire to know Brahman; penance is Brahman."

He performed penance. Having performed penance:—

[[P293]]

### VI (Bliss as Brahman)

He understood bliss (ānanda) as Brahman. For from bliss, verily, these beings are born; by bliss, when born, they live; to bliss they go and merge (thereinto).

This is the knowledge of Bhṛgu, the son of Varuṇa, fixed in the highest heaven.

He who knows this, becomes fixed; becomes possessor of food (and) eater of food; becomes great in progeny, cattle, splendour of sacred knowledge (brahma-varcas) (and) great in fame.

### VII (Importance of food)

(One) should not abuse food. That is the duty (vrata).

Breath, verily, is food; body, the eater of food. The body is fixed in breath; breath is fixed in the body. That is, this food is fixed in food.

He who knows this food fixed in food, becomes fixed; becomes possessor of food (and) eater of food; becomes great in progeny, cattle, splendour of sacred knowledge (brahma-varcas) (and) great in fame.

### VIII (Importance of food)

(One) should not despise food. That is the duty.

Waters, verily, are food; light, the eater of food. Light is fixed in waters; waters are fixed in light. That is, this food is fixed in food.

He who knows this food fixed in food, becomes fixed; becomes possessor (and) eater of food; becomes great in progeny, cattle, splendour of sacred knowledge (brahma-varcas) (and) great in fame.

### IX (Importance of food)

One should make food much. That is the duty.

[[P294]]

The earth, verily, is food; space, the eater of food. Space is fixed in the earth; the earth is fixed in space. That is, this food is fixed in food.

He who knows this food fixed in food, becomes fixed; becomes possessor of food (and) eater of food; becomes great in progeny, cattle, splendour of sacred knowledge (brahma-varcas) (and) great in fame.

### X (Food and Knowledge of Brahman)

(One) should not despise anyone at his dwelling. That is the duty.

Therefore, in any way whatsoever, one should obtain much food.

'I have asked cooked food for this' they say. This, verily, is the food cooked primarily; (hence) primarily food is cooked for him. This, verily, is the food cooked in the middle; (hence) in the middle food is cooked for him. This, verily, is the food cooked in the end; (hence) in the end food is cooked for him. (1)

He who knows this (he obtains the aforesaid things).

As protection in speech, as acquisition and protection in the in-breath (prāṇa) and the out-breath (apāna), as work in the hands, as motion in the feet, as discharge in the anus: these are the human worships (of Brahman). Now the divine ones: as satisfaction in the rain, as power in lightning, as fame in the cattle, as light in the stars, as procreation, immortality and bliss in the generative organ, as all in space.

(One) should worship That as support; (then he) becomes possessor of the support. (One) should worship That as greatness; (then he) becomes great. (One) should worship That as mind; (then he) becomes honourable. (One) should worship That as salutation; (then) all the desires bow him down. (One) should worship That as Brahman; (then he) becomes realizer of Brahman. (One) should worship That as Brahman's round-place of destruction; (then) around him die envious rivals (and those) who are unloving foes.

[[P295]]

He who is in the man and who is in the sun—he is one. (4)

He who knows this, after departing from this world, proceeding on to this self, consisting of food; proceeding on to this self, consisting of breath; proceeding on to this self, consisting of mind; proceeding on to this self, consisting of knowledge; proceeding on to this self, consisting of bliss; roaming in these worlds according to his desire, and having food according to his desire, lives singing this sāman:—

Oh, oh, oh!

I am food, I am food, I am food! I am eater of food, I am eater of food, I am eater of food! I am maker of fame, I am maker of fame, I am maker of fame! I am the first-born of the settled order (ṛta), before the gods, (and I am) the navel of immortality. Who gives me, he protects me. I am food; I eat the eater of food. I have conquered all the world. I am light like (that of) sun.

He who knows thus—he obtains the aforesaid things. This is the mystic doctrine (upaniṣat).

Om. May (Brahman) protect us both together. May (He) nourish us both together. May we both show strength together. May the learning of us both be brilliant. May we not hate each other. Om. Peace, peace, peace.

[[P296]]

## X

[[P297]]

### I

Hariḥ Om.

May He protect us both together; may he nourish us both together; may we show vigour together; may our learning be brilliant; may we not hate each other.

Om Peace!

Peace!

Peace!

1\. Across the water, in the world, at the back of the heaven, greater than the great, entered the lights by shining, in the womb (of the world-creation) Prajapati moves.

2\. In whom this all is born and in whom this all vanishes; in whom all the gods live; that is the being and that is what is to be; this all is in that immortal and supreme heaven.

3\. By whom has been pervaded the sky, the heaven, the earth, by whom the sun shines by brilliance and splendour; whom the poets see woven in the midst of the sea (world); in that supreme and immortal one are the subjects.

4\. From whom the birth of the world has been born, who created by the water (matters) all the beings in the earth; who entered all the plants, men, cattle, moving and immoving creatures.

5\. There is nothing better than this, which is better than the good, greater than the great; which is one, unknown, infinite, all, ancient and above the darkness.

The Narayana or the Yajniki Upanisad.[^P297_note]

[^P297_note]: The Narayana or the Yajniki Upanisad.

[[P298]]

6\. To that, indeed, they say Ṛta (Settled Order), truth; that is the best Brahman of the poets; that is the stored-up sacrifices; as the navel that nourishes variously all that are born or that are still to be born.

7\. That is, verily, the fire; that is the wind, that is the sun, that is also the moon, that is the shining nectar, that is Brahman, that is the waters, that is Prajapati.

8\. All the nimeṣas, kalās, kāṣṭhās, days and nights (measuring parts of the time) all these were born of the Shining Person; half-months, months, seasons were also made of Him; He milked both the waters, these heaven and the earth and also the mid-region and the heaven.

9\. None holds Him above, sidewise or in the middle; none is able to hold Him; hence His name is 'Great Fame'. His form does not stand to be seen; none sees Him by the eye; by the heart, by the thinking, by the mind He is found; who know Him, they become immortal.

10\. "Hiranyagarbha, born of the waters" Mantras (of Prapāṭhaka III, Anuvāka XIII), these eight.[^P298_10]

[^P298_10]: *(Mantras)* "Hiranyagarbha, born of the waters" Mantras (of Prepathake III, Anuveke XIII), these eight.

11\. This God is, verily, in all the quarters; He was born before and even he is in the womb; He is being born; He is to be born; He is the Head of all the limbs; He stands having his face all round. He has eyes all round, mouth all round, hands all round, feet all round; He bends well by hands and falling ones (rules by the religion etc.); He is One producing the heaven and the earth.

12\. Learned Vena, the learned Gandharva observing all the worlds and that, where all the worlds rest as in a nest, in which this all is born and in which this all vanishes; in which this all is woven, which is pervading in the subjects taught that nectar and the three feet (rising, dreaming and sleeping conditions) which are put in the minds; he, who knows that, becomes even the father of his producer (father).

[[P299]]

13\. That (god) is our brother, father, disposer; He knows all the places, where, in the third sphere, all the gods, drinking the nectar obtained their places (who know Him) they pervade the heaven and the earth soon, the worlds, the quarter and the paradise; who knows the line of the Ṛta (Settled Order = God) discriminatingly, he becomes That among the subjects.

14\. Prajapati, the first born of the Ṛta (God) pervading the worlds, pervading the beings, pervading all the great quarters and the sub-quarters obtained the Self by himself.

15\. I have obtained the lord of the assembly, the wonderful one, dear to Indra, worth desiring, winning and the intellect.

16\. O fire, be shining up destroying my poverty; bring for me cattle and life from all the directions.

17\. O fire, do not kill our cow, horse, man and the moving ones; O fire, come forgetting our sins; meet us with prosperity.

18\. May we know and meditate the form of the person, having thousand eyes, the great God (Mahadeva); may that Rudra impel us.

19\. May we know and meditate the form of the person, the great god (Mahadeva); may that Rudra impel us.

20\. May we know and meditate the form of Vakratunda; may that Danti impel us.

21\. May we know and meditate the form of the person, having golden feathers; may that Garuda impel us.

[[P300]]

22\. May we know and meditate the form of Katyayana, Kanyakumari; may that Durgi impel us.

23\. May we know and meditate the form of Narayana Vasudeva; may that Visnu impel us.

24\. May the Durva-grass, the goddess, better than thousands, having hundred roots, the destroyer of the bad dreams destroy our all sin.[^P300_24]

[^P300_24]: *(Mantra 24)* Mantra to be uttered while taking the bath.

25\. O earth, trodden by the horse, trodden by the chariot, trodden by Visnu protect us at every step being put on my head (by me).

26\. You have been raised up by the Black Boar, having hundred arms; you are the earth, the cow, the holder (of the plants etc.) and the holder of the beings.

27\. O earth, destroy my sin and whatever bad deed I have done; may I live hundred autumns, my sins being destroyed by you.

28\. O earth, give me nourishing; everything stands firm in you.[^P300_25_29]

[^P300_25_29]: *(Mantras 25–29)* Mantras for sanctifying lumps of earth at the time of taking bath.

29\. I invite that prosperity (earth), which has the doors of the perfume, non-injurable, always satisfied, full of dung, the lord of all the beings.

30\. May the waters and the plants be good friends to us; may they become bad friends to them, who hate us and whom we hate.[^P300_30]

[^P300_30]: *(Mantra 30)* Mantra to be uttered after going in the water up to the navel.

[[P301]]

31\. I obtain Varuna, having the gold born (crown); give me entering place being prayed; may Indra, Varuna, Brhaspati, Savitr purify again and again whatever I have eaten of wicked ones, whatever I have taken from the sinful ones, whatever sin has been done by me by mind, speech or work.

32\. Salutation to the fire, full of the water, salutation to Indra, salutation to Varuna, salutation to Varuni (the wife of Varuna), salutation to the waters.[^P301_31_32]

[^P301_31_32]: *(Mantras 31–32)* Prayer to the gods of the water after rubbing the body with the sanctified earth.

33\. What is cruel, impure and peaceless of the waters; may that go away.[^P301_33]

[^P301_33]: *(Mantra 33)* Mantra for purifying the bad water while diving.

34\. May Varuna take away by his hand whatever sin I have done by eating much, drinking much, taking gifts cruelly; may that I, becoming sinless, without dust, free, free from the fault, ascending the back of the heaven go to the eternal Brahman.[^P301_34]

[^P301_34]: *(Mantra 34)* Mantras for diving.

35\. Hear this (my prayer) and be gathered O Ganges, O Yamuna, Sarasvati, O Sutudri with Parusani, Asikni, O Marudvrdha with Vitasta, O Arjikiya with Susoma.[^P301_35]

[^P301_35]: *(Mantra 35)* Invitation to the sacred rivers to be gathered.

36\. Ṛta (settled order) and truth was born of the shining One after austerity; then the night was born, then the various seas; from the various seas the year, the lord of the all moments was born creating the days and the nights; the disposer made the sun and the moon as before and also the heaven, the earth, the mid-region and the paradise.[^P301_36]

[^P301_36]: *(Mantra 36)* Mantra to be uttered standing in the water for destroying the sins.

[[P302]]

37\. Whatever is our sin in the earth, in the mid-region and in the places between the heaven and the earth; may the waters and sin-destroying Varuna purify that. This (Varuna) is the protector of the world among the beings; this is the worlds of the pious ones; this is the golden world of the death; this is the golden place of the heaven and the earth depending on the paradise; may he sharpen my paradise.[^P302_37]

[^P302_37]: *(Mantras 37)* Mantras for diving again uttering three Ṛcas.

38\. The wet (water) is shining; I am the light. The light is shining; I am Brahman. Whoever am I, I am Brahman. I am Brahman, I am. I am I; I sacrifice for me.[^P302_38]

[^P302_38]: *(Mantra 38)* Mantra for washing the face after diving.

39\. Performer of improper deeds, one who has split his semen virile, the thief, the killer of the embryo, one who sleeps on the bed of his teacher (these are the great sinners); Varuna, the lord of the waters is the destroyer of the sins; one becomes released from that sin.[^P302_39]

[^P302_39]: *(Mantra 39)* Mantra for diving again after washing.

40\. (Though you can) cause us, the sinners to weep; but may the seers purify, may Vasus purify, may Varuna purify, may the destroyer of the sins purify.[^P302_40]

[^P302_40]: *(Mantra 40)* Mantra for destroying the unknown sins.

41\. The sea, pervaded all in the beginning creating the beings according to their past deeds; the king of the world (God), the sprinkling and impelling one, delighting one, the great Soma increased himself on the pious, great and non-destroyable peak (of the heart).[^P302_41]

[^P302_41]: *(Mantra 41)* Mantra to be uttered repeatedly after taking the bath.

42\. We press Soma for Fire (Jatavedas); his knowledge burns those, who wish to be our enemies; he makes us across all the difficulties and sins like the crossing of the sea by a boat.[^P302_42_47]

[^P302_42_47]: *(Mantras 42–47)* Six Mantras for destroying the evils.

[[P303]]

43\. I obtain the shelter of the goddess Durga, the fire-coloured one, shining by the austerity, brilliant, standing in the fruits of the works; O good-crossing one, salutation to you, who make across.

44\. O fire, worth praising, make us across all the difficulties by protections; may the village be vast, much broad for us; be wealful for our sons and grandsons.
